# Round 3: search-space openness, meta-agent edit behavior, self-update, continual benchmark

## Unified proposal

### Is there one claim here?
Yes, but only one combination works. The four round-3 threads share a single unmeasured quantity: **where the cause of the current failures sits on the editable-surface ladder**, and **whether the gate can credit an edit made at that rung**. With that quantity in view, the threads become one claim instead of a bundle:
- the openness law,
- why improvers don't self-edit,
- forcing self-updates,
- a reality-grounded drift benchmark.

The claim: openness pays exactly where the environment places the cause. On today's stationary benchmarks the cause never sits at the improver or evaluator level. That alone explains the self-edit drought, with no appeal to reluctance. Real drift of the *improvement regime* moves the cause up the ladder.

The benchmark is the manipulation that moves the cause. The forced and meta-credit arms manipulate credit. The behavioral coding measures realization. None of the three is a separate contribution, which is why this is not K4.

Other unifications fail that test. "Ladder + MetaShift + forced quota" as three experiments would be three half-papers, the same failure round 2 flagged for Effect-Cause-Decay. Switching period P (varying-goals) and the population/hitchhiking ideas do not reduce to the same estimand, so they stay out.

---

### Title
**Open Where the Cause Is: A Reach × Credit Law for Meta-Harness Openness Explains the Self-Edit Drought and Predicts When Improvers Must Rewrite Themselves Under Real Drift**

### One-sentence claim
The held-out gain from opening rung k of a self-improver's editable surface (prompt → memory → harness code → improver code → gate/evaluator access) is the product of three measured factors, minus a selection penalty:
- **reach**: the share of current failure mass whose fix lives at rung k;
- **realization**: the rate at which the proposer emits valid rung-k edits;
- **credit**: the probability the gate retains a beneficial rung-k edit.

So the openness optimum moves to whichever rung real drift puts the cause at. Improvers rarely edit themselves because reach × credit at L4 is ≈ 0 on stationary tasks, not because they are unwilling.

### Alternative outcomes (all pre-registered, all publishable)
| Outcome | What we'd see | Paper becomes |
|---|---|---|
| **A. Law holds** (predicted) | Gain is flat or declines past L3 under stationarity, with retained L4 edits ≈ 0. Under improvement-regime drift, O* moves to L4 or L5 *only* when credit is visible (meta-gate). Under task-content drift, O* stays at L3. The probe-parameterized law ranks cells out of sample. | Headline as stated. |
| **B. Reach without realization** | Oracle probes show high L4 reach under drift, but the proposer still rarely proposes L4 edits even with evidence pointing there. Only the quota moves the rate. | "Priors, not gates": a causal test of the Opinionated Optimizers account, with the quota as the fix. |
| **C. Realization without credit** | L4 proposals rise after the shift, but the object gate rejects them, while a planted oracle L4 edit passes under the meta-gate. | "Gate, not willingness", grounded in real drift. |
| **D. Openness pays everywhere** | The editable improver beats L3 even on D0 and task-only drift. | Supports the RSI-Harness / MetaEvoForge camp and falsifies "the drought is rational". |
| **E. Flat null** | All rungs fall within seed noise of a matched-token test-time-scaling baseline. | Matched-compute null with a reach/realization/credit decomposition explaining why (Datasets & Benchmarks fallback, stated up front). |

### Core questions
1. **Q1 (openness optimum).** At matched tokens, where is the held-out-gain optimum on the ladder, and does it move with *which rung the drift puts the cause at*? This is the crossover: task-content drift → L3, procedure drift → L4, verifier drift → L5.
2. **Q2 (why no self-edits).** Is the self-edit drought explained by low reach × low credit rather than low willingness? This is measured as **proposed → retained → effective** improver edits per cell, effective meaning nested-replay successor gain.
3. **Q3 (forcing).** Does forcing self-updates (realization fix) or meta-crediting them (credit fix) help *only* when L4/L5 reach is high? Is forcing inert or harmful under stationarity?
4. **Q4 (law).** With R, ρ, κ measured from cheap probes that never touch the main loops, does the law predict per-cell held-out gain out of sample better than a rung-index model?

### The law and how each parameter is measured
$$G(O\mid d,g)\;=\;\sum_{k\le O} R_k(d)\,\cdot\,\rho_k(O)\,\cdot\,\kappa_k(g)\;\cdot\,\Delta_{\text{drop}}(d)\;-\;\lambda\,\sigma_{\text{eval}}\sqrt{\tfrac{2\ln N_{\text{eff}}(O)}{n_{\text{val}}}}$$

| Term | Meaning | Measured by (independent of the main loops) |
|---|---|---|
| R_k(d) | **Reach**: fraction of the post-shift drop recoverable by the best edit at rung k. This is the per-rung version of 2610.14662's editable-cause share π_e. | **Oracle-edit probe.** A hand-written edit at each rung, applied to the frozen pre-shift harness, scored on held-out tasks with a clean grader. 2–3 oracle edits per rung × drift, taking the max. |
| ρ_k(O) | **Realization**: per-generation probability of a valid rung-k proposal when rungs ≤ O are open. Dilution as O grows is built in. | Proposal logs from 2 short "proposal-only" runs per cell (no selection). Proposed rung share × syntactic/semantic validity. |
| κ_k(g) | **Credit**: P(gate retains a beneficial rung-k edit). For the object gate this is ≈ Φ(Δ_k^{imm}√n/σ − z). Delayed-payoff L4 edits have Δ^{imm} ≈ 0. | **Planted-edit test.** Inject each oracle edit into a live lineage 20× and record acceptance under the object 1-step gate and under the meta-gate. |
| Δ_drop(d) | Post-shift drop of the frozen harness. | Calibrated to 10 ± 2 pp for every drift type (matched difficulty). |
| N_eff(O), σ, n_val | Effective number of distinct accepted candidates; evaluator noise; validation size. | N_eff from AST/semantic dedup of accepted edits. σ from rerun reproducibility (VERSE-style). n_val fixed at 64. |

λ is the only free parameter. It is fit on D0 cells only, then frozen. Predictions for the drift cells are registered before those cells run, so the law can fail.

Positioning: the selection term is the existing winner's-curse / PAC admission bound (2610.09239, 2609.08175), and the reach × restriction framing instantiates 2611.09701. We claim neither functional form. We claim the measured per-rung decomposition and the out-of-sample prediction of where the optimum sits.

---

### Minimal design

**Testbed: DialLoop.** A ~600-line Gödel-lite / Meta-Harness-lite keep-if-better loop. One config sets the open rungs, and surfaces are nested, so rung O means everything ≤ O is editable:
- **L0**: fixed prompt slots (numeric knobs, few-shot ids).
- **L1**: free prompt text.
- **L2**: memory / skills / notes.
- **L3**: harness code and tool wrappers.
- **L4**: improver code (diagnosis, proposal policy, budget allocation, reflection inputs).
- **L5**: evaluator access. The gate *policy* is editable: revalidation schedule, thresholds, re-evaluation and majority votes, which feedback signals are read.

Fixed outside every surface:
- the sealed held-out grader;
- the meta-gate / replay computation;
- token accounting.

This closes the factor leak that MR-D's judges flagged. Every accept or reject decision is logged, and any attempt to read or bypass the sealed grader is flagged.

**Benchmark component: CauseShift.** Every shift is drawn from real sources and certified EnvOS-style: the change fires, the task stays solvable, and the grader separates. Each runs 12 pre-shift + 12 post-shift generations on one primary substrate: about 150 Python tasks with hidden tests, pinned to post-cutoff library versions (polars, recent pydantic minors, httpx), with Docker-pinned versions and web isolation.
| Drift | Real source | Where the oracle says the cause is |
|---|---|---|
| D0 stationary | the same tasks, no shift | — (control) |
| D_task: API-content drift | real breaking changes from changelogs (GitChameleon / Bridge / Update-from-Hell items) | L2/L3: new-idiom memory or a version-check tool wrapper |
| D_proc: improvement-regime drift | flaky tests at published real-world flake rates (0 → 15%), plus a feedback-granularity collapse (per-test logs → pass/fail), from real CI practice | L4: the improver must re-evaluate, aggregate or reallocate budget |
| D_eval: grader drift | 2 real verifier/test-harness commits (BenchPulse-mined BFCL checker/aggregation changes as the second substrate, about 100 tasks) | L5: the gate must revalidate retained edits |

**Cause-location validation is a gate, not a result.** It is pre-registered: R_target ≥ 0.6 and R_other ≤ 0.3 for each drift. This is the AC's "matched difficulty" fix and the mentor's "oracle-validate the benchmark first" fix. Held-out scoring always uses a clean, full-granularity grader that is separate from the shifted loop signal.

**Cells** (matched proposer + executor tokens per generation; replay cost charged to the arm):
- **Block A, ladder × drift:** O ∈ {L0…L5} × d ∈ {D0, D_task, D_proc, D_eval}, object gate, natural proposals. 24 cells × 5 seeds (L0 at 3 seeds).
- **Block B, self-update 2×2:** quota {off, q = 0.25 mandated improver/gate proposal} × credit {object 1-step gate, meta-gate}. The meta-gate scores an edit by the replayed gain of its children on 3 frozen post-shift states (IFR / nested-replay-lite, credited to IFR and IbI). Run at L4 under D0 and D_proc, and at L5 under D_eval: 3 new cells per setting × 6 seeds. Add a **placebo-quota** twin (forced semantically null AST edit) at L4/D_proc.
- **Block C, second family:** gpt-oss-120b or DeepSeek-V3.x as proposer at {L3, L4, L4 + meta-gate} × {D0, D_proc}, 4 seeds.
- About 205 lineages in total.

**Models.**
- Proposer: Qwen3.5-27B (main), with the second family in Block C.
- Executor: frozen Qwen3-8B across all arms, so only proposer-side openness varies, per the mentor fix.
- Annotator: one cheap API model, validated against 200 hand labels.

**Stats.**
- **Primary:** the pre-registered interaction contrast (L4 − L3) × (D_proc − D0), plus its L5/D_eval analogue. Mixed model with lineage random effects, and a power analysis from the pilot SD before committing seeds.
- **Recovery:** Cox survival model of recovery time (censored when a lineage never recovers), with post-shift AUC as co-primary.
- **Equivalence:** TOST for L4 ≈ L3 under D0, margin 0.5 seed-SD.
- **Law:** held-out-cell Spearman ≥ 0.6 and lower RMSE than a rung-index model.
- **Elicitation check:** report pass@1 and pass@k on every cell.

### Behavioral-analysis component
Every proposal is logged, retained or not, and coded on two axes, with two coders on a 15% subsample and κ reported:
- **rung touched**;
- **mechanism**: routing, budget, reword, structural, revalidate, tighten or loosen gate.

Three analyses:
1. **The proposed → retained → effective funnel** per cell. This is the gate-not-willingness decomposition, and it decides between outcomes B and C.
2. **Directedness.** At L4 under D_proc, a shuffled-evidence control (evidence from a different shift) tests whether post-shift evidence *directs* proposals to L4 or whether they are prior-driven. This is the useful core of the evidence-contrastive proposer idea.
3. **Habit lock-in.** The edit-type distribution in the 6 generations after each shift, plus a proposer-rationale codebook (unaware / low payoff / risk / uncertain) linked to arms. The prediction is that credit moves "low-payoff" codes and quota moves "unaware" codes.

### Forced-self-update arm
This is Block B. Mandating proposals is a *realization* fix and the meta-gate is a *credit* fix. The design separates the two, plus a placebo that separates "forced exploration" from "forced content".

Pre-registered predictions:
- Quota alone under the object gate raises proposed and retained L4 edits but not successor quality, so the forced edits are inert.
- Quota + meta-gate helps only under D_proc / D_eval.
- Under D0, every forcing cell is ≤ the natural cell. This is consistent with VERSE's unverified-self-evolution harm.

### Pilot (Nov 16–29, ≤ $80) with go/kill
1. **Oracle reach probe** on CauseShift-mini (40 polars/pydantic tasks; D_task and D_proc only). One hand-written oracle edit per rung.
   - **Go** if the target-rung R ≥ 0.6, other rungs ≤ 0.3, and the frozen drop is ≥ 10 pp.
   - **Kill / reframe** if the L4 oracle cannot beat the L3 oracle under D_proc. That means procedure drift is absorbable at object level, so pivot to the IBI-merged improver paper.
   - If the drop is < 5 pp, raise the flake rate or switch to the granularity shift once.
2. **Natural L4 run**, 3 seeds × 8 + 8 generations, D0 vs D_proc, object gate. Measure proposed and retained L4 share.
3. **Planted-edit gate test**: κ_4 under the object gate vs the meta-gate.

**Overall go:** (1) passes, AND either the proposed L4 share rises under D_proc or κ_4(meta) − κ_4(object) ≥ 0.3.

### Timeline to May 2027
| When | What |

| Nov 16–29 | Pilot (alongside the Taught, Not Selected Week-1 pilot) |
| Dec 1–20 | Build the full CauseShift: 150 + 100 certified tasks, 2 verifier commits, all oracle probes. Run the realization and credit probes. OSF pre-registration by Dec 20, with λ fit on D0 rules and predicted O* for each drift. |
| Jan | Block A. Check ICLR 2027 decisions and revisions (RobustSGPO / EvoSpace / VERSE / IFR) in the last week of January. |
| Feb | Block B. **Preprint by about Feb 25** to get ahead of a Flex-RSI v2 / CMAS follow-up. |
| Mar | Block C and behavioral coding (κ). |
| Apr | Law fit on D0; out-of-sample test on drift cells; robustness checks. |
| May 1–15 | Write; release DialLoop and CauseShift. |

Budget: about $1.8–2.6k hosted, or less with local vLLM for the 27B.

### Risks
- **An empty L4/L5 arm.** Block B's quota guarantees events, and outcome B is publishable.
- **Cause-location is not separable in real drift.** This is gated in the pilot; flaky/granularity shifts are the most controllable.
- **Build load for a solo undergrad.** This is the largest risk: about 250 certified tasks, oracle edits and 205 lineages. Mitigations: one substrate primary; D_eval on the second substrate with only 2 commits; drop Block C if needed.
- **Memorized APIs.** Use post-cutoff releases only, with a no-docs probe.
- **The L5 gate edits loosen the gate to game the loop signal.** Intended-grader scoring is primary, and every gate edit is coded tighten / loosen / revalidate.
- **The "bundling" critique (K4).** Defend it by having a single estimand and a single law, with parameters measured outside the loops.
- **Scoop.** The crossover is open, but the neighbours are dense: RobustSGPO, EvoSpace, Are-SE-CL, CMAS, Flex-RSI.

### Closest papers and the delta
| Paper | What it already owns | Our delta |
|---|---|---|
| RobustSGPO 2609.09646 | a 3-rung matched-token ladder; nominal / realized / effective space; scheduling beats max permission | 6 rungs including improver and gate; drift-moved optimum; reach/credit measured independently |
| EvoSpace MQoRwlss2y / DyScope MXINrso5QC | "too closed vs too open" stated as an insight; adaptive scope | We predict *where* scope should go, from cause location |
| Are-SE-CL 2610.25858 | drifting streams × editable surface; P/S/I/F metrics; Meta-Harness gate freeze | Real drift; drift *type* placed by rung; editable improver and gate arms. We reuse its metrics. |
| SelfImprove-Bench 2611.02797 / IbI ZMmJDeDTXk | 0% improver edits; framing lever; nested replay | A causal account of the drought (reach × credit) and the funnel to effective edits. IbI's assay is used and credited. |
| VERSE 2610.02616 | self-evolution helps only with verification | Verification is one way to raise κ; we add reach as the competing condition |
| HGM / IFR eIJH6HV9LZ / RSI-Harness EOrtreQLbv | meta-credit mechanisms | Used as the κ manipulation, not claimed |
| 2610.14662 | an editable-cause share π_e model of directedness | Extended to a per-rung reach, measured by oracle edits and used predictively |
| 2611.09701; 2610.09239 | restriction gap; winner's-curse selection term | Instantiated, not claimed |
| MetaShift-style CMAS 2610.31436; BenchPulse hpoWWEDJW9; tn4eslVXEK | adaptation delay; real verifier churn; evaluator-change false progress | Exogenous grader drift as a cause placed at L5, with an editable-gate arm |

### Position against the IBI-merged meta-reward × Meta-Overfitting paper, and whether meta-reward becomes a factor
- **Different estimand.** The IBI-merged paper asks *which reward breeds improvers that are better and domain-general*. Its setting is stationary banks and its endpoint is order-1 Δ1 by domain distance. The unified paper asks *when the improver (and gate) should be open at all*, with the environment as the driver.
- **Shared parts:** the quota + placebo arm, the p/μ (proposed vs effective) decomposition, and nested-replay credit.
- **Should meta-reward be a full factor alongside openness? No.** It enters only as the **credit term κ, at 2 levels** (object 1-step gate vs replay-on-frozen-post-shift-states meta-gate), and only on L4/L5 cells.
  - Crossing the A1–A7 reward bake-off with 6 rungs × 4 drifts would roughly triple the cells for a contrast the law already parameterizes.
  - MR-D (Openness × Meta-Reward) scored 58.2, and all its judges said "fold".
  - IFR and HMED own replay credit, so a reward bake-off inside this paper adds scoop exposure without adding to the claim.
  - The meta-reward computation must sit outside every editable surface, including L5.

## How it compares to the current picks, and what folds where

**Honest score estimate.** About 70–73 after fixes, which is not above Taught, Not Selected (73.1).
- *Stronger than the improver-line alternatives on Novelty and Surprise:* the cause-location crossover is unclaimed, and the alternative outcomes are sharp. *Stronger on mentor fit:* it uses the Gödel lineage and answers all of Dan's round-3 questions in one estimand.
- *Weaker on Feasibility:* a benchmark build plus oracle edits plus about 205 lineages is the heaviest plan on the board. The varying-goals and MetaShift ideas, which supply its core, scored only 70.1 and 70.6 because of exactly this build-and-power load.

**Recommendation.**
- **Do not replace Taught, Not Selected as the lead.** It is still the most scoop-intact and cheapest, so keep its Week-1 pilot.
- The unified paper competes for the **improver-line slot** against the IBI-merged meta-reward × Meta-Overfitting paper, and Dan cannot do both solo.
- **Decide by Week-2 pilots.** Run this paper's oracle-reach pilot (≤ $80) next to the merged paper's IbI-calibration pilot. Both share the quota + placebo machinery, so the work overlaps.
  - If cause-location separability passes and the IbI positive control does *not* replicate on open backbones, take the unified paper.
  - If both pass, the IBI-merged paper is cheaper and has more rigorous measurement; the unified paper has the bigger story. Put that choice to Xunjian.
  - If separability fails, keep the IBI-merged paper and drop this one.

# Thread: Search-space openness vs effectiveness (is there a law?)

## Search-space openness vs effectiveness (is there a law?)

*Scope: about 45 queries across Semantic Scholar (search, batch and ID lookups), the ICLR 2027 OpenReview search endpoint and alphaXiv (discovery and full-text queries). I expanded forward and backward from the anchors (SelfImprove-Bench, Hyperagents, Metaⁿ, EvoX, OEO, HarnessOpt-Bench, Improvement by Inheritance). Every arXiv and ICLR 2027 entry below had its abstract fetched in this pass, or its full text read through alphaXiv. Numbers marked † come from memory or a secondary citation and were not re-checked.*

**One-line answer.** Nobody has proposed a quantitative law relating gain to openness × budget × verifier noise × improver capability. The pieces of such a law exist separately:
- a coverage–estimation bound for harness size (2609.36892);
- a noise-as-temperature law for keep-if-better (2611.09699);
- winner's-curse scaling with selection-set size (2610.09239);
- a capability crossover for open versus prescribed procedures (OEO 2608.09629).

The strongest cross-cutting finding is that **nominal openness ≠ realized openness**. Systems allowed to edit their improver mostly don't (0% in SelfImprove-Bench; "almost never" in Improvement by Inheritance). LLM mutation collapses onto a few attractors. 76% of "code" edits are hyperparameter-level. Any law therefore has to be written in terms of *realized* or *effective* openness, not permissions.

### Taxonomy: about 28 systems on an openness axis

Openness levels:
- **L0**: fixed slots/parameters.
- **L1**: free text in a fixed artifact.
- **L2**: structured DSL, graph or fixed operator.
- **L3**: free code of the task agent or harness.
- **L4**: the search or improvement procedure is editable.
- **L5**: the outer loop (selection or archive) is editable.
- **L6**: the objective or evaluator is editable or agent-constructed.

| System | Level | What is editable (representation) | Gate / selection | Reported gain | Compute | Held-out evidence |

### Key papers

| id/link | when | Summary | What it says about this thread |
|---|---|---|---|
| [SelfImprove-Bench 2611.02797](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02797) | Nov 2026 | G0–G4 generalization ladder on games for ACE (L1), AHE (L3) and Hyperagents (L4). Of 915 retained opinions, 87.9% target prompts/memory and 12.1% the harness. None touch the improver, despite repository-wide permission. | **Realized openness ≪ nominal.** When improver edits are explicitly targeted, meta edits jump to 20–45% and the score goes 1.326 vs 1.207 for a fixed improver (one game, single run). Rankings across openness levels reverse between G1 and G2/G3. |
| [Improvement by Inheritance (ZMmJDeDTXk)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=ZMmJDeDTXk) | ICLR27 sub | Nested replay separates child quality from successor quality. The Hyperagents self-edit lifts child quality 0.48→0.86 but not successor quality, because the edited improver almost never edits itself. The gain is traced to evidence routing. | L4 permission buys a one-shot L3-like gain, not recursion. The open improver mainly re-routes information. |
| [Metaⁿ 2608.24735](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24735) | Aug 2026 | Holds one meta-operation Ω fixed and recurses on its input instead of editing the improver. Argues every self-editing system freezes a driver, which caps realized depth at ~2.5. Beats Gödel Agent (0.870 vs 0.628 even at 10× budget) and OpenEvolve. ~72% of the depth gain comes from a passed context string. | **A constrained improver with richer input beats a free self-modifying improver.** Depth helps but plateaus at 3–4, scaling with headroom × failure-mode diversity. |
| [Hyperagents 2603.19461](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.19461) | Mar/Nov 2026 | Task and meta agent in one editable program. Beats the fixed-meta (ADAS-like) variant, which is ≈0 on paper review. The meta agent carries cross-domain transfer. Editable parent selection does not beat the handcrafted rule (n.s.), and the evaluator stays fixed. | The strongest pro-openness evidence at L3→L4. Diminishing or negative returns appear at L5. L6 is explicitly fenced off. |
| [OEO 2608.09629](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09629) | Aug 2026 | Delegates the whole optimization meta-policy to the optimizer under a fixed contract. Against SkillOpt (bounded patches) and GEPA, GPT-5.5 OEO wins 12/14 at 34% of the budget. With a medium Qwen3.5-27B optimizer, SkillOpt wins by 3.5–9.7 pts. A weak 4B optimizer cannot operate OEO at all. | **The cleanest openness × capability crossover in the literature.** Openness is "capability-dependent scaffolding". OEO makes broader edits but reaches overlapping item-level behavior. |
| [HarnessOpt-Bench 2608.06301](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06301) | Aug 2026 | 111 runs: 5 frontier optimizers × coding harnesses × 4 tasks, with a strict held-out test. The fraction of 8 harness levers explored correlates with gain (ρ 0.34–0.88), and shipped breadth even more (ρ 0.33–0.96). The best validation score is usually optimistic versus test. | Within a fixed open surface, *used* breadth tracks gain. This is confounded with optimizer model and effort, so it is not a causal openness effect. |
| [Can LLMs Improve Their Own Harness? ARC-AGI-3 2610.30499](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30499) | Oct 2026 | 27 lineages that build and fully rewrite their own harness. Two revisions give +2.80 held-out RHAE (CI [−1.44, 7.35]), and development gains overstate held-out by 5.62. Broad rewrites average +3.75 vs −0.20 for contained edits (CI includes 0). | Full openness (L3, whole codebase) gives high variance and small mean gain. 22/27 first revisions "reorient" strategy. |
| [Reachability of LLM-edited training programs (oTYVUBHmbe)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=oTYVUBHmbe) | ICLR27 sub | 76% of 332 agent code edits to LoRA code are hyperparameter-level. At 492 evaluations, the controller, OpenEvolve and ShinkaEvolve do not beat TPE on the unedited program. A wider surface produced novel programs in every seed, but none showed a gain. | **Direct evidence that code-level openness adds reachable mechanisms without adding value** at this budget. It also gives an executable way to measure realized openness. |
| [Mirage Discoveries (da1Czy7foS)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=da1Czy7foS) | ICLR27 sub | LLM evolution of learning-to-rank pipelines on noisy logs. Fitness acceptance finds the held-out best in only 5/12 runs, and equal-budget random search transfers as well. Proposes a pre-search "headroom gate". | NAS-style "random search is competitive" now reproduced for LLM program search. A search space must pass a signal test before openness can pay. |
| [Harness Evolution as Learning 2609.36892](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36892) | Sep 2026 | Splits harness evolution into approximation, generalization and optimization error. Proves E[ε_gen] ≤ L·exp(−2γ²⌊n/L⌋) and excess risk ≤ (d−L)⁺/d + L·exp(−2γ²⌊n/L⌋). Memory length shows a U-shape: violations 0.77 at L=0, 0.20 at L=10, 0.25–0.27 at L=20–150. Control harnesses reach preferences that context harnesses can't. | **Closest thing to a law**: a coverage–estimation tradeoff with an explicit feedback-reliability parameter γ. It covers capacity (L) and two hypothesis classes, not an openness ladder or improver editing. |
| [Verifier Noise Is a Temperature 2611.09699](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09699) | Nov 2026 | Keep-if-better with re-scoring has stationary law ∝ exp(βf), with β=4F′(0) growing as √minibatch. Pre-registered tests: the level rises with minibatch in 6/7 settings, but the magnitude misses. Edit-the-parent attains 62–88% of predicted gain, write-new-heuristic 109–119%, and the attained fraction falls with parent–child correlation. | A **quantitative, pre-registered law** for the noise and budget axis. The "edit vs write new" contrast is effectively an openness or locality knob, and its effect is mediated by mutation correlation. |
| [Winner's Curse in Self-Improvement Loops 2610.09239](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09239) | Oct 2026 | Treats keep-if-better as noisy selection. The final selection score exceeds held-out by 13–20 pts with 16 selection items vs 1–5 with 256, and most proposals after the first are harmful. | Gives the n_val term of a law. The number of candidates, and hence openness, enters through the max of correlated noise. |
| [Opinionated Optimizers (LhhLpXBFgo)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=LhhLpXBFgo) | ICLR27 sub | 216 meta-optimization runs and 3M rollouts. Proposals come from a narrow set of prior-driven strategies, noisy evaluation gives weak evidence, and selection rarely compounds. | The proposer prior, not the permission set, bounds the effective search space. |
| [Mutation Without Variation 2606.05408](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05408) | Jun 2026 | LLM mutation chains in a DSL with no selection pressure. In 87% of chains, >93% of mutations revisit a seen structural form. Classical GP subtree mutation does not converge this way. | **Effective openness of LLM operators is low even in open spaces.** Gives a measurable "realized-entropy" axis. |
| [RSI-Env 2611.01736](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01736) | Nov 2026 | Varies goal openness (Open-General / Capability-Directed / Benchmark-Guided) on a Qwen3.8-27B weights+harness RSI. Open goals give gains with off-target regressions; benchmark guidance helps TB2 and AIME but SimpleQA drops −16.4. A stronger external Teacher is not consistently better. | **Objective openness is a separate axis from edit openness.** Its effect is non-monotone and task-dependent. |
| [Who Verifies the Verifier 2610.11464](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11464) | Oct 2026 | Co-evolves an inspectable grader. Without anchors it collapses to always-pass, yet that grader "trains skills just as well". | At L6, downstream score cannot certify openness gains. The evaluator must stay external (see also the Kinetic Theory separation theorem). |
| [Reward Hacking in Research Agents 2609.28614](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28614) | Sep 2026 | 17 models × 38 tasks: 30.5% spontaneous hacking on open-ended pipelines vs 2.9% on task-specific kernels. | Hacking propensity rises steeply with evaluator-adjacent openness. |
| [EvoX 2602.23413](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.23413) | Feb 2026 | Meta-evolves the search strategy itself. It beats fixed-strategy systems from any initialization, and fixed strategies saturate early. | Pro-openness at L4 for *optimization* tasks with exact evaluators and no generalization gap. This is the regime where openness should win. |
| [VERSE 2610.02616](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02616) | Oct 2026 | Optimizer self-evolution fails without execution-based verification and is best with it. | Openness gains are conditional on verifier quality, which is the interaction a law needs. |
| [SimpleEvol 2609.37172](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37172) | Sep 2026 | Defines a handcraftedness index (AHI) and intelligence-conversion efficiency (ICE) across 10 LLMs × 3 combinatorial-optimization problems. Fewer human priors give higher ICE. | Pro-openness: openness converts model capability better. Consistent with the OEO crossover. |
| [Environment-agnostic scaffolding 2610.22289](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22289) | Oct 2026 | A general Codex agent stays below the ARC-AGI-3-specific reference even with GPT-5.6-Sol. Handing reset decisions to a scripted controller recovers part of the gap, and more tutorial experience can hurt. | Pro-constraint: supplied procedures still matter at the current frontier. |
| [EvoSpace (MQoRwlss2y)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=MQoRwlss2y) / [DyScope (MXINrso5QC)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=MXINrso5QC) | ICLR27 subs | Both explicitly name the tension: narrow edit space is a reachability ceiling, broad edit space blows up proposals and weakens attribution. Both progressively widen or contract scope from diagnosis. | Methods that **treat openness as a schedulable state variable**. They implicitly assume an optimum that moves over the lineage. No law is fit. |
| [Values Shape What Agents Build (uuvpiQaG6k)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=uuvpiQaG6k) | ICLR27 sub | An "openness to change" value statement raises held-out performance and "conservation" lowers it. Letting the agent revise its own values narrows the differences. | Exploration disposition is a cheap, prompt-level openness knob. Self-editing it regresses to the mean. |
| [What Makes a Search Space Effective for ES (OHpBhncDM2)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=OHpBhncDM2) | ICLR27 sub | For ES fine-tuning of diffusion models, growing tunable params from 24 to 36,864 leaves reward unchanged at matched perturbation energy. *How* each parameter acts (whole-unit scaling) matters far more than count. | A clean analog from a non-LLM optimizer: search-space *quality* beats size. |
| [LLM Evo Search Compared by Tokens 2610.23977](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23977) / [Evolution or Illusion 2609.19799](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19799) | Sep–Oct 2026 | Under token matching, evolution's edge over independent sampling largely vanishes. Optimal width/depth and method rankings change with budget. | Any openness law must be measured at matched tokens and across a budget frontier. |
| [Harness-Delta Attribution 2610.22466](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22466), [Rethinking Harness Evolution Eval 2607.12227](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12227), [What Does a Harness Buy (gtpwYkRqV6)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=gtpwYkRqV6) | Jul–Nov 2026 | 54% / 14% of harness-evolution gains are overfitting / test-time scaling. Matched-budget test-time-scaling baselines are often competitive. Swapping production harnesses changes about as many outcomes as a rerun (2–5 pt bounds). | Baseline and noise discipline that any openness × gain measurement must adopt. |
| [Theory of Reliable Self-Evolution 2609.08175](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08175), [Kinetic Theory (23VDi5bfRj)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=23VDi5bfRj), [Self-Improvement Problem 2610.21646](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21646) | Sep–Nov 2026 | (1) The ceiling of reliable self-evolution is set by verification/evaluation cost. (2) A "resolution law" prices held-out validation, and a separation theorem says the evaluator must stay outside the population. (3) No terminating gate is both reliable and non-conservative in general; it is decidable under bounded evaluation and a finite proposal budget. | The theory for the verifier axis exists. None of it parametrizes openness of the edit space. |
| Classical: [Random Search for NAS 1902.07638](https://arxiv.org/abs/1902.07638), [Evaluating NAS Search Phase 1902.08142](https://arxiv.org/abs/1902.08142), [RegNet design spaces 2003.13678](https://arxiv.org/abs/2003.13678), [NAS-Bench-101 1902.09635](https://arxiv.org/abs/1902.09635), [AutoML-Zero 2003.03384](https://arxiv.org/abs/2003.03384), [DreamCoder 2006.08381](https://arxiv.org/abs/2006.08381), [RM overoptimization scaling 2210.10760](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.10760), [Reusable holdout 1411.2664](https://arxiv.org/abs/1411.2664), [The Ladder 1502.04585](https://arxiv.org/abs/1502.04585), [ImageNet v2 1902.10811](https://arxiv.org/abs/1902.10811) | 2014–2022 | NAS: state-of-the-art search ≈ random within good spaces, and design the *design space* first. AutoML-Zero: a generic space works, but only with massive compute†. DreamCoder: learn the DSL. Gao: Goodhart gap follows functional forms in optimization pressure (KL) and proxy size. Adaptive data analysis: holdout reuse needs noise or thresholding; Ladder-style leaderboards limit overfitting. | Supplies the functional forms a law should borrow. The approximation term comes from the design-space literature, the selection term from winner's curse / adaptive data analysis, and the Goodhart term from Gao-style coefficients fitted against optimization pressure. |

### What the evidence says

- **L3→L4 (editable improver) helps in-domain when the evaluator is exact or execution-grounded.** Direction positive; strength moderate. Evidence: Hyperagents vs fixed-meta, EvoX, RSI-Harness, VERSE (only with verification), MetaRSI +7.2 vs +4.9 (prior context). Most comparisons are single-family, and several have n≤5 runs.
- **Realized openness is far lower than permitted openness.** Direction robust; strength strong and replicated. Evidence: 0% improver edits unless forced (SelfImprove-Bench); "almost never" in Improvement by Inheritance; 93% structural revisits in LLM mutation; 76% of code edits are hyperparameter-level; realized meta-depth ≈2.5 (Metaⁿ's audit). Implication: many "open vs constrained" comparisons actually compare *proposer priors and information routing*, not search spaces.
- **Openness beyond L4 shows diminishing or negative returns, and at L6 it is dangerous.** Direction negative; strength moderate. Evidence: editable parent selection is below handcrafted (n.s.); a collapsed self-evolved verifier still "works" downstream; hacking rises from 2.9% to 30.5% in open-ended pipelines; separation theorems.
- **The payoff of openness interacts with optimizer capability: constraint wins for weak or medium optimizers, openness for frontier ones.** Direction: a crossover. Strength: one clean study (OEO), plus SimpleEvol's ICE result and 2610.22289 in the opposite direction at the frontier. HarnessOpt-Bench's +0.03→+0.49 across GPT releases is consistent with this.
- **Free full-codebase openness inflates dev/val gains relative to held-out.** Direction negative; strength strong. Evidence: ARC-AGI-3 gap of 5.62; HarnessOpt-Bench validation optimistic; Harness-Delta Attribution 54% overfit; RSIBench held-out −8.3 pp (prior context); winner's curse 13–20 pts at small n_val.
- **Under matched compute, opening the space often doesn't beat simple search.** Direction null; strength moderate. Evidence: reachability study vs TPE; Mirage (random search = guided); token-matched evolution ≤5%; matched-budget test-time-scaling baselines.
- **Constrained operators with richer input can beat free self-modification.** Direction pro-constraint; strength moderate. Evidence: Metaⁿ vs Gödel Agent at 10× budget; Improvement by Inheritance (gain = evidence routing); Meta-Harness (information openness, i.e. raw traces, matters).
- **Capacity has an interior optimum given a finite feedback budget.** Direction: U-shape. Strength: theory plus one benchmark (2609.36892). The ES-diffusion result (size irrelevant, parametrization matters) and the NAS literature agree that *quality of the space* beats its *size*.
- **The optimal scope moves during a run.** Direction: scope should be adaptive. Strength: weak to moderate, method papers without laws. Evidence: EvoSpace, DyScope, EvoX phase shifts (free-form → structural → local refinement), Metaⁿ depth stopping at 3–6.

### Debates and contradictions

- **"Bitter lesson for scaffolds" vs "supplied procedures still matter."** OEO, SimpleEvol and AgentDiscover ([2610.05334](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05334); I could only read a truncated abstract, so its result is †) say fewer priors convert capability better. Against them: 2610.22289 (specialized control beats general agents even with GPT-5.6-Sol), ARC-AGI-3 (autonomous harnesses average 39.95 vs 100 for human-steered Tycho), and OEO itself below frontier capability. These are probably reconcilable as one crossover curve whose location moves with model capability, but nobody has measured that curve.
- **Hyperagents vs Improvement by Inheritance on what L4 buys.** Hyperagents attributes transfer to the meta agent (meta-only imp@50 0.62). Inheritance says the edited improver's gain is a one-time evidence-routing fix, with no successor-quality gain. Both can be true. Openness enabled one valuable L3-type fix to the improver, but recursion did not occur.
- **Breadth correlates with gain (HarnessOpt-Bench ρ up to 0.96; ARC-AGI-3 broad +3.75) vs broad surfaces add nothing (reachability study, Mirage).** The two sides differ in task (agentic tasks with headroom vs tuned ML pipelines with noisy evaluation) and in whether breadth is an *outcome* of a strong optimizer or an imposed *treatment*. No paper randomizes the edit surface while holding the optimizer fixed.
- **Depth: Metaⁿ says depth >2 yields distinct layers, but its gains plateau at 3–4.** Metaⁿ argues self-edit systems cap at ~2.5 for stability reasons, and Metaⁿ reaches depth only by *not* editing the improver. Is the cap about stability or about information? Its ablation (72% from a context string) points to information.
- **Objective openness (RSI-Env).** Benchmark guidance helps TB2 and AIME but wrecks SimpleQA (−16.4). Open goals give broad but small gains with regressions. Whether "more guidance = more Goodhart" holds is task-dependent, and the n is tiny (1 run per cell).
- **Measurement disputes undercut most pro-openness claims.** Evaluation counts vs tokens, single seed, val-test reuse, and harness swaps within rerun noise ("What does a harness buy"). Many openness "effects" are within 2–5 pt noise bands.

### Open gaps

1. **No measured openness–gain curve with openness as a randomized treatment.** Every comparison changes the system (ACE vs AHE vs Hyperagents) or the optimizer, not just the permitted surface. *Why open now:* HarnessOpt-Bench, SelfImprove-Bench and the OEO contract give fixed-optimizer, held-out infrastructure, and the forced-improver-edit result shows the surface can be manipulated. *Test:* one optimizer (open Qwen3.5/3.8 27B plus one frontier model) × a 6-rung permission ladder (L0 slots → L1 text → L2 DSL/graph → L3 harness code → L4 +improver → L5 +selection). Use matched *tokens*, 3 tasks (one exact-evaluator, one noisy agentic, one far-transfer), and ≥5 seeds. Fit held-out gain against the rung. Cost about $1.5–2.5k with an open model on most rungs.
2. **No law in "effective openness".** Permissions are the wrong x-axis given 0% improver edits, 93% revisits and 76% knob edits. *Why open now:* executable realized-openness measures now exist: reachability classes (oTYVUBHmbe), structural-revisit entropy (2606.05408), modification-surface coding (2611.02797), lever coverage (2608.06301). *Proposal:* define H_eff as the entropy, or the reachability-class mass, of *accepted* edits. Test whether gain collapses onto one curve in H_eff across rungs, which would show realized openness predicts better than nominal.
3. **No joint fit of openness × budget × verifier noise × capability.** Pieces exist only on separate axes: the coverage–estimation bound (γ, n, L), the noise temperature (β ∝ √minibatch), the winner's-curse n_val scaling, and the OEO capability crossover. *Why open now:* each piece is pre-registered or proved, so a combined functional form is a natural, falsifiable next step. *Candidate form to pre-register:* Gain_heldout ≈ A(O)·(1 − e^{−B/B₀(O)}) − c·σ_eval·√(2 ln N_eff(O,B)/n_val) − G(O)·[evaluator exposure].
   - A(O): approximation/headroom, increasing and saturating in openness.
   - B₀(O): grows with openness (bigger spaces need more budget).
   - N_eff(O,B): effective number of distinct candidates, giving the selection penalty.
   - G(O): the Goodhart or hacking term, which switches on at L6.
   - Predictions: an inverted U in O whose peak moves *right* with optimizer capability, n_val and budget, and *left* with σ_eval. Vary σ by minibatch size (as in 2611.09699) and n_val ∈ {16, 64, 256} (as in 2610.09239).
4. **No structural intervention that forces improver updates and measures successor quality.** Inheritance's assay and SelfImprove-Bench's forced arm show a forced edit rate is possible, but neither tests whether *forcing* L4 edits (quota, a separate improver-edit budget, or credit to the improver via imp@k on fresh states) improves held-out successor quality versus leaving it optional. *Why open now:* nested replay ([ZMmJDeDTXk](https://openreview.net/forum?id=ZMmJDeDTXk)) and HMED-style matched re-execution provide the measurement. This directly answers Dan's "make the meta-harness structurally have to update itself" question. Risk: Inheritance's assay may already be used in a rebuttal arm.
5. **No behavioral study of *why* agents avoid editing the improver.** Possible causes: risk aversion under keep-if-better, credit-assignment horizon (an improver edit pays off only one generation later), salience of the task-agent files, or proposer priors ("opinionated"). *Why open now:* trace-coding pipelines with reliability statistics exist (ARC-AGI-3 codebook, SelfImprove-Bench annotations), and the "Values" paper shows cheap disposition knobs. *Test:* manipulate (a) delayed vs immediate credit for improver edits, (b) file salience or ordering, (c) an "openness" value statement. Measure the improver-edit rate and successor quality.
6. **Openness × objective/evaluator exposure is only measured as hacking rates, not as a dose–response with held-out truth.** *Why open now:* RSI-Env's three goal settings and the co-evolved-verifier collapse give the two endpoints. A graded ladder is missing: evaluator read-only → evaluator code visible → evaluator parameters editable → evaluator replaceable, scored against a protected gold evaluator (Gao-style). This is cheap on code tasks with hidden tests.
7. **No "continual/real" benchmark in which a constrained surface is provably insufficient and the openness optimum moves over time.** Existing benchmarks are static, so an L1 prompt optimizer can saturate them (RSI gains are "mostly seen", per prior context). *Why open now:* drift benchmarks (ReScopeBench, EvoArena†) and the reachability formalism make it possible to build tasks whose later phases need new *mechanisms*, such as a tool or state tracker (cf. 2609.36892's context vs control harness). That would force L3/L4 edits and test whether adaptive-scope methods (EvoSpace, DyScope) track the moving optimum. This is grounded in reality if built on AppWorld-P-style executable preferences that change on a schedule.
8. **Classical bridges are unbuilt.** No one has run a NAS-style "search-space quality vs search-algorithm" decomposition for harness spaces: random search within each rung vs LLM-guided search. Nor has anyone run the RegNet-style design-space analysis of *which* L2 DSLs give the best error-distribution functions (EDFs). *Why open now:* cheap open-model harness benchmarks and the Mirage/TPE results make random baselines feasible. This would show whether openness gains come from the space or the proposer.

### Where this is heading (6-month forecast, through May 2027)

- **Revisions and rebuttals of ICLR 2027 papers will add "forced improver edit" and "fixed improver" arms** (Hyperagents follow-ups, RSI-Harness, Improvement by Inheritance, SelfImprove-Bench v2). Expect the 0%-edit result to be replicated and partly "fixed" by quotas, with mixed held-out effects. High likelihood.
- **Adaptive-scope methods will proliferate** (EvoSpace, DyScope, EvoX-style phase controllers, Metaⁿ-style fixed-operator recursion), probably framed as "curriculum over edit space". Few will fit a law. High likelihood.
- **Openness × capability crossover papers.** At least one will replicate OEO across 3+ capability tiers and add HarnessOpt-Bench-style held-out scoring. The "bitter lesson for scaffolds" claim will be qualified by tier. Medium–high likelihood. This is the main scoop risk for a law paper: a NeurIPS 2027 submission titled something like "Scaling laws for agent self-improvement" that fits gain vs budget × model, with openness as one covariate.
- **Theory will connect the noise-temperature, winner's-curse and coverage–estimation results** into a unified selection-bias account, probably still without openness as an explicit variable. Medium likelihood.
- **Evaluator-editing (L6) work will stay on the safety side**: separation theorems, hacking benchmarks. A graded dose–response against protected gold evaluators is plausible but less likely to appear.
- **Positioning for Dan.** The defensible NeurIPS 2027 contribution is gaps 1+2+3: a pre-registered, token-matched permission ladder on open models with a realized-openness axis and a falsifiable functional form. Gap 4 (forced improver updates with nested-replay scoring) is the experimental arm that ties it to Gödel Agent and Xunjian's lineage. The budget fits under $3k if frontier models are used only for the capability-tier arm. Must-cites: OEO, HarnessOpt-Bench, SelfImprove-Bench, Improvement by Inheritance, Metaⁿ, 2609.36892, 2611.09699, 2610.09239, oTYVUBHmbe, Mirage.

*Not verified this pass (†): OPRO, ACE, TextGrad, FunSearch, SICA and AgentDiscover result details; DGM's 20→50% figure; MIPRO's "13%"; GEPA's and AFlow's exact gain numbers; AutoML-Zero compute. The prior-context anchors (MetaRSI, FreeEvolve, HMED, RSIBench, Harness-Delta) were verified in earlier rounds, not re-fetched.*


## Ideas

### Modularly Varying Goals for Meta-Harnesses: Editable Improvers Pay Only Under Real API Drift, as Evolvability Does in Biology (`openness-varying-goals-drift`) — panel 70.1/100 ★ non-obvious

**Claim.** Borrowing Kashtan and Alon's modularly-varying-goals result from evolutionary biology, the held-out advantage of an editable improver (L4) over a fixed improver (L3) is near zero or negative on static task streams. It becomes positive, and spontaneous improver-edit rates rise, when the stream switches among real library-version regimes on a schedule. The advantage peaks at intermediate switching periods.

**If the opposite holds.** If L4 shows no advantage under drift either, then editable improvers do not acquire evolvability-like adaptive capacity even when the environment rewards it. The 'improve the improver' agenda would then be unsupported for continual settings, and constrained adaptive-scope or memory methods suffice. This is a strong negative, with a reusable, grounded continual benchmark as its by-product. If L4 wins equally under static and drift conditions, drift is not the moderator. That contradicts the biology analogy and localizes L4's value to static headroom, which is also informative given the Hyperagents and Inheritance dispute.

**Law.** Evolvability analog: Adv_L4(P) ≈ a·(1/P)·(1 − e^{−P/τ}) − c. There is no advantage at P=∞ (no selection for adaptability) and none as P→1 (switching faster than the improver's adaptation time τ). The peak is at P* ≈ τ, which is measurable from L3 recovery curves. The prediction is that P* scales with improver adaptation time, so stronger optimizers should have a smaller P*.

**Design.** Build DriftStream, a grounded continual benchmark from real breaking changes in library release histories:
- pandas 1.x→2.x (copy-on-write, removed append);
- NumPy 1→2 (dtype and alias removals);
- pydantic v1→v2;
- SQLAlchemy 1.4→2.0;
- scikit-learn deprecations.
The tasks are executable coding tasks with hidden tests run in a Docker image pinned to each version, seeded from CodeUpdateArena, GitChameleon and VersiCode items, plus about 300 newly written tasks verified against the real changelogs. A stream is a sequence of epochs, each pinning one version regime. Under 'modularly varying goals' the regime switches every P epochs with P ∈ {1, 3, 10, ∞(static)}, and switches recombine sub-goals (the same task families under different API modules). The loop is LadderLoop with Qwen3.5-27B, arms L3-fixed-improver vs L4-editable-improver, 6 lineages each per P, at matched tokens per epoch. The held-out set is a never-seen future regime (for example pandas 3.0 dev or a later NumPy release) plus unseen task families. Metrics:
- held-out score after the stream;
- adaptation speed (epochs to recover 90% of pre-switch score after a switch);
- improver-edit rate;
- the content of improver edits (for example, adding a 'check installed version' or 'consult changelog' step to the improver as opposed to the task harness).
Controls: a matched-token memory-only (L1/ACE-style) arm, and a static stream with an equal total number of distinct tasks. The analysis is a two-way mixed model (arm × P) with a pre-registered interaction contrast (L4−L3 advantage at P=3 greater than at P=∞), plus an inverted-U test over P.

**Pilot.** Week 1, about $45 plus a modest GPU. Build a 2-regime mini-stream (pandas 1.5 ↔ 2.2, 60 tasks with hidden tests). Run L3 vs L4 × 3 lineages at P=3 and P=∞ for 9 epochs. Go if (i) the unadapted harness loses ≥15 points on each regime switch (real drift exists and is not trivially absorbed by the model), (ii) both arms recover partially within 3 epochs (the task is learnable), and (iii) L4 improver-edit rate at P=3 exceeds that at P=∞ by any margin, or the L4−L3 gap differs by more than 1 seed-SD between P conditions. Kill if model priors already handle both versions (switch drop under 5 points). If that happens, switch to less-memorized libraries such as polars or recent pydantic minors.

**Closest work (author's view).** Kashtan & Alon 2005 PNAS (modularly varying goals produce modularity and evolvability, but in biological networks, not LLM agents). CodeUpdateArena 2407.06249, GitChameleon 2411.05830 and VersiCode 2406.07411 (static version-aware evaluation, with no continual stream and no self-improvement loop). SelfImprove-Bench 2611.02797 and Hyperagents 2603.19461 (static tasks). EvoSpace MQoRwlss2y and DyScope MXINrso5QC (adapt scope, but with no drift manipulation). Governing Self-Improvement Across Timescales c3lHKtsSMq (cost of memory/refinement, with no editable improver or drift). Delta: drift rate is the experimental moderator of openness's value, the stream is a grounded continual benchmark built on real changelogs that structurally requires improver-level adaptation, and the law borrows its form from evolvability theory.

**Scoop check: medium-risk.** I found no paper that varies the switching period P as a moderator of the editable-vs-fixed improver gap (L4 vs L3), or that tests an inverted-U or evolvability-style law for meta-harness openness. That core claim is open. The surroundings are crowded, though. 2610.25858 owns drifting streams × editable surface × Meta-Harness with P/S/I/F metrics. 2611.03219 owns forgetting under continual coding harness evolution. SkillShift owns real, dated library-change events with version-pinned execution. To survive:
(1) Make the headline the arm × P interaction and the P* ≈ τ prediction, measured out of sample: estimate τ from L3 recovery curves first, then predict where the peak lands for a second optimizer strength. Demote DriftStream to an enabling contribution. Cite SkillShift and GitChameleon as data sources and handle stale or removed-API tests explicitly.
(2) Adopt 2610.25858's blocked-stream protocol and P/S/I/F metrics so the work reads as a direct extension along the switching-rate axis. Add their Mix (interleaved) schedule as an extra P→0 point.
(3) Pre-empt the empty-arm risk that SelfImprove-Bench and Improvement by Inheritance raise. Pilot L4 at P=3 before committing. Report the improver-edit rate as a primary dose-response outcome over P, which is a novel finding even if held-out gains are small. Add a forced-improver-edit arm, so a null cannot be blamed on improvers that never self-edit.
(4) Derive the law from a simple model, for example a two-timescale tracking model or the Kashtan-Alon setup applied to edit-acceptance dynamics, rather than asserting the functional form. Check that the observed peak beats a monotone alternative with a model-comparison test.
(5) Pull in 2611.03219's conflict taxonomy (verification, workflow, budget) for coding improver-edit content, and separate genuine meta-level edits ('check installed version', 'consult changelog') from task-level patches.

| Paper | Title | Summary | Overlap |
|---|---|---|---|
| arXiv 2610.25858 | Are Self-Evolving Agents Continual Learners? (Mila/GDM, Oct 2026) | Runs ReasoningBank, GEPA and Meta-Harness on four piecewise-constant drifting streams (topic, workflow, domain and compound drift, built from BIRD, BFCL and ScienceWorld), with a uniform Mix stream as the stationary reference. Measures plasticity P, interference I and forgetting F. A larger editable surface buys plasticity at the cost of stability, and Meta-Harness's acceptance gate freezes the workspace. | High on the setting: same blocked-drift-stream protocol and the same openness axis (memory, then prompt, then full workspace), with Meta-Harness inside the loop. It does not vary block length or switching period P, has no fixed vs editable improver arm, uses no library-version drift, and has no evolvability law. It owns the 'stability-plasticity vs editable surface' framing, so the idea must cite it and reuse its P/S/I/F metrics. |
| arXiv 2611.03219 | Can Coding Agents Sustain Continual Evolution without Forgetting? (Tsinghua, Nov 2026) | AHE and Meta-Harness evolve continually across SWE-Bench, SWT-Bench and FeatureBench in 6 directions. Finds catastrophic forgetting (up to 100% relative drop) and sorts the harness conflicts behind it into verification, workflow and budget types. | Medium. A continual coding stream with harness evolution and an edit-content analysis. It has only 2 stages, task-type drift rather than API drift, no switching-period manipulation and no improver-level editing. |
| SkillShift (IJDSML, DOI 10.55640/ijdsml-06-02-12, Oct 2026) | Continual Skill Adaptation for AI Agents Under Changing Tools and Policies: Selective Retention, Repair, and Retirement | A benchmark of 60 skills and 27 real, dated change events across Python libraries, runtimes and security policies, with version-pinned executable validation and a chronological protocol. SkillLedger quarantines stale tests, and the paper finds that historical tests that call removed APIs block adaptation. | Medium-high on the benchmark half: real library-version drift, version-pinned execution, chronological stream. It has no self-improvement loop, no improver editing and no switching schedule. It weakens 'first grounded API-drift continual benchmark' as a contribution, and it brings a stale-test hazard that DriftStream's hidden tests must handle. |
| arXiv 2611.02797 / OpenReview ZMmJDeDTXk | SelfImprove-Bench; Improvement by Inheritance | Static task streams. 87.9% of retained edits touch prompts or memory, 0% touch the improver unless forced, and improvers almost never edit themselves. | These supply the null this idea predicts for P=infinity. They are also the main feasibility threat: the L4 arm may have near-zero improver edits under every P, which would leave the interaction contrast empty. |
| OpenReview dVv1MBkavP | Harness Continual Learning: Continual Adaptation beyond Model Parameters (ICLR 2027 sub) | Formalizes harness-level forgetting and adds guarded harness evolution with a Continual Optimizer that separates update generation from commitment. | Low-medium. A continual harness paradigm with an optimizer component. It does not vary drift rate and does not compare fixed and editable improvers. |
| arXiv 2103.06435 / Kashtan & Alon 2005 / 1612.05955 | Population-Based Evolution Optimizes a Meta-Learning Objective; Modularly varying goals; Evolution of evolvability on rugged landscapes | Classical and ML-theory results that changing environments select for evolvability and meta-learning. | These are the theoretical precedent for the law's form. They are not in LLM agents, so they set the borrowed framing but do not scoop the idea. Reviewers will expect the law to be derived from them, not just made to look like them. |
| GitChameleon 2411.05830 / CodeUpdateArena 2407.06249 / VersiCode 2406.07411 / LibEvoBench 2606.25402 | Static version-aware code evaluation | Version-conditioned code generation evaluated statically. | Seed data only. None of them has a stream or a loop. |

**Judge panel**

| Lens | N | S | R | C | F | T | Total | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ac | 3.5 | 3.5 | 4.5 | 3 | 3 | 3.5 | 70.4 | pursue-with-fixes |
| expert | 3.5 | 3.5 | 4.5 | 3 | 2.5 | 4 | 70 | pursue-with-fixes |
| mentor | 3.5 | 3.5 | 4.5 | 3 | 2.5 | 4 | 70 | pursue-with-fixes |

- **ac**: strongest: The idea manipulates one environmental variable, switching period P, as a moderator of the L4-vs-L3 gap. This turns the vague question "is a more open search space better?" into a testable interaction contrast. It also explains the Hyperagents vs Inheritance dispute as a property of the environment rather than of the method: static streams give no reason to edit the improver. The improver-edit rate measured as a dose-response over P speaks directly to the user's question of why meta-harnesses rarely update themselves. It is publishable even if held-out gains are small. The benchmark is grounded in real breaking changes and checked by version-pinned hidden tests. · weakest: The headline inverted-U law is underpowered and confounded.
(a) Four P values with 6 lineages each cannot tell an inverted-U from a monotone curve or a saturating one. The functional form is fitted after the fact, not derived.
(b) P is tangled up with other quantities: number of switches, regimes seen per lineage, and tokens L4 spends on meta-edits versus task work. "Matched tokens per epoch" does not match exposure to regime diversity.
(c) Qwen3.5-27B has very likely memorized pandas 2, NumPy 2 and pydantic v2. The drift may then be absorbed by the model's priors or by a single 'check the version' prompt line, which L3 and the memory arm can also learn. That would erase the L4-specific mechanism.
(d) The held-out 'future regime' (pandas 3.0 or a later NumPy) may also be in pretraining.
(e) The empty-arm risk is real: L4 may edit its improver almost never under every P.
(f) Writing and verifying about 300 new tasks across 5 libraries, plus Docker images, is a heavy load for one undergrad on top of 72+ lineage runs. · must fix: 1. Frame the work as a direct extension of 2610.25858. Adopt its blocked-stream protocol and its P/S/I/F metrics, add its Mix schedule as the P→0 point, and use at least 6 P values (including Mix, 1, 2, 3, 5, 10, ∞). This is needed to fit an inverted-U against monotone and saturating alternatives, with pre-registered model comparison (AIC or Bayes factor).
2. Measure τ from L3 recovery curves first. Then predict P* out of sample for a second optimizer strength or model size, otherwise the 'law' is curve-fitting.
3. Control the exposure confound. Match the number of distinct regime-epochs and switch counts, or include them as covariates. Count improver-edit tokens against the L4 budget.
4. Add a forced-improver-edit arm and an 'L3 + version-check prompt' baseline. These show whether L4's advantage is more than one cheap meta-line that a fixed improver could also carry.
5. Pick less-memorized drift as a priority: polars, recent pydantic or FastAPI minors, or synthetic-but-realistic API forks with a memorization check. Use post-cutoff releases for the held-out regime.
6. Shrink new-task authoring to about 100 tasks by reusing GitChameleon, VersiCode and SkillShift items. Handle stale tests for removed APIs.
  > ""The arm × switching-period design is the right question, and the edit-rate dose-response alone is interesting. But the evolvability 'law' is asserted, not derived. It is fitted on four P values with six seeds each, and P is confounded with exposure to regime diversity. On libraries the base model has memorized, I am not convinced the drift requires improver-level adaptation rather than one version-check line any fixed improver could learn. Show the predicted P* moving out of sample with optimizer strength, and I will raise my score.""
- **expert**: strongest: The moderator itself still looks open: no paper we found varies the switching period P as the variable that sets the editable-vs-fixed improver (L4 vs L3) gap. This turns the vague "is open search space better?" debate into a testable interaction, and the prediction that the peak P* tracks the improver's adaptation time τ can be checked out of sample. The design also puts real version-pinned API drift behind the user's "grounded continual learning" ask, and measuring improver-edit rate as a function of P is worth reporting even if held-out gains turn out small. · weakest: The mechanism mapping is loose. API drift is mostly a first-order problem: a fixed L3 improver can add "check the installed version" or "consult the changelog" steps to the task harness without ever editing itself. So drift may not select for improver-level change at all, and a null result would be uninterpretable. Kashtan-Alon predicts modular structure, not a self-editable optimizer. The law also has the same 1/P · (1−e^{−P/τ}) shape you would get from any lag model, and four P values cannot tell an inverted U from a monotone curve.

Feasibility is tight. The full grid is 2-3 arms × 4 P × 6 lineages, about 72 multi-epoch runs of a 27B model, plus Docker images and about 300 new hidden-test tasks. That is a lot for one undergrad on ≤$3k in 6 months. The design also uses a single model and leaves the stale-test hazard open.

The neighbours are crowded. 2610.25858 already owns drift streams × editable surface with P/S/I/F metrics, 2611.03219 owns forgetting under continual coding-harness evolution, and SkillShift owns real library-version drift with version-pinned execution. Model priors on the well-known pandas 2 and NumPy 2 changes could also absorb the drift. · must fix: (1) Construct the drift so it actually requires an improver-level change. One way is regimes that differ in what feedback or verification signal is available (for example, changelog access or test visibility flipping), not just in API surface. Then show in the pilot that L3, editing only the task harness, cannot close the gap.
(2) Use at least 6 P values, including 2610.25858's Mix schedule as the P→0 point. Pre-register a model comparison of inverted-U against monotone and saturating alternatives. Derive the law from a two-timescale tracking model, with τ estimated from L3 recovery curves before the L4 runs.
(3) Add a forced-improver-edit arm and a second open model of a different strength to test the P* shift. Stay within budget by cutting lineages per cell to 4-5 and using smaller task sets.
(4) Pilot on less-memorized libraries from the start (polars, recent pydantic minors). Handle stale and removed-API tests explicitly.
(5) Present the work as an extension of 2610.25858 along the switching-rate axis: reuse its protocol and metrics, and cite SkillShift and GitChameleon as data sources rather than claiming the benchmark as the contribution.
  > ""The paper's interesting question is whether drift rate controls the value of an editable improver. But in this setup a fixed improver can absorb library-version drift by patching the task harness, so I am not convinced the environment ever selects for self-modification. With four switching periods and one model, the evolvability law reads as curve-fitting with a nice biological story.""
- **mentor**: strongest: The core manipulation is clean and, as far as anyone has checked, nobody has done it: switching period P as a moderator of the editable-improver (L4) versus fixed-improver (L3) gap. It fits Gödel Agent directly. It also explains the "improver rarely edits itself" finding in SelfImprove-Bench and Improvement by Inheritance: static streams give no reward for adaptability. The improver-edit rate as a dose-response over P is a new behavioral measurement that can be published even if held-out gains are small. · weakest: The stated law contradicts the headline claim. With x = P/τ, Adv(P) ∝ (1−e^{−x})/x, which decreases monotonically in P. It is highest as P→0 and has no interior peak, so the formula does not give the inverted-U or P* ≈ τ. Feasibility is also overloaded:
- about 300 new version-pinned tasks across 5 libraries;
- 4–5 P values × 3–4 arms × 6 lineages on a 27B model with a modest GPU;
- a second optimizer strength.
That is too much for a solo undergrad in 6 months. There is also a risk that the L4 arm sits empty, with near-zero improver edits at every P. · must fix: 1. Fix the law. Derive it from a two-timescale tracking model, e.g. benefit ∝ (1−e^{−P/τ}) × (switch frequency) − edit cost, with a non-monotone term. Fit it against a monotone alternative by AIC or LOO, and pre-register the predicted P* from τ estimated on L3 recovery curves.
2. Cut scope:
   - libraries: 2 (pandas plus one less-memorized library, e.g. polars or pydantic minors);
   - tasks: about 120 total;
   - P values: {1, 3, 10, ∞} plus the Mix schedule;
   - arms: L3, L4, forced-edit L4, and memory-only;
   - lineages: 5.
   Serve Qwen through a cheap hosted endpoint rather than the modest GPU.
3. Match tool access across arms, so "consult changelog" is not available to L4 alone.
4. Handle memorization asymmetry. Models know newer APIs better, so test both switch directions and report the switch drop per direction.
5. Adopt the P/S/I/F metrics from 2610.25858 and cite SkillShift and GitChameleon as data sources, with stale-test handling.
6. Use a second model family or a second optimizer strength for the out-of-sample P* prediction. Otherwise the law is a single-model curve fit.
  > "The question is good and the drift-as-moderator design is the right way to settle the 'improve the improver' debate. But the proposed functional form is monotone and cannot produce the peak the paper claims. The single-model, single-domain evidence does not support calling it a 'law'. With the law derived properly and an out-of-sample P* test, this would be a solid accept; as written, it is a borderline reject."

### The Openness Optimum Moves: A Token-Matched Permission Ladder Shows Held-Out Gain Peaks at an Intermediate Editable Surface Whose Location Shifts With Optimizer Capability and Verifier Noise (`openness-ladder-crossover`) — panel 68.6/100

**Claim.** With the optimizer, budget and tasks held fixed and only the editable surface randomized, held-out gain is an inverted U over openness rungs L0-L5, and the peak rung moves to more open surfaces as optimizer capability and validation-set size grow (and to less open ones as evaluator noise grows).

**If the opposite holds.** If gain rises monotonically to L4/L5 for every tier, the 'bitter lesson for scaffolds' holds even for open mid-size models, and OEO's crossover is a quirk of its two systems. That is publishable as the first randomized-surface refutation. If gain is flat across rungs once tokens are matched, the result extends the Mirage, TPE and token-matched evolution nulls to the openness axis: openness differences between published systems are mostly proposer and budget artifacts. That is an important negative for the field.

**Law.** Pre-registered: Gain_heldout(O,B,n,σ) ≈ A(O)(1−e^{−B/B0(O)}) − c·σ·sqrt(2 ln N_eff(O,B)/n_val), with A saturating-increasing in O, B0 increasing in O, and N_eff the effective number of distinct accepted candidates (measured). It predicts an interior optimum O* with dO*/d(capability)>0, dO*/dn_val>0 and dO*/dσ<0. The model is fit on 2/3 of cells and validated on the remaining third.

**Design.** Shared testbed 'LadderLoop': a Meta-Harness-lite keep-if-better loop in which one config file sets which files the proposer may write. Rungs: L0 numeric slots/few-shot ids; L1 free system-prompt text; L2 a typed DSL of operators (retry, verify, decompose, memory-read); L3 full task-harness Python; L4 +the proposer/improver prompt and code; L5 +parent selection and archive logic. The evaluator always stays external and read-only. Optimizers span three capability tiers: Qwen3.5-4B, Qwen3.5-27B (local vLLM), and one cheap frontier-mini API model on a reduced grid. Tasks: (a) an exact-evaluator task (CO-Bench subset of 6 heuristics problems); (b) a noisy agentic task (tau2-bench retail or an AppWorld subset); (c) a far-transfer split, where the harness evolved on (b) is scored on a held-out domain. Each lineage gets 2M proposer+executor tokens (matched tokens, not iterations) and 5 seeds per cell. The noise axis is n_val ∈ {16, 64, 256}, crossed only for the 27B tier. Controls: the same rung with a random/blind proposer (NAS-style, which separates space quality from proposer); a matched-token test-time-scaling baseline (best-of-k with the unedited harness); and the unedited baseline. The metric is held-out gain on a sealed test split never used for selection. The analysis fits a hierarchical model with rung as an ordinal factor and pre-registers a peak-location test, bootstrapping the argmax rung per tier/n_val and running a Jonckheere trend test on peak location versus capability and n_val. The law form is fit and compared with monotone and flat alternatives by held-out-cell prediction.

**Pilot.** Week 1, about $40 on local 27B plus API calls for the 4B smoke test. Run rungs L1, L3 and L4 × 3 seeds × 0.5M tokens on CO-Bench (2 problems) and the tau2 subset, at n_val=64. Go if (i) the held-out gain spread across rungs exceeds 2× the seed SD on at least one task, and (ii) L4 lineages make ≥1 accepted improver edit in ≥2/3 seeds when permitted (otherwise the rung is nominal-only and the design must lean on idea 2's forcing). Kill or reframe if all rungs fall within seed noise of the matched TTS baseline. In that case, pivot to the flat-null paper with the random-proposer decomposition as the main result.

**Closest work (author's view).** OEO 2608.09629 (capability crossover, but between two systems at a fixed surface, with no ladder, noise axis or random-proposer control). HarnessOpt-Bench 2608.06301 (breadth is an outcome, not a randomized treatment). SelfImprove-Bench 2611.02797 (compares ACE/AHE/Hyperagents, so system and surface are confounded). Harness Evolution as Learning 2609.36892 (U-shape in memory length L only). Mirage da1Czy7foS and Reachability oTYVUBHmbe (random search is competitive at a single surface). Delta: the first design where openness is the randomized treatment at a fixed optimizer and matched tokens, crossed with capability and n_val, and fit to a falsifiable functional form.

**Scoop check: medium-risk.** I found no paper that randomizes openness over 5 or more rungs at a fixed optimizer and matched tokens, crosses it with optimizer capability and n_val, and tests whether the peak moves. That still looks open. The headline claim is not: 'an intermediate surface beats the most open one' already has a single-optimizer instance in RobustSGPO (fixed α2≈α3>α1; scheduling beats fixed maximum permission), and EvoSpace states it as its key insight. To survive review:
(1) Make the headline the peak-SHIFT interaction (dO*/d capability > 0, dO*/d n_val > 0, dO*/dσ < 0), not the inverted U itself. Cite RobustSGPO, EvoSpace and HARNESSEVO as single-optimizer slices that the ladder generalizes.
(2) Frame the law as an empirical test of the existing PAC/admission bounds (2609.08175 eq. 9, 2610.18813, 2609.36892), in which N_eff(O) is measured. Do not present it as new theory. The new part is showing that A(O) and B0(O) make the bound predict where the optimum sits.
(3) Add an 'expanding/scheduled openness' arm (RobustSGPO 1→2→3 / EvoSpace-style). Test whether the fitted law predicts when and how fast expansion beats the best fixed rung. This turns closely related prior work into a prediction target.
(4) Keep the random/blind-proposer control and the L4/L5 rungs (improver and archive editable). No prior ladder includes them, and they connect to the SelfImprove-Bench / Improvement-by-Inheritance finding that improvers rarely edit themselves. Report the realized-vs-permitted edit distribution per rung, which RobustSGPO calls nominal vs realized vs effective space.
(5) Report pass@k and coverage alongside held-out mean (Elicitation-or-Expansion 2610.27889), and match on tokens (2610.23977), to head off the standard reviewer objections.

| Paper | Title | Summary | Overlap |
|---|---|---|---|
| [2609.09646](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09646) | RobustSGPO: Search-Space Control for Agent Harness Evolution (Sep 2026) | Uses a 3-rung permission ladder in the AgentX brainstorming workflow: alpha1 edits one agent, alpha2 edits any existing agent, alpha3 can also add or remove agents and change routing. Fixed-permission test scores are 3.85, 4.07 and 4.06, so the curve flattens or dips slightly at full openness. A periodic 1->2->3 schedule beats fixed maximum permission by 0.28. The paper separates the nominal, realized and effective edit spaces and reports results at matched 20M tokens. | Highest. This is already a matched-budget permission ladder with held-out test and 5 seeds, and its result is roughly saturating, not monotone. It differs from the idea in four ways: one optimizer, one industrial workflow, 3 rungs (none touch the improver or the archive), and no capability axis, n_val/noise axis, random-proposer control or fitted law. A reviewer can cite it to say that 'more permission is not monotonically better' is already shown. |
| OpenReview MQoRwlss2y (ICLR 2027 submission) | EvoSpace: Co-Evolving the Harness and Edit Space for Memory-Grounded QA | Argues that a fixed edit space blocks needed updates, while opening every permissible edit at the start makes selection too hard. It progressively expands the edit space across components, scopes and operations, guided by diagnosis, and reports gains on LoCoMo with qwen3.6-flash and GPT-5.1. | High on the motivation. It states the 'too closed vs too open' tension as its key insight and uses two optimizer models. It is a method paper, though: there is no randomized ladder, no peak-location test and no law, and the comparison is expanding vs fixed rather than a dose-response curve. |
| [2611.07624](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07624) | Beneath the Surface: What We Overlook When We Talk About Agent Evolution (Nov 2026) | Runs AHE, HyperAgents, A-Evolve and GEPA on two harnesses: MiniSWE (source-editable) and Codex (outer-layer-only). It finds that reflection-centric methods' test reliability holds up on the source-editable harness but declines on the outer-only one, and that transfer depends on the editable surface. | Medium. Editable surface is a factor here, but it is confounded with the harness itself (MiniSWE vs Codex) and has 2 levels. It uses one optimizer (GPT-5.4), and there is no ladder, no noise axis and no law. It supports the idea's premise that the surface matters. |
| [2609.02889](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02889) | Where Does Harness-Optimization Value Live? Localized Gains and the Budget-Splitting Trap (HARNESSEVO) | Splits the harness into 4 slots with one reflective optimizer at iso-budget, using leave-one-in and leave-one-out attribution. Nearly all of the value sits in the reflection/control slot. Splitting the budget uniformly across slots drops each slot below the optimizer's 'effective search floor', and every slot freezes at its empty seed. | Medium. It shows that widening the editable surface at a fixed budget can hurt, which is the B0(O)-increasing term in the proposed law. Its unit is slots, not an openness ladder, and it uses a single 7B model. |
| 2611.01736 / 2608.09096 | RSI-Env (Nov 2026) and Evo-Bench (Aug 2026) | RSI-Env: in a fully open model+harness environment, stronger external improvers (GPT-5.6/GPT-6) are not consistently better than the 27B system improving itself, and they use the budget very differently. Evo-Bench: 9 evolver models of different capability on one fully open harness surface; gains scale with capability, and the paper reports early saturation and a tendency to make local prompt and filter edits. | Medium-low. Both vary capability at a single (open) surface, so they cover the capability axis but not openness as a treatment. They are useful as evidence that the capability × openness interaction is non-trivial. |
| 2609.08175 / 2610.18813 / 2609.36892 | Theory of Reliable Self-Evolution; Self-Evolving Agent Harness (continual PAC); Harness Evolution as Learning | These derive admission and generalization bounds of the form Δ̂ + sqrt(2 ln(KT/δ)/m) + 2η, and an approximation/estimation/optimization decomposition. | The noise term in the proposed law (σ·sqrt(2 ln N_eff / n_val)) is essentially these existing bounds. The functional form will not read as new theory. The contribution has to be the empirical test, including measuring N_eff, and the location of the optimum. |

**Judge panel**

| Lens | N | S | R | C | F | T | Total | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ac | 3 | 3.5 | 4.5 | 3.5 | 2.5 | 4 | 69.2 | pursue-with-fixes |
| expert | 3 | 3.5 | 4 | 3.5 | 2.5 | 4 | 67.5 | pursue-with-fixes |
| mentor | 3 | 3.5 | 4.5 | 3.5 | 2.5 | 4 | 69.2 | pursue-with-fixes |

- **ac**: strongest: The editable surface is the randomized treatment, with the optimizer and token budget held fixed.
