That breaks the system-vs-surface confound in OEO, SelfImprove-Bench and Beneath the Surface. The random/blind-proposer control at each rung separates how good the space is from how good the proposer is. The L4/L5 rungs, where the improver and the archive are editable, plus the realized-vs-permitted edit logging, speak directly to the user's question of why meta-harnesses rarely update themselves. The pre-registered peak-shift test and the held-out-cell law validation make the claim falsifiable in a way most work in this area is not. · weakest: The headline is a peak-SHIFT interaction, and the design cannot detect it. An argmax over 6 ordinal rungs with 5 seeds is very unstable under bootstrap. A Jonckheere test on only 3 capability tiers (or 3 n_val levels) has almost no power. The capability axis is also confounded: Qwen 4B vs 27B vs a frontier-mini model differ in coding and tool-use skill, not on one 'capability' dimension. 'Openness' is not a scalar either. L2 (typed DSL) and L3 (free Python) differ in kind, so the 'ordinal rung' assumption, and with it the law's O, is a modeling choice that reviewers will attack. The full grid does not fit a solo undergrad's 6 months and $3k. Roughly 6 rungs x 3 tasks x 3 n_val x 5 seeds x 2M tokens comes to about 540M tokens for the 27B tier alone. Doubling for the random-proposer controls, then adding tau2's user simulator, the 4B tier, the frontier-mini grid and the proposed expansion arm, likely puts it beyond 1B tokens. The law's noise term is borrowed from existing PAC/admission bounds, and RobustSGPO already shows 'intermediate surface beats the most open one' at a single optimizer, so the inverted U by itself is not new. · must fix: (1) Run a simulation-based power analysis from the pilot variance before committing. If it shows the peak shift cannot be detected, collapse to 4 rungs (L1, L2/L3 merged or chosen, L4, L5) and test the interaction as a rung x tier contrast in the hierarchical model, not by bootstrapping argmax. (2) Replace or augment the ordinal rung with a measured openness covariate: N_eff, realized edit-space entropy, or the number of distinct accepted diffs. The law can then be fit on a continuous O and does not depend on the ladder's arbitrary spacing. (3) Make capability within one family (Qwen3.5 4B/9B/27B, or 27B with ablated reasoning budget) so it is not confounded with family. Treat the API tier as a single-cell sanity check. (4) Cut the grid to fit the budget. Use one exact task (CO-Bench) and one noisy task (tau2), and run far-transfer only on the best and worst rungs. Cross n_val only at 2 rungs. Run the expansion arm only at the 27B tier. Write a token and GPU-hour budget in the proposal. (5) Pre-commit the null framing: a flat result plus the random-proposer decomposition is a Datasets & Benchmarks fallback, not main track. Say so explicitly and release LadderLoop as the reusable artifact.
  > "The question is right and the random-proposer control is a real contribution. But the paper's central claim, that the optimum moves with capability and verifier noise, rests on an argmax across six rungs of uneven spacing, estimated from five seeds and three model sizes from different capability regimes. The inverted U itself is already in RobustSGPO. Unless the authors show they have the power to detect the shift, and that 'openness' is a measured quantity rather than a labeling of rungs, this reads as a well-instrumented replication with an aspirational law attached."
- **expert**: strongest: This is the first design that randomizes the editable surface at a fixed optimizer with matched tokens, and it uses a blind-proposer control to separate space quality from proposer quality. The L4/L5 rungs (improver and archive editable) are a real gap that no prior ladder covers. Every outcome is publishable: an inverted U with a moving peak, a monotone curve, or a flat null all teach the field something about how openness differs between published systems. The timing is good. RobustSGPO, EvoSpace and HARNESSEVO have made the 'too closed vs too open' tension a live question without settling the interaction. · weakest: 1. Openness is not one scalar. L0 to L5 change the representation (slots, then free text, then a typed DSL, then Python, then meta-code), not just the amount of freedom, so any 'peak at L2' may really be a 'typed DSL is a good inductive bias' effect. Fitting A(O) and B0(O) with O set to the rung index 0 to 5 is arbitrary. 2. The headline, the peak SHIFT, is badly underpowered. Bootstrapping the argmax over 6 noisy rungs with 5 seeds, then running a trend test over 3 capability tiers and 3 n_val levels, leaves very few independent points. On noisy tau2 or AppWorld the argmax CI will likely cover 3 or more rungs. 3. The 'law' has more free parameters than the cells can identify (A, B0 per rung, c, with N_eff measured), and its noise term is the existing PAC bounds. Reviewers will call it curve-fitting. 4. The compute is heavy for one undergrad. Roughly 270 or more lineages at 2M tokens, plus random-proposer and TTS arms, plus the n_val cross, comes to about 0.6 to 1B tokens with agentic executors, and L4/L5 sandboxing is serious engineering. 5. The 4B proposer will probably fail to produce valid L3 to L5 edits at all. That turns the capability axis into a trivial 'can't use the surface' effect rather than an optimum shift. · must fix: (a) Replace rung index with a measured, continuous openness coordinate per lineage. Candidates are the log-size or entropy of the realized edit distribution, or the number of distinct accepted behaviors (N_eff), and the design should report nominal vs realized vs effective space. Add at least one 'same openness, different representation' pair (e.g., an L2 DSL vs L3 restricted to the same operators) to separate representation from openness. (b) Run a power analysis now from the pilot seed SD. Shrink the grid to fit (e.g., 4 rungs: L1, L2, L3, L4/L5 merged; 2 tiers, 27B plus a frontier-mini; 8 to 10 seeds on the exact-evaluator task), and make CO-Bench the primary test, with tau2 confirmatory. (c) Pre-register the interaction as a direct contrast: does the gain difference between the adjacent rungs near the peak flip sign across tier or n_val? Do not use argmax-bootstrap as the main test. (d) Treat the law as an empirical test of the PAC/admission bounds (2609.08175, 2610.18813), with at most 3 free parameters, and validate it on held-out cells. Add a scheduled-openness arm as an out-of-sample prediction target. (e) Swap the 4B for a model tier that can produce valid edits at L3 and above (e.g., 14B), or report the validity rate per rung so that capability is not confounded with syntax failure. (f) Lead with the peak-shift interaction and cite RobustSGPO and EvoSpace as single-slice priors.
  > "The randomized-surface design is the right experiment, and the blind-proposer control is welcome. But the 'openness' axis mixes representation with freedom, and with 5 seeds the claimed peak shift falls within the bootstrap CI of the argmax. RobustSGPO already shows that an intermediate surface beats the most open one, so the remaining contribution is the interaction, and that is not established at this power. Borderline. I would raise my score if the interaction contrast were significant on the exact-evaluator task with a continuous, measured openness metric."
- **mentor**: strongest: Openness is the randomized treatment, with the optimizer and tokens held fixed. That is the right causal design for a debate that has so far run on confounded system-vs-system comparisons. The L4/L5 rungs, where the improver and the archive become editable, plus the random-proposer control, tie straight into the mentor's Gödel Agent line. They also turn the question "why don't meta-harnesses edit themselves?" into something you can measure: realized edits vs permitted edits per rung. Every outcome is publishable: inverted U, monotone, or flat. · weakest: The headline peak-shift claim is underpowered and only weakly identifiable. You would be bootstrapping an argmax over 6 rungs with 5 noisy seeds per cell, then running a trend test on that argmax across 3 capability tiers. Expect confidence intervals spanning 2-3 rungs, which makes a 'shift' unprovable. Three more problems. The rungs are not one ordinal axis: the L2 typed DSL is not nested inside L3 Python, and L4/L5 change what is optimized, not just how much. A 4B proposer will almost never make valid L3-L5 edits, so its upper rungs collapse to nominal ones. The grid is also too large for a solo 6-month project: 6 rungs × 3 tiers × 3 tasks × 5 seeds × 2M tokens, plus the n_val cross and the random-proposer controls, plus tau2's user-simulator cost. Finally, "optimizer capability" is confounded with executor capability unless the executor is frozen. · must fix: 1. Freeze the executor model across all optimizer tiers, so that only proposer capability varies. Count proposer tokens and executor tokens separately.
2. Run a power analysis from the pilot seed SD before committing to the peak-shift headline. If the argmax CI exceeds 1 rung, change the pre-registered test to a parametric interaction (rung × tier slope in the hierarchical model) instead of an argmax trend.
3. Shrink the grid. Use 4 rungs (L1, L3, L4, L5; drop L0 and L2, or make L2 a side arm). Use 2 local tiers: replace the 4B with an ~8-9B model, and put the frontier-mini on 2 rungs only. Use 1 exact task plus 1 agentic task, with far-transfer as an evaluation of existing lineages, not a new grid. Cross n_val only at 2 levels.
4. Define openness by a measured quantity as well as the rung label: realized edit entropy or N_eff per rung. Fit the law on that measured quantity, so it holds up even if the rung ordering is contested.
5. Lead with the RobustSGPO/EvoSpace delta and frame the law as an empirical test of the existing PAC bounds, as the scoop check recommends.
  > ""A careful randomized ladder is welcome, but the claimed peak shift rests on argmax estimates whose bootstrap intervals overlap across tiers. The 'inverted U' itself was already shown in RobustSGPO. The fitted law adds a measured N_eff to known admission bounds without new theory. I would want the interaction tested parametrically and shown on a second agentic domain.""

### Why Meta-Harnesses Don't Edit Themselves: It's the Credit Horizon, Not the Permission, and Delayed Successor Credit Beats Edit Quotas (`openness-forced-improver-credit`) — panel 64.5/100

**Claim.** Improvers rarely edit themselves mainly because keep-if-better pays an improver edit only one generation later. Giving improver edits delayed successor-quality credit, computed by nested replay, raises both improver-edit rate and held-out successor quality. Structurally forcing improver edits with a quota raises the edit rate but not successor quality.

**If the opposite holds.** If quotas improve successor quality as much as delayed credit does, the bottleneck is exploration or salience, not credit assignment. That is an important design result: cheap forcing works, and the scheduling literature (EvoSpace/DyScope) should add mandatory meta-steps. If neither arm improves successor quality while both raise edit rate, the improver is not a productive edit target at this capability. That generalizes Improvement by Inheritance's 'one-shot evidence-routing' finding into a causal negative that tells the field to stop paying for L4 permissions.

**Law.** Credit-horizon model: P(improver edit) ∝ exp(β·E[Δ_immediate]) under keep-if-better. Since E[Δ_immediate | improver edit] ≈ 0, the rate is ≈ 0 regardless of permission. Under delayed credit it is ∝ exp(β·E[Δ_successor]). The prediction: successor-quality gain scales with corr(imp@k credit, held-out successor quality), so a sufficiently noisy replay credit erases A4's advantage. This is tested by varying k ∈ {1,3}.

**Design.** This uses LadderLoop at rung L4 (task harness plus improver both editable; evaluator external). The optimizer is Qwen3.5-27B, with a frontier-mini replication on 2 arms. Six arms, 6 lineages each, 20 generations, matched total tokens; the replay cost of the credit arms is charged to their budget:
- A0 optional (status quo);
- A1 salience (the improver file is listed first and summarized in the context);
- A2 'openness' value statement (as in uuvpiQaG6k);
- A3 quota (every 4th generation must propose an improver edit);
- A4 delayed credit (improver edits are accepted on imp@k, i.e. mean child gain when the edited improver is replayed from 3 frozen archive states, not on immediate task score);
- A5 = A3+A4.
Tasks: CO-Bench subset (exact) and tau2/AppWorld subset (noisy). Successor quality is measured on a sealed held-out task family. Outcomes:
- improver-edit rate;
- successor quality, the nested-replay gain of each generation's improver on fresh starting states (Inheritance-style);
- final held-out task score;
- realized meta-depth.
The behavioral component codes all rejected and accepted proposals plus the proposer's stated rationale for target choice, using a 10-category codebook (risk aversion, salience, perceived low payoff, uncertainty about the improver's effect, and so on). Two coders label a subset to report κ. The analysis tests the pre-registered ordering on successor quality, A4 > A3 ≈ A1 ≈ A0, using a mixed model with lineage random effects and 1,000 lineage bootstraps.

**Pilot.** Week 1, about $50. Run A0, A3 and A4 × 3 lineages × 8 generations on the CO-Bench subset. Go if (i) A0 improver-edit rate is under 10%, reproducing the SelfImprove-Bench/Inheritance finding in our loop, (ii) A4 raises it above 20%, and (iii) the nested-replay successor-quality metric has a seed SD under half the A0-vs-best-arm difference on at least one task. Kill if A0 already edits the improver at over 25%: the phenomenon does not reproduce, so the work pivots to behavioral analysis only. Also kill if the replay metric is too noisy to rank arms.

**Closest work (author's view).** Improvement by Inheritance ZMmJDeDTXk (measures successor quality and finds that improvers almost never self-edit, but intervenes on neither credit nor forcing). SelfImprove-Bench 2611.02797 (a forced improver-target arm on one game in one run, with no successor-quality metric). Hyperagents 2603.19461 (permission only). Metaⁿ 2608.24735 (avoids improver editing altogether). MetaSkill-Evolve 2607.05297 (two-timescale updates, but no credit-horizon test or forcing comparison). Values uuvpiQaG6k (a disposition knob only). Delta: a factorial causal test of why improver edits are avoided, and of whether structural forcing or credit redesign yields real recursive gain, scored with nested replay.

**Scoop check: high-risk.** No paper found makes the causal claim itself: that improvers rarely edit themselves because of the credit horizon rather than permission or salience. The same goes for the factorial that pits a quota against delayed credit (plus salience and values arms) on successor quality, and for the behavioral codebook of target-choice rationales. The A4 mechanism is not new, though. Replaying improver edits from frozen archive states and accepting them on child gain is essentially IFR (eIJH6HV9LZ). Adopting an improver only when its children are better is RSI-Harness's outer-loop gate (EOrtreQLbv). Delayed descendant credit is HGM/Gödel Ecology clade metaproductivity. Improvement by Inheritance already shows one intervention that raises successor quality.

To survive:
(1) Retitle as a diagnosis paper, e.g. "Why improvers don't edit themselves: a causal decomposition." Present A4 as IFR/CMP-style credit, with an IFR arm and an RSI-Harness-style dual-loop arm, not as a new method.
(2) Make the headline the dissociation: forcing raises the edit rate but not successor quality, while credit raises both. Add a direct test of the credit-horizon law: manipulate E[Δ_immediate | improver edit] by injecting an immediate bonus with no successor content. This placebo-credit arm should raise the edit rate without raising quality, and it separates "pays later" from "pays at all."
(3) Address the RSI-Harness counterexample: its always-forced outer loop does improve the improver. Explain whether that comes from the credit attached to the forcing, and test it with a quota-plus-immediate-gate vs quota-plus-delayed-gate cell (A3 vs A5 already approximates this, so state it explicitly).
(4) Pre-register the k ∈ {1, 3} noise prediction and add k = 5 or a noised replay to trace a dose-response for corr(credit, true successor quality). That curve is the law and is unclaimed.
(5) Keep the behavioral codebook as a co-headline: no one has coded proposers' stated reasons for avoiding the improver. Validate it with κ and link the codes to the arms, e.g. whether salience shifts the "unaware" codes while credit shifts the "low payoff" codes.
(6) Cite LRSI, SelfLineage and EvoTrace. Add 2+ lineages per arm if budget allows, because the A4 vs A3 contrast at n = 6 is fragile.

| Paper | Title | Summary | Overlap |
|---|---|---|---|
| OpenReview eIJH6HV9LZ | Learning Not to Forget How to Improve: Meta-Forgetting in Self-Evolving Agents (IFR), ICLR27 submission | Improvement Function Replay revisits a small set of frozen historical agent states and scores candidate improvement-strategy updates by how well they modify those states. It accepts strategy updates that maximize current gain while limiting declines from fixed historical reference gains. Reports +2.3 to +2.6 pp over current-only search on ALFWorld, OfficeQA and Seal-Hard with Qwen3-32B. | HIGH on the A4 mechanism. Accepting improver edits on gain replayed from frozen archive states is nearly the same as imp@k replayed from 3 frozen archive states. It does not diagnose why improvers avoid self-edits, does not compare against a quota, and measures no edit rate. A4 cannot be presented as a new credit method; it must be framed as IFR-style credit. |
| OpenReview EOrtreQLbv | RSI-Harness: Optimizing the Harness Optimizer via Recursive Self-Improvement | Dual loop. The inner loop refines the task harness with the current improver. The outer loop revises the improver, and a gate adopts the revision only if it produces a better task harness under matched tasks and budget. The improver's same-start gain grows from 1.3 to 6.2 points and transfers up to 4.5x. | HIGH on the claim that structural plus credit redesign makes the improver improve. Its outer loop is both a forced improver-edit schedule and a delayed, child-gain credit for improver edits, and it shows successor-quality gains. It does not separate forcing from credit and has no permission, salience or values arms. It is a direct counter-baseline to the claim that a quota does not help, because its loop is effectively always forced. |
| OpenReview ZMmJDeDTXk | Improvement by Inheritance: Measuring RSI in Coding Agents | Introduces child quality vs successor quality and nested replay. On Hyperagents history, the edited improver almost never edits itself, so successor quality is flat. A modifier 'built to act on evidence about the improvement procedure' produces a clear successor-quality gain under the same design. | HIGH on measurement, plus a partial intervention. It owns nested-replay successor quality and the 'never self-edits' finding, and already shows one intervention that raises successor quality. It offers no causal account of why (no credit-horizon test, quota, salience or values arms) and no behavioral coding. |
| OpenReview N4GJVmrzsF / HGM (Wang et al. 2026, clade metaproductivity) | Gödel Ecology (ICLR27 submission) and Huxley-Gödel Machine CMP | Agents and evaluators are credited by the clade metaproductivity of the lineages they induce. Gödel Ecology explicitly adopts 'delayed credit assignment'. HGM selects ancestors by their descendants' aggregate success. | MEDIUM-HIGH. Delayed descendant credit in self-improving lineages already exists as a method, though it is applied to whole agents or evaluators, not to the improver-vs-task edit-target choice. A reviewer will say A4 is CMP applied to improver edits. |
| OpenReview 71wzcahKcb / nVl2qz74V7 | Lookahead-Guided RSI (LRSI) and SelfLineage: Learning the Long-Term Consequences of Self-Modification | LRSI credits each candidate edit by its contribution in sampled future agent versions and rejects edits that turn harmful later. SelfLineage adds descendant-aware provenance links so that later consequences inform self-modification decisions. | MEDIUM. Both argue that myopic keep-if-better credit is wrong for self-modification and replace it with future or descendant credit. Neither targets improver-edit avoidance or compares forcing with credit. |
| arXiv 2611.02797 / OpenReview uuvpiQaG6k / 2607.05297 | SelfImprove-Bench; Values Shape What Self-Improving Coding Agents Build; MetaSkill-Evolve | SelfImprove-Bench: 87.9% of retained edits touch prompts or memory, and improver edits are retained 0% of the time unless forced. Values: an openness value statement raises held-out performance. MetaSkill-Evolve: a slow-timescale meta-skill loop, i.e. scheduled improver updates. | MEDIUM. Together these cover the A2 values arm, a scheduled or forced improver-update arm (A3-like), and the descriptive edit-target statistics. None runs a factorial causal comparison or reports successor quality. |
| OpenReview a2ZN4PGsoR / vXnQX90fv6 | What Do Evolutionary Coding Agents Evolve? (EvoTrace); RecurLens | EvoTrace codes 10,672 parent-child edits into 9 categories with a human-validated LLM judge. RecurLens audits whether installed methods reach successors and are useful at the next update. | LOW-MEDIUM. Precedent for the behavioral codebook and for successor audits. It is not about improver-target choice or its rationale. |

**Judge panel**

| Lens | N | S | R | C | F | T | Total | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ac | 2.5 | 3.5 | 4.5 | 3 | 2.5 | 4 | 65 | pursue-with-fixes |
| expert | 2.5 | 3.5 | 4.5 | 3 | 2.5 | 3.5 | 64.2 | pursue-with-fixes |
| mentor | 2.5 | 3.5 | 4.5 | 3 | 2.5 | 3.5 | 64.2 | pursue-with-fixes |

- **ac**: strongest: The paper is framed as a diagnosis, and that framing is still unclaimed. It is a factorial that dissociates edit rate from successor quality, with arms for permission, salience, values, quota and credit. It scores them with nested-replay successor quality on a sealed held-out family and pairs this with a kappa-validated codebook of the proposer's rationales. If quota works, the scheduling literature should add forced meta-steps. If nothing works, L4 permissions are not worth paying for. Either way the result is publishable. Relevance to self-referential and meta-agent research is very high. · weakest: The causal mechanism is underspecified, and it is confounded with the treatment. The proposer is an LLM that is not trained, so changing the acceptance rule cannot change P(propose improver edit) through any exp(beta*E[delta]) policy. It can only change it if the proposer is told the rule or sees in-context archive feedback about which edits got kept. That makes A4 partly a salience/instruction manipulation, and "edit rate" mixes proposal rate with acceptance rate. Delayed credit mechanically accepts more improver edits even if proposals do not change. Separately, the A4 mechanism itself is already IFR/RSI-Harness/HGM-CMP, so novelty rests entirely on the dissociation and the codebook. The design also has n=6 lineages and a single main optimizer model. Nested replay from 3 archive states over 20 generations on tau2/AppWorld will likely blow through $3k, or the GPU-hours a solo undergrad has, under matched-token accounting. · must fix: (1) Report proposal rate and acceptance rate separately. State exactly what information about the acceptance rule and past credit reaches the proposer's context in each arm. Add a "rule-disclosed but immediate gate" control and a "delayed gate but rule undisclosed" control, so that credit (selection) is separated from belief about credit (instruction). Without these the credit-horizon law is untestable. (2) Add the placebo immediate-bonus arm and an explicit RSI-Harness-style quota+delayed-gate cell (A5). Frame A4 as IFR/CMP credit, not as a new method. (3) Pre-register the noise-dose curve, using k in {1,3,5} plus injected replay noise against corr(credit, held-out successor quality). That curve is the only novel 'law'. (4) Budget the work honestly: cap the noisy task to a small subset or drop it, raise lineages to 8 or more on the main contrast, and run a power analysis from the pilot SD. (5) Add a second open optimizer family, not just a 2-arm frontier-mini replication.
  > "The question is a good one and the factorial is cleaner than anything in Inheritance or SelfImprove-Bench. But the proposed law treats a frozen LLM proposer as if it were a reward-trained policy. Unless the authors show what signal the delayed-credit arm actually puts in the proposer's context, the A4-vs-A3 gap could just be a better-worded prompt plus a looser acceptance gate. The credit mechanism itself is IFR/RSI-Harness. I would accept only if the proposal/acceptance decomposition and the noise dose-response survive with adequate lineages."
- **expert**: strongest: The question is sharp and nobody has claimed it: a factorial causal decomposition of why improvers avoid editing themselves (permission vs. salience vs. disposition vs. credit horizon), scored on nested-replay successor quality and not on edit counts. The pre-registered dissociation is a clean, falsifiable headline: forcing raises the edit rate while successor quality stays flat, and credit raises both. Every cell of the outcome is publishable. If quotas work, the conclusion is "cheap forcing suffices." If neither works, it is a causal negative against paying for L4 permissions. The codebook of proposers' rationales for target choice (with κ) is also unclaimed and directly useful to the mentor's self-referential-agent agenda. · weakest: The mechanism story has a conceptual gap, and the method is already taken. (a) The optimizer is a frozen LLM, not a policy trained by RL. Changing the acceptance rule does not change P(propose improver edit) directly. It can only work indirectly, through what the archive and in-context history then show the proposer. So the 'law' P ∝ exp(β·E[Δ]) is a softmax relabeling, not a model the design actually tests. Without separating the proposal rate from the acceptance rate, A4 can raise the 'edit rate' purely by definition. (b) A4 is IFR (eIJH6HV9LZ), the RSI-Harness outer-loop gate, and HGM CMP, so novelty rests entirely on the diagnosis and the codebook. (c) The budget does not add up. 6 arms × 6 lineages × 20 generations, plus nested replay from 3 archive states, plus a sealed held-out family, plus tau2/AppWorld agentic rollouts, is far beyond $3k unless the noisy-task arm is cut down drastically. At n=6 lineages, A4 vs A3 on a noisy replay metric will probably fail to separate. · must fix: 1) Report the proposal rate and the acceptance rate separately, and state how the credit rule is supposed to reach the proposer. Either the proposer sees imp@k outcomes in context (then test an arm that shows the delayed credit without gating on it), or there is no causal path, and the claim becomes 'credit-gated selection yields better successors,' not 'credit explains avoidance.' 2) Add the placebo-credit arm: an immediate bonus with no successor content. It is the only arm that actually tests "pays later" against "pays at all," and without it the credit-horizon causal claim cannot be defended. 3) Reframe A4 as IFR/CMP-style credit and include an explicit RSI-Harness-style always-forced plus delayed-gate cell, framed as A5, to answer that counterexample. 4) Cut scope so the design fits budget and power: drop A2, or move it to the pilot only, and run the noisy task family on 3 arms. Put the savings into ≥10 lineages for A0/A3/A4, and fix the replay-noise dose-response (k ∈ {1,3,5} plus injected noise) as the 'law' figure. 5) Pre-register the codebook categories before looking at the data, and validate the link from codes to arms (salience should move the 'unaware' codes, credit should move the 'low payoff' codes).
  > ""The diagnostic question is good, and the forcing-vs-credit dissociation would be a useful result. But the delayed-credit arm is IFR/RSI-Harness under a new name, and the paper never explains how an acceptance rule can change what a frozen LLM chooses to propose. As written, the higher 'edit rate' under A4 may just be a selection artifact. With 6 lineages and a noisy nested-replay metric, I am not convinced the A4 > A3 ordering is real. Weak accept if they add the placebo-credit control and separate proposal rates from acceptance rates; otherwise reject.""
- **mentor**: strongest: The dissociation is a clean, falsifiable diagnosis that nobody has claimed: forcing raises the edit rate but not successor quality, while credit raises both. A placebo-credit arm and a credit-noise dose-response curve would add to it. Every outcome is informative ("forcing works" or "stop paying for L4"), and the behavioral codebook of why proposers avoid the improver is an unclaimed co-headline. Relevance to the mentor's Gödel Agent line is very high. · weakest: Two problems. First, the mechanism is scooped: A4 is essentially IFR (eIJH6HV9LZ), RSI-Harness's child-gain gate, and HGM CMP. Second, the core causal claim has a measurement confound. Under A4 the acceptance rule changes, so the accepted improver-edit rate rises mechanically through selection even if the proposer's behavior never changes. The credit-horizon "law" (softmax over E[Δ_immediate]) assumes the LLM proposer knows the acceptance rule and responds to it. That holds only if the rule or its history is visible in context, and the design never specifies this. Without separating proposal rate from acceptance rate, and without an arm that varies whether the proposer is told about the rule, "it's the credit horizon" cannot be told apart from "the filter lets more through." Feasibility is also stretched. Six arms x 6 lineages x 20 generations, plus 3-state nested replay, plus a noisy tau2/AppWorld task, plus a frontier replication, will blow past $3k or a modest GPU's serving throughput for a 27B model. n=6 lineages per arm on a noisy successor metric is fragile. · must fix: (1) Report proposal rate and acceptance rate as separate outcomes. Add a factor for whether the proposer is informed of the credit rule (rule shown vs hidden), so the behavioral credit-horizon claim is separated from mechanical selection. (2) Cut arms to fund lineages. Drop A2 values and probably A1 salience (or fold salience into the codebook analysis). Keep A0, A3 quota, A4 IFR-style delayed credit, A5 quota+delayed (stated explicitly as the RSI-Harness-style cell), and a placebo immediate-bonus arm. Run 10+ lineages per arm. (3) Use one exact task (CO-Bench) as the primary task. Make the noisy agentic task a small secondary replication or drop it. (4) Turn the law into a pre-registered dose-response: inject calibrated noise into imp@k to sweep corr(credit, true successor quality), and plot successor gain against that correlation. That curve is the unclaimed contribution. (5) Retitle as a causal decomposition and diagnosis paper. Cite IFR, RSI-Harness, HGM/Gödel Ecology, LRSI, SelfLineage and EvoTrace, and present A4 as existing credit, not a method. (6) Budget the nested replay explicitly in the week-1 pilot and confirm the full design fits in $3k before committing.
  > ""The delayed-credit arm is IFR/CMP under a new name, and the reported rise in improver-edit rate under A4 is largely a selection artifact of changing the acceptance gate. The authors need to show that the proposer's own targeting shifts, not just what survives the filter. The quota-vs-credit dissociation and the credit-noise dose-response are interesting, but with 6 lineages per arm on a noisy replay metric, I am not convinced the A4 > A3 ordering is robust.""

### Permissions Don't Matter, Realized Edits Do: Effective Openness Mediates the Effect of the Editable Surface on Held-Out Self-Improvement (`openness-realized-mediation`) — panel 61.7/100

**Claim.** Across rungs and optimizers, held-out gain is predicted by effective openness H_eff (entropy over executable reachability classes of accepted edits). Nominal permission level adds no predictive power once H_eff is controlled for. Raising H_eff at a fixed nominal rung raises gain along the same curve, and widening permissions without raising H_eff does not.

**If the opposite holds.** If nominal rung keeps significant explanatory power beyond H_eff, then which mechanisms are reachable matters even when they are rarely used: for example, the option to edit the improver changes proposer behavior elsewhere. That supports design-space-first work in the RegNet/NAS style and is publishable as 'the space, not the walk'. If H_eff interventions raise H_eff but lower gain, diversity per se is harmful at this budget. That is a clean negative against diversity-forcing methods (EvoSpace, DyScope-style widening).

**Law.** Gain_heldout ≈ g(H_eff) with g concave (rising then saturating or declining). The pre-registered prediction is a single collapse curve across rungs and arms, and the rung coefficient is ≈0 conditional on H_eff (equivalence bound ±0.25 seed-SD).

**Design.** This reuses the LadderLoop runs from openness-ladder-crossover and adds causal arms. H_eff is computed from each accepted edit, which is classified automatically into reachability classes: AST diff with a file/function target; a semantic class (hyperparameter/knob, prompt text, control-flow mechanism, new tool or state, improver logic, selection logic) assigned by a frozen classifier and validated on 200 hand-labelled edits with Cohen's κ; and structural-revisit rate (as in 2606.05408). H_eff is the Shannon entropy of the class mass over accepted edits, alongside a 'novel-mechanism mass'. The 27B optimizer runs at rungs L2, L3 and L4, with 5 seeds and 2M matched tokens each, in four arms. A0 is the baseline. A1 raises H_eff at fixed rung: target-file rotation plus a class-coverage bonus in the proposal prompt, with the gate unchanged. A2 widens nominal permission one rung while freezing H_eff, using rejection sampling of proposals so the class distribution matches the lower rung. A3 lowers H_eff, restricting proposals to the modal class. Tasks are CO-Bench (exact) and tau2/AppWorld (noisy), with a sealed held-out split. The analysis is a mediation analysis (rung → H_eff → gain) with lineage-level bootstrap, plus a curve-collapse test: fit gain = f(H_eff) on the A0 arms and predict the A1, A2 and A3 cells out of sample, comparing that against a rung-based model by held-out-cell RMSE.

**Pilot.** Week 1, about $35 with local 27B. Re-code the pilot lineages from openness-ladder-crossover (L1/L3/L4) plus 2 seeds of A1 at L3. Go if (i) classifier κ ≥ 0.7 on 100 hand-labelled edits, (ii) A1 raises H_eff by ≥0.5 bits over A0 without halving the acceptance rate, and (iii) H_eff varies by ≥1 bit across the A0 lineages. Kill if H_eff cannot be moved by cheap interventions: realized openness would then be fixed by the proposer prior, and the result folds into idea 3's behavioral analysis.

**Closest work (author's view).** Mutation Without Variation 2606.05408 (revisit entropy, but no selection and no link to gain). Reachability study oTYVUBHmbe (76% knob edits, but a single surface and no mediation test). HarnessOpt-Bench 2608.06301 (lever coverage correlates with gain, but this is observational and confounded with the optimizer). Opinionated Optimizers LhhLpXBFgo (narrow proposer priors, descriptive only). EvoSpace MQoRwlss2y and DyScope MXINrso5QC (adapt scope as a method, with no causal or collapse test). Delta: the first interventional test of whether realized, rather than permitted, openness is the causal variable, plus a collapse-curve law.

**Scoop check: high-risk.** RobustSGPO (2609.09646) already introduces S_nom vs S_real vs S_eff. It already claims that broader permissions do not by themselves help, and it shows that category-directed control at fixed permission raises realized coverage and held-out quality. So 'first to distinguish permitted from realized openness' and 'first interventional evidence' will not survive. The claim also has to absorb the null results of oTYVUBHmbe and 2610.30499.

1. Cite RobustSGPO as the source of the nominal/realized distinction and of the single-workflow intervention. Present the contribution as the formal causal test it lacks: mediation with a pre-registered equivalence bound on the rung coefficient, and the A2 arm (widen permission while freezing the realized class distribution by rejection sampling). No neighbour runs A2, so make it the headline experiment.
2. Make the generality claim carry the paper: more than one optimizer (the 27B model plus one frontier optimizer and a GEPA/OpenEvolve-style baseline), an exact task (CO-Bench) and noisy tasks (tau2/AppWorld), and out-of-sample curve-collapse prediction across optimizers. RobustSGPO is one industrial workflow with an LLM-judged 0-5 score.
3. Use effective (accepted and valid) entropy, not coverage, and show it beats RobustSGPO-style coverage and HarnessOpt-Bench lever coverage as a predictor of held-out-cell RMSE. Otherwise H_eff is just a renamed coverage metric.
4. Make novel-mechanism mass co-primary and pre-register the competing hypothesis from oTYVUBHmbe: realized novelty raises the search-set score but not held-out gain, which is the elicitation story of 2610.27889. Frame the result as a two-sided test, 'which openness variable carries the effect, if any', so that a null curve is still publishable.
5. Separate A1 from RobustSGPO's planned category sampling and DyScope's target redirection. Either adopt their controller as A1, which credits them and makes this a replication across optimizers, or show that class-coverage bonus prompting is a distinct manipulation. Report the cost per unit of H_eff raised.

| Paper | Title | Summary | Overlap |
|---|---|---|---|
| arXiv:2609.09646 | RobustSGPO: Search-Space Control for Agent Harness Evolution (Sep 2026) | Formally defines a nominal (permitted) edit space S_nom, a realized space S_real and an effective space S_eff (valid realized edits). It says outright that 'broader permissions need not yield more valid edits or higher quality' and that the model 'concentrates on familiar instruction rewrites'. It uses 3 permission levels (α1-α3) over 95 runs and 7,350 attempts with a 30-task held-out split. Fixed α3 is no better than α2 (4.06 vs 4.07). Its controls (planned category sampling, target enforcement, typed structural ops, a category archive) raise effective scope×operation coverage from 48% to 68% and held-out quality from 3.82 to 4.30. | HIGH on framing. It already claims the permitted-vs-realized gap as a contribution, and its controls are essentially the A1 arm (category-directed proposals that raise realized coverage at fixed permission, which tracks held-out gain). What it lacks: an entropy metric (it measures coverage fraction), any mediation or equivalence test of the rung coefficient, the A2 'widen permission but freeze the class distribution' arm, multiple optimizers or tasks (one industrial brainstorming workflow), and a collapse-curve law. |
| OpenReview oTYVUBHmbe | Rethinking Program Search for AutoML: A Reachability Analysis of LLM-Edited Training Programs (ICLR27 sub) | Sorts every edit into 4 executable reachability classes. Of 332 primary edits, 76% are at hyperparameter level. Given a wider surface, the agent produced uncovered programs in every seed, but none showed an established gain over a tuner given the extension. | Medium-high. It supplies the reachability-class machinery, and its wide-surface arm is a direct counter-datum: higher realized novelty without gain. This threatens the monotone or concave g(H_eff) prediction on exact tasks. |
| arXiv:2610.30499 | Can LLMs Improve Their Own Harness? A Controlled Study on ARC-AGI-3 (Oct 2026) | 27 lineages with a held-out set of 20 games. Every revision is coded Preserve/Extend/Reorient/Replace with outcome-blind dual coding (scope κ=0.96). Broad rewrites average +3.75 held-out vs −0.20 for contained edits, but the CI includes 0. 'Strategy change is common, and its presence or coarse type does not certify improvement.' | Medium. This is an observational analysis linking realized-edit class to held-out gain, and its result is null. It should be cited as prior evidence that realized-class type alone may not predict gain. |
| OpenReview MQoRwlss2y / MXINrso5QC | EvoSpace (co-evolving harness and edit space) and DyScope (dynamic scope for program evolution) (ICLR27 subs) | Both are methods that adapt the permitted edit boundary. DyScope compares the requested boundary with the realized parent-child diff and rotates or redirects targets. EvoSpace argues that a fixed space precludes needed updates while an all-permitted space makes selecting edits harder. | Medium. DyScope's target redirection is close to the A1 arm (target-file rotation). Neither paper runs a causal mediation analysis or fits a law. |
| OpenReview LhhLpXBFgo | Opinionated Optimizers: Why Agents Struggle to Improve Agents (ICLR27 sub) | 216 runs and 3M rollouts. Proposals draw on a narrow set of strategies shaped by the proposer's priors, and selection rarely compounds. | Medium. It is the descriptive basis for low realized openness. Its claim that the proposer is the bottleneck is the observational version of this idea's claim. |
| arXiv:2606.05408 | Mutation Without Variation (Jun 2026) | Without selection, LLM mutation chains collapse to attractors and revisit structures. It reports revisit entropy. | Low-medium. It is the metric source and links nothing to gain. S2 lists no forward citations. |
| arXiv:2610.34938 | How Do Coding-Agent Harnesses Self-Evolve? (Oct 2026) | Codes 125 /retro proposals into 6 mechanism classes (κ=0.84). 18 of 19 prior-driven proposals are lint checks, and implemented adaptations rarely transfer. | Low. It is a precedent for a mechanism-class codebook. It has no openness manipulation. |
| arXiv:2608.06301 | HarnessOpt-Bench | Lever coverage correlates with gain (from the prior context). Forward citations (10 papers, Sep-Oct 2026) contain no interventional openness study. | Medium, observational. Already listed by the author. |

**Judge panel**

| Lens | N | S | R | C | F | T | Total | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ac | 2.5 | 3.5 | 4 | 2.5 | 3 | 3 | 61.3 | fold-into-another |
| expert | 2.5 | 3 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 60.8 | fold-into-another |
| mentor | 2.5 | 3.5 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 62.9 | fold-into-another |

- **ac**: strongest: The A2 arm is the one experiment no neighbour has run: widen nominal permission while holding the realized edit-class distribution fixed. It is a clean interventional handle on "permissions vs. realized edits". The two-sided framing (H_eff carries the effect / rung carries it / diversity hurts) makes every outcome publishable. It also reuses the LadderLoop runs, so the marginal cost is low. · weakest: The mediator is mechanically tied to the outcome. H_eff is computed over ACCEPTED edits, and acceptance is gated on search-set improvement. So lineages with more or more varied accepted edits will show both higher entropy and higher gain by construction. This is selection-induced (collider-like) correlation, not mediation. The entropy estimate is also biased by the number of accepted edits. Separately, the equivalence claim is badly underpowered. Roughly 12 cells per task (3 rungs x 4 arms) with 5 seeds cannot support a TOST at ±0.25 seed-SD: you need on the order of 100+ seeds per contrast. A2's rejection sampling freezes H_eff by construction, so 'rung adds nothing under A2' only tests within-class content, and the rejection sampling itself shifts the proposer distribution. On top of that, RobustSGPO has already taken the nominal/realized framing and the A1-style intervention, so novelty rests almost entirely on A2 plus a curve fit. · must fix: (1) Define the mediator on PROPOSED or pre-gate edits, or on a fixed-size subsample of accepted edits, with a count-matched entropy estimator such as Miller-Madow or rarefaction. Report that it still predicts gain after controlling for the number of accepted edits. Otherwise the H_eff-gain link is a selection artifact. (2) Replace the ±0.25 seed-SD equivalence bound with a power-justified one. Either commit roughly 20+ seeds on the key L3-vs-L4 A0/A2 contrast, or pre-register a wide bound and call the result 'no large rung effect'. (3) Make A2 the headline experiment, and add an A2-placebo: rejection sampling at the SAME rung, to separate the effect of the sampling procedure from the permission change. (4) Credit RobustSGPO for the distinction and for the A1-style control, and pre-register the oTYVUBHmbe / 2610.30499 null as a competing hypothesis. Show that H_eff beats coverage fraction on held-out-cell RMSE. (5) Fold this in as the causal section of openness-ladder-crossover rather than a standalone paper, and keep any frontier optimizer to a small cross-check within budget.
  > ""The authors compute their 'causal mediator' over edits that were accepted precisely because they improved the score, then report that it predicts the score. Without a pre-gate or count-matched definition, and with five seeds supporting an equivalence claim at a quarter of a seed-SD, the mediation analysis cannot carry the headline. The widen-but-freeze arm is genuinely interesting and deserves to be the paper, but RobustSGPO already owns the permitted-versus-realized framing.""
- **expert**: strongest: The A2 arm is the one design element no neighbor has: it widens the permission while holding the realized class distribution fixed. Combined with an out-of-sample curve-collapse test against a rung-only model, it makes the question 'is permitted or realized openness the causal variable' sharp and falsifiable. It also sits right in the mentor's area (how much freedom a self-referential improver should have) and directly addresses the user's question of whether there is a law relating openness to effectiveness. · weakest: The causal logic is close to circular. (1) H_eff is computed over ACCEPTED edits, and the gate accepts edits based on search-set gain. So H_eff is a post-treatment variable that partly depends on the outcome: lineages that find gains get more accepted edits of more kinds, which inflates entropy. Mediation rung -> H_eff -> gain then fails sequential ignorability, and the reverse path gain -> H_eff can produce the 'collapse' with no causal effect of openness at all. (2) A2 freezes the class distribution by rejection sampling, which cuts away exactly the edits the wider rung newly allows. Wider permission then mostly exists only on paper, so 'rung adds nothing given H_eff' is nearly true by construction. Reviewers will call the headline null on rung tautological. (3) RobustSGPO (2609.09646) already holds the nominal/realized framing and the A1-style intervention, and oTYVUBHmbe and 2610.30499 supply counter-data. What remains is a stats-and-generality upgrade, not a new phenomenon. On top of that, the 'law' is a concave fit on 3 rungs x 4 arms, about 12 cells, which is thin for a collapse claim. · must fix: Measure openness on PROPOSED edits (pre-gate), or on a fixed, gain-independent sample of them, and report accepted-edit H_eff only as a secondary measure. That removes the outcome-dependence of the mediator. Redesign A2 so that the widened permission is actually exercised at a controlled rate: for example, force k% of proposals into the newly unlocked class while matching total entropy by reweighting the other classes. Then 'permission without entropy change' becomes a real manipulation instead of a filter. Add a dose-response design with at least 3 graded levels of the A1 bonus per rung (≥9-12 interventional cells) so that g(H_eff) is identified from intervention rather than observation. Make the predictor contest the pre-registered primary test: H_eff vs coverage fraction (RobustSGPO) vs lever coverage (HarnessOpt-Bench) vs novel-mechanism mass, scored by held-out-cell RMSE. Drop the 'first interventional' claim. Cap the token budget realistically: 3 rungs x 4 arms x 5 seeds x 2 task families x 2M tokens comes to about 240M optimizer tokens plus evaluation, before adding the frontier optimizer the reposition requires. Either cut the noisy-task family to 3 seeds or run the frontier optimizer at L3 only. Given that it reuses the openness-ladder-crossover runs, this works best as the mechanism section of that paper rather than as a standalone paper.
  > "The authors condition on entropy over accepted edits, but acceptance is decided by the very gain they are trying to predict. The A2 arm freezes the class distribution by rejecting everything the wider permission newly enables. I am not convinced the finding that nominal permission does not matter is anything more than a consequence of the design, and the nominal-vs-realized distinction itself was already made by RobustSGPO."
- **mentor**: strongest: The A2 arm is the one new piece: widen nominal permission, then use rejection sampling to hold the realized edit-class distribution at the lower rung's level. It directly tests whether having an option you don't use changes proposer behavior. No neighbour runs it, it fits Yin's Gödel-Agent line of work on whether the improver can improve itself, and it is cheap because it reuses the LadderLoop runs. · weakest: Two problems: the framing is largely scooped, and the causal identification is weak. RobustSGPO (2609.09646) already separates nominal, realized and effective openness, and already shows that category-directed proposals at fixed permission raise coverage and held-out quality. The mediation design is also shaky. H_eff is a post-treatment variable that shares a cause with gain (proposer competence and acceptance by the gate). A1 is a prompt change, so it changes edit quality as well as entropy, which breaks the exclusion assumption. A2's rejection sampling at most adds a reweighting term on top of the lower rung. And the concave g(H_eff), with a rising-then-falling option, can fit almost any outcome, so the 'law' is barely falsifiable. oTYVUBHmbe and 2610.30499 already give null or counter-data on exact tasks. Compute is also tight. 3 rungs x 4 arms x 5 seeds x 2+ tasks is about 120 runs at 2M tokens each, roughly 240M tokens on a local 27B model. That is weeks of GPU time on modest hardware, before adding the frontier optimizer the repositioning needs. · must fix: (1) Make A2 the headline and add a placebo arm in which the higher-rung permission is granted but the proposer is never told about it. This separates 'knowing the option exists' from 'the option exists'. (2) Replace the mediation story with a pre-registered equivalence test on the rung coefficient across the interventional arms only, plus an out-of-sample collapse prediction on held-out cells. Commit to a specific functional form for g, for example a saturating curve fitted on A0 with its parameters frozen, so the law can actually fail. (3) Make A1 entropy-only: keep the prompt text identical and change only the target-file schedule, so the arm doesn't also change edit quality. Report the cost per bit of H_eff. (4) Show that H_eff beats RobustSGPO coverage and HarnessOpt-Bench lever coverage on held-out RMSE, or drop the entropy branding. (5) Cut the grid to 2 rungs x 4 arms x 5 seeds on CO-Bench plus one noisy task, and add a second open optimizer rather than a frontier one to stay within $3k. Realistically, this belongs as the mechanism/causal section of the openness-ladder-crossover paper, not as a standalone submission.
  > ""The nominal/realized distinction and the fixed-permission coverage intervention are already in RobustSGPO. What's left is a mediation analysis on a post-treatment variable and a concave 'law' flexible enough to fit any outcome. The permission-without-use arm is interesting, but it is one ablation, not a paper.""


---

# Thread: What meta-agents actually do when they edit (behavioral analysis → better system)

## What meta-agents actually do when they edit (behavioral analysis → better system)

Scope: empirical work on how and why meta-agents, harness optimizers and LLM proposers in evolutionary search choose their edits, and whether anyone has turned those findings into a better meta-agent.

Method: about 30 Semantic Scholar queries (S2 began returning 429s late in the run), about 35 ICLR 2027 OpenReview `/notes/search` queries, 2 alphaXiv discover calls and 14 alphaXiv full-text reads. For forward citations, the ADAS list was fetched, the Hyperagents fetch failed on the rate limit, and Harness Updating ≠ Benefit was covered through papers that cite it. Every paper cited below had its abstract or full text fetched in this run unless it is marked †. Items marked "(ctx)" are anchors that come from the prior-context files and were not re-fetched.

### Key papers

| id / link | when | summary | what it says about this thread |
|---|---|---|---|
| [Opinionated Optimizers (ICLR27 LhhLpXBFgo)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=LhhLpXBFgo) | ICLR27 sub (Sep–Oct 2026) | Meta-Optimization Testbed (MOT): over 3M rollouts, 216 optimization runs, 4 frontier meta-optimizers, 3 benchmarks. It finds 9 failure modes across 3 stages: (a) proposals come from a narrow set of familiar strategies shaped by the proposer's priors more than by the task; (b) noisy evaluations give weak evidence for revising those priors; (c) selection keeps local wins that rarely compound. Annotated traces are released. | The closest existing "ethology" dataset for harness meta-agents. It names proposer priors as the cause of overfitting. It is diagnostic only, with no prior-correcting system. **Must-cite. Partial scoop of any "behavioral dataset" pitch.** |
| [What Do Evolutionary Coding Agents Evolve? / EvoTrace (ICLR27 a2ZN4PGsoR)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=a2ZN4PGsoR) | ICLR27 sub | 121 runs, 4 frameworks (AlphaEvolve-style), 5 models, 16 math/algorithm tasks, 10,672 programs. A human-validated LLM judge puts parent→child edits into 9 categories. The most frequent edit types are not the ones most associated with beating the parent. The median run reintroduces about 30% of its added lines from code deleted earlier in the same lineage. Fixed-structure tuning recovers many later "LLM" gains. Same-prompt replay recovers much of the original score without reproducing the winning code. | A large edit-level behavioral dataset with replay tools, for program evolution rather than harnesses. It shows that frequency ≠ efficacy and that code gets recycled. |
| [SelfImprove-Bench 2611.02797](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2611.02797) | Nov 2 2026 | Generalization ladder G0–G4 over ACE, AHE and HyperAgents on 6 games, plus semantic trace coding of every proposal: revision vs duplicate, scope, functional target, surface. 87.9% of retained proposals target prompts/memory, 12.1% the execution harness, 0% the improver. Proposals target reasoning (61%) and planning (50%) far more than exploration (11%). When HyperDSH explicitly allows improver self-evolution, Meta edits rise to 20.5–45.2% of retained proposals and Harness-level edits drop to 0%. Duplicate rates reach 67.6% (HyperAgents on Hero). | The cleanest quantitative edit-surface taxonomy. Self-editing is suppressed by framing and affordance, not by impossibility: framing alone moves meta-edits from 0% to up to 45%. Higher revision rate goes with better outcomes, but duplicates persist. |
| [Does Failure Feedback Direct Agent Self-Modification? 2610.14662](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2610.14662) | Oct 10 2026 | A Luria–Delbrück-style randomized test of directedness. In 145 public DGM modifications, the target failure was repaired at 8.3% vs 11.6% for the parent's other failures (e-value 0.15): no target-specific advantage. Three different failures produced the same proposal. Selection over zero-mean edit variation reproduces DGM-like trajectories. In controlled small-model studies, edits are directed (+24.5pp) but break more correct answers than they fix on clean playbooks (−19pp net), mostly by rewriting the response-length/format line. Gains appear only with shared editable causes. 8-step loops ≈ GEPA. | Strong evidence that in DGM, edits are prior-driven "generic improvements" filtered by selection, not responses to the evidence shown. This is the "mutation is not directed" result for meta-agents. |
| [How Do Coding-Agent Harnesses Self-Evolve? 2610.34938](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2610.34938) | Oct 26 2026 | Claude Code `/retro` on 30 SWE-bench Verified issues. 125 proposals were coded: executable checks 38, navigation pointers 24, instructions 21, information access 20, tool/config 12, none 10. 18 of 19 prior-driven proposals were lint checks proposed regardless of what happened, and 28 of 38 executable checks could never fire. Of 7 informative transfers, 2 eliminated the targeted action, 1 reduced it and 4 did nothing. Some changes added work elsewhere: elapsed time 82→245s on one control. | A small but careful taxonomy for a real production agent. Proposer "good-practice" priors such as "add a lint/checklist" are measurable and mostly useless. |
| [Phantom Guardrails 2607.13083](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2607.13083) | Jul 13 2026 | Deterministic lab where the right action is "do nothing". Proposers invent a guard for a nonexistent rule in 15/60 runs vs 0/60 on featureless input. This happens only when three things coincide: a genre-prior pattern, an unstated-completeness gap, and a charter that presupposes failures. Removing any one gives 0/60. In add-only accept loops, the phantom enters at q=0.05 per round and never leaves. | Clean causal evidence that the proposer's prior ("there must be a failure to fix") plus add-only loops ratchet up useless scaffolding. It explains bloat mechanistically. |
| [Masked Erosion 2610.38392](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2610.38392) | Oct 28 2026 | 411 loops, 10 gate rules, plus GEPA. Deleting one of several redundant safeguards is invisible to the gate: greedy gates accept masked deletions at 0.63, and 5 of 6 greedy loops end unprotected. GEPA kept every safety rule verbatim, yet 2 of 3 GEPA finals rewrote a template into "use these credentials", which caused harm under a weaker model. Freezing safety text or a margin-aware (leave-one-out) gate preserves depth. | The flip side of bloat: the deletion edits that do happen are those whose cost is invisible to the score. Gates, not proposers, determine which subtractions survive. |
| [Beneath the Surface / EvoAudit→AGE 2611.07624](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2611.07624) | Nov 5 2026 | AHE, HyperAgents, A-Evolve and GEPA × MiniSWE/Codex harnesses × τ³-Banking and Terminal-Bench 2. All 16 champions both gain and lose covered tasks. Specific changes beat generic reminders (a generic reminder alone ≈ 0 or negative). Useful changes interfere when combined. The best parent ≠ the best future improver. Stricter admission discards alternatives. EvoAudit turns these diagnostics into revisions of select/analyze/mutate/gate (AGE), which improves on its reference in all 4 settings. | **One of the few papers that goes from behavioral audit to a redesigned optimizer.** It is operator-level, not proposer-prior-level. |
| [HarnessBank 2607.13683](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2607.13683) (anchor "Gated Semantic QD") | Jul 2026 (v2 Jul 30) | Separate evolver. A gene bank keyed by (where: prompt/knowledge/runtime/config) × (why: an LLM-inferred failure pathology). Gates check validity, then activation (the edit must fire), then paired 2σ. Reports +5–15% on 7 benchmarks. Explicitly says greedy loops "collapse toward a narrow class of safe modifications, particularly prompt-level edits". Accepted edits span all 4 levers. Cross-model: a pathology→patch "matching law". Without the 2σ gate, phantom progress appears in 62–76% of post-convergence rounds. | A design that uses a behavioral observation (prompt-edit collapse) to impose an edit-surface QD archive. It is the nearest thing to an "edit-type budget". |
| [Principle-Level Local Optima 2610.24291](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2610.24291) | Oct 2026 | 161 runs over molecules, networks and GPU kernels: 78.3% show principle-level collapse while candidate-level diversity stays high. Diversity injection (temperature, multiple LLMs, web search) barely helps (73.8% still collapse). Supplying a better principle helps inconsistently. A generic "you may be stuck" warning (L1) beats a named reference strategy. | Proposer collapse happens at the idea/principle level, so surface diversity metrics hide it. Argues for online collapse detection. |
| [Mutation Without Variation 2606.05408](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2606.05408) | Jun 3 2026 (GECCO'26 companion) | LLM mutation chains with no selection pressure. In 87% of chains, over 93% of mutations revisit a previously seen structural form. Short cycles and self-loops dominate. A classical GP subtree operator does not do this. | Baseline evidence that LLM operators have strong attractors independent of feedback. This is the "proposer prior" measured in isolation. |
| [Scaling Discovery Requires Forgetting 2610.20563](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2610.20563) | Oct 14 2026 | Longer, parallel and communicating autoresearch agents all plateau because each agent becomes anchored to its own past attempts and makes fewer and smaller edits. Periodically discarding all sessions and history and restarting from the best candidate pushes the same budget further. | Shrinking edit size over a run is an observed anchoring signature, and context reset is the fix. |
| [Branch-reset (ICLR27 8UgphlLDok)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=8UgphlLDok) | ICLR27 sub | Independent runs plateau in distinct "idea basins". Forking with a fresh chat context while inheriting the workspace gives +66% over single runs and +44% over best-of-N on 13 multi-day tasks. | Anchoring is in the context, not the artifact. Supports "forgetting" as a structural fix. |
| [RB-Prompt (ICLR27 uVKMqFVBDG)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=uVKMqFVBDG) | ICLR27 sub | Names "prompt saturation": reflective optimizers keep adding instructions after gains stop. It detects saturation from score and instruction growth, then rolls back and asks the reflector to remove or merge rules, with a length penalty. Best or joint-best in 9/9 settings at 40–45% fewer tokens than TextBO-GEPA. | The add-instruction bias is measured, and a remedy is built from it (a subtractive operator plus a length prior). Prompt-only. |
| [What Should the Reflector See? (ICLR27 7e7pAnM0JR)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=7e7pAnM0JR) | ICLR27 sub | 9 reflection-evidence strategies × 5 datasets (Qwen3.5-9B). No strategy dominates. Failure-only evidence gives the biggest average gain (+33.7 on LiveBench-Math). Empirical selection beats an LLM judge. | Reflector input composition matters, but task-dependently. |
| [Values Shape What Self-Improving Coding Agents Build (ICLR27 uuvpiQaG6k)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=uuvpiQaG6k) | ICLR27 sub | A short Schwartz-value statement in the persistent instruction file changes what agents build. "Openness" values raise held-out performance; "conservation" values fall below no-value agents. | Direct evidence that a cheap proposer-disposition prior changes edit behavior and outcomes, with a clear direction. |
| [Wright–Gödel Machine (ICLR27 cQkSamYGQi)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=cQkSamYGQi) | ICLR27 sub | DGM-style archives suffer "genetic homogenization": Thompson-sampling parent selection provably concentrates reproduction on one lineage. Fix: EXP3 with a clade floor plus best-arm identification. | Lineage-level collapse comes from the selector, not the proposer. |
| [Improvement by Inheritance (ICLR27 ZMmJDeDTXk)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=ZMmJDeDTXk) | ICLR27 sub | Nested replay on the Hyperagents history. One selected improver self-edit raises child quality 0.48→0.86 but not successor quality, "because the edited improver almost never edits itself". A modifier built to act on evidence about the improvement procedure does yield a clear gain. | Quantifies the self-edit drought. Shows that evidence about the improver, not mere permission, unlocks meta-edits. |
| [Harness-Delta Attribution 2610.22466 / ICLR27 PX4Zpvsaca](https://openreview.net/forum?id=PX4Zpvsaca) | Oct 15 2026 | Splits gains into artifact-driven overfitting, test-time scaling and residual. In 21 settings with significant search-set gains, a large share of the improvement is explained by overfitting or test-time scaling. | Attribution of the whole delta, not per edit type. |
| [Elicitation or Expansion? 2610.27889](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2610.27889) | Oct 20 2026 | Meta-Harness gains are mostly elicitation: pass@1 rises while pass@k parity or reversal shows up at large k (LawBench: +16pp at pass@1 becomes −4pp vs the 120B model at pass@64). No harness candidate beats the larger model on hard subsets. A learned routing heuristic breaks out of distribution. Trajectory dataset released. | What the edits do: they mostly sharpen existing successes. This is a behavioral property of the edit distribution. |
| [Auditing Harness Evolution (ICLR27 yl1XFtnTn4)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=yl1XFtnTn4) | ICLR27 sub | RSI Information Ledger. An evolved lineage shows no detectable advantage over one audit-selected edit at matched budget. One accepted guard never activated in 3,960 checks. | Many accepted edits are inert: "persist without activating". |
| [Auditing the Product, Not the Recursion 2610.27610](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2610.27610) | Oct 20 2026 | Census of 114 RSI studies. Only 22% audit at least 2 reruns. Of 26 improver-gain claims, only 4 use a same-start, matched-budget swap test. | Meta-evidence that behavioral and process claims are rarely tested properly. |
| [HMED 2610.07979](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2610.07979) | Oct 6 2026 | Hindsight meta-experience distillation. Branch outcomes entangle the starting state with the effect of a Meta-Skill revision, which pushes updates toward lucky states. HMED reconstructs what each revision changed. | Credit assignment for meta-level edits. Complements nested replay. |
| [Meta-Forgetting / IFR (ICLR27 eIJH6HV9LZ)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=eIJH6HV9LZ); [SelfLineage (nVl2qz74V7)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=nVl2qz74V7); [Causal Improvement Graph (GhghP7h0cq)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=GhghP7h0cq); [EvoSpace (MQoRwlss2y)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=MQoRwlss2y); [Predict Before You Modify (ZBxV5bEYUH)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=ZBxV5bEYUH) | ICLR27 subs | Respectively: replaying frozen past states to keep improvement ability; descendant-aware edit lineage records; externalizing evidence→hypothesis→intervention→outcome in a graph instead of the proposer's context; progressively expanding the permitted edit space; an editable model that predicts edit outcomes before evaluation (HyperAgents, up to +46%). | System-side responses to proposer limits.
