### What the evidence says

- **Edits concentrate on the cheapest, most familiar surface (strong, replicated).** Prompts and memory make up 87.9% of retained proposals in SelfImprove-Bench. HarnessBank reports greedy collapse to prompt-level edits. MOT says proposals come from "a narrow set of familiar strategies". Treude's study of Claude Code `/retro`: lint and checklist proposals appear in 29 of 30 sessions, and 18 of 19 prior-driven proposals are lint. Direction: toward additive, instruction-type edits.
- **Edits are mostly not directed by the evidence shown (moderate-strong, but from one system).** The DGM log reanalysis finds a null on directedness, with the same proposal produced from different failures. Mutation Without Variation finds attractors even without selection. Phantom Guardrails shows the prior ("there must be something to fix") producing edits for failures that do not exist. Small controlled models do show directedness when the answer is visible, but net effect is often negative.
- **Additive bias produces bloat and inert edits (strong).** RB-Prompt documents prompt saturation. Phantom Guardrails shows the add-only ratchet. yl1XFtnTn4 has a guard that never fired in 3,960 checks. HarnessBank's activation gate exists for this reason. Treude: 28 of 38 checks could never fire. Removal happens mainly when its cost is invisible (Masked Erosion), and lineages re-add deleted code (EvoTrace's ~30% recycled lines).
- **Edit frequency does not track edit efficacy (moderate).** EvoTrace: the most common categories are not the winning ones. Beneath the Surface: specific beats generic, and generic reminders ≈ 0. Failure Feedback: format and length rewrites break the most answers. HarnessBank: the pathology-matched runtime mechanism (thinking-runaway recovery) carries the gains, and GEPA's prompt-only search cannot reach it.
- **Edit size shrinks and ideas anchor as history grows (moderate, autoresearch-side).** Scaling Discovery Requires Forgetting ("fewer and smaller edits"), Branch-reset (idea basins) and Principle-level collapse (78%). Context reset helps; diversity injection does not.
- **Improvers rarely self-edit, and this depends on affordance and evidence, not on capability (moderate-strong).** SelfImprove-Bench: 0% meta edits under default HyperAgents, but 20–45% when the framing explicitly targets improver evolution. Improvement by Inheritance: the edited improver "almost never edits itself", yet a modifier fed evidence about the procedure gains clearly. Metaⁿ (ctx): depth stalls.
- **Selection, not proposal, decides much of what survives (moderate).** Wright–Gödel: lineage concentration. Masked Erosion: gate statistics decide which deletions pass. Beneath the Surface: stricter admission loses alternatives. HarnessBank ablation: without a significance gate, false elites appear. A behavioral analysis has to separate proposal distributions from retention distributions. SelfImprove-Bench and EvoTrace partly do this; most papers do not.
- **Gains are mostly elicitation plus redistribution (strong).** Elicitation-or-Expansion shows pass@k reversals. Beneath the Surface shows all 16 champions both gaining and losing tasks. Harness-Delta finds a large overfit/test-time-scaling share.
- **Behavior-to-design loops exist but are narrow (moderate).** RB-Prompt adds a length prior and a delete operator. HarnessBank uses where×why QD cells plus an activation gate. EvoAudit/AGE revises operators. Values Shape uses a disposition prior ("openness"). EvoSpace expands edit space gradually. Freezing safety text and margin-aware gates come from Masked Erosion. None calibrates the proposer's prior against a measured prior-only baseline, and none budgets edit types across surfaces (prompt / tool / control flow / improver) using measured efficacy.

### Debates and contradictions

- **Directed vs. undirected edits.** The DGM reanalysis finds no directedness. Small-model controlled studies find directedness, though it often loses on net. HarnessBank claims its edits fix the dominant pathology (a "matching law"). The likely resolution: directedness exists only when failures share an editable cause and the edit surface can reach it (Eq. 6 of 2610.14662). Nobody has tested this across edit surfaces.
- **More openness helps vs. hurts.** "Openness" values help (Values Shape) and wider edit-surface QD helps (HarnessBank). But diversity injection fails (Principle-level), wider editable scope can overfit (RRSI, ctx), and improver editability does not help by itself (Inheritance, SelfImprove-Bench HyperDSH: the self-evolving variant scored 1.326 vs 1.207 for a fixed improver, a small difference). Openness of the proposal disposition and openness of the search space seem to be different variables that papers conflate.
- **Revision helps vs. churn.** SelfImprove-Bench says higher revision rates go with better outcomes. EvoTrace finds code recycling mostly repairs weak parents rather than advancing the best. Masked Erosion shows revision can silently delete safety depth.
- **Restriction is safer vs. not.** In 2610.14662, a one-module edit scope was not generally safer, and oracle-localized edits gave the biggest gains. Masked Erosion favors freezing some text.
- **Context reset vs. lineage memory.** Forgetting and Branch-reset say discard history. SelfLineage, CIG, IFR, HMED and Predict-Before-You-Modify say give the proposer more structured history. This is unresolved, and likely depends on whether history is raw context (anchoring) or externalized and structured (credit).
- **Taxonomy instability.** SelfImprove-Bench (surface × scope × target), Treude (6 mechanisms), EvoTrace (9 categories), MOT (9 failure modes) and HarnessBank (4 levers × pathology) use incompatible schemes. Cross-paper comparison is not yet possible.

### Open gaps

1. **A prior-subtracted behavioral measurement across systems.** Nobody runs the proposer with null, shuffled or wrong evidence (as in 2610.14662 and Melcer et al.) across harness systems such as GEPA, AHE, Meta-Harness, HyperAgents and Gödel Agent to estimate, per edit type, how much is prior and how much is evidence. MOT asserts "opinionated" priors but, from its abstract, does not decompose them this way. Why now: MOT, EvoTrace and SelfImprove-Bench traces are being released, and the directedness test only needs logs with randomized targets plus some re-runs. Cheap: about $300–800 of API.
2. **Edit-type efficacy vs. propensity, measured causally on held-out tasks.** EvoTrace does this for program evolution. For harnesses, SelfImprove-Bench reports only propensities, and Harness-Delta only whole-delta attribution. Gap: a knockout/replay ledger per edit type (prompt add, prompt delete, tool, control flow, memory, gate/selection, improver) with held-out effect and activation rate. Why now: the activation-gate tooling (HarnessBank), LRSI-style toggles (ctx) and RSI Ledger exist but have not been combined into an edit-type "price list".
3. **A proposer calibrated by that price list.** No system sets proposal mixtures, edit-type quotas or priors from measured propensity/efficacy gaps. Example: a rule like "allocate proposal budget ∝ efficacy/propensity", with forced subtractive and meta quotas. RB-Prompt (length only, prompt only) and HarnessBank (QD over where-cells, not budgeted by efficacy) are the nearest. Why now: both anchors are fresh (Jul–Oct 2026), and the add-only and prompt-collapse biases are well documented.
4. **A causal account of the self-edit drought.** Two observations exist: framing raises meta-edits to 20–45% (SelfImprove-Bench), and evidence about the procedure unlocks useful self-edits (Inheritance). Nobody has a factorial over (permission × salience in the prompt × improver-level evidence in traces × credit horizon) that predicts meta-edit rate and successor quality. Why now: nested replay and same-start swap tests (Inheritance, MetaRSI, Auditing-the-Product) give the right outcome metric. Only 4 of 26 papers use it.
5. **A structural forcing benchmark for continual meta-learning.** No benchmark makes the improver's own deficiency the binding constraint. For example: a drifting task stream where the fixed improver's dominant edit type stops paying (prompt-addressable failures phase out and runtime-only pathologies phase in, as in HarnessBank's thinking-runaway), so only meta-edits keep progress going. Grounding could be real repo or tool churn (SWE-style with library version drift). This also links to the user's "law of openness": sweep edit-surface openness against efficacy under drift. Why now: AgentStream, EvoPathBench and Continual-Learning Bench exist but test the task agent, not the improver.
6. **Principle-level collapse detection for harness search.** Principle-level collapse (2610.24291) is measured for molecules and kernels. Nobody tracks principle/strategy entropy of harness edits online, or tests whether a branch-reset-style intervention triggered on that signal beats fixed schedules. Why now: Forgetting and Branch-reset show resets work, and the trigger is the open part.
7. **Subtractive behavior.** No study of when and why proposers delete, and whether a deletion-proposing operator plus a margin-aware gate (Masked Erosion) beats add-only loops on held-out tasks and cost. Phantom Guardrails and RB-Prompt each cover one slice.
8. **A unified edit taxonomy and shared schema.** No common schema exists across MOT, EvoTrace, SelfImprove-Bench and Treude. Without one, a "meta-agent ethology" cannot pool evidence. A low-cost, high-citation contribution, but weak as a NeurIPS main-track paper by itself.

### Where this is heading (6-month forecast, to about May 2027)

- ICLR27 decisions in January will likely accept several of MOT, EvoTrace, Beneath the Surface, Masked Erosion and Phantom Guardrails-type audits. "Behavioral audit of meta-optimizers" becomes an accepted genre, and reviewers will then demand that it lead to a mechanism. Pure taxonomy or dataset papers will face scoop risk from MOT and EvoTrace.
- Expect a wave of "debiased proposer" methods: prior subtraction, null-evidence baselines, length/additivity penalties, delete operators, edit-surface QD (HarnessBank successors), and value/disposition prompts.
- Activation gates, paired significance and same-start swap tests are likely to become standard reporting (Auditing-the-Product's reporting standard, HarnessBank, RSI Ledger).
- Expect more papers on context reset vs. structured lineage memory (Forgetting / Branch-reset vs. CIG / SelfLineage / IFR). A synthesis ("forget raw context, keep structured credit") is a likely winner.
- Self-edit work will move from "allow the improver to edit itself" to "feed the improver evidence about itself" (Predict Before You Modify, HMED, Inheritance's evidence-fed modifier). That leaves room for a clean factorial on the self-edit drought (gap 4) plus a drift benchmark that forces it (gap 5).
- Best-positioned project for Dan (≤$3k API): gaps 1+3. Measure prior-vs-evidence per edit type with null-evidence controls on 2–3 open harness optimizers. Then build an efficacy-calibrated proposal mixer with forced subtract and meta quotas, evaluated with same-start, matched-budget and pass@k tests. Gap 5 is the stretch benchmark component. Expected scoop risk: medium, because MOT is closest. The differentiator is the causal prior subtraction plus the designed system, not the dataset.

Notes: S2 rate-limited (429) late in the run, so forward-citation expansion was done fully only for ADAS. Hyperagents citations could not be fetched. † = abstract not fetched in this run. "(ctx)" = from prior-context files, not re-verified.


## Ideas

### Why Improvers Don't Improve Themselves: A Factorial on the Self-Edit Drought and a Drift Benchmark That Makes Self-Editing Necessary (`behav-meta-necessity`) — panel 67.5/100

**Claim.** Neither permission nor prompt framing produces self-edits that help. The meta-edit rate and successor quality are driven by improver-level evidence: a counterfactual ledger showing which of the improver's own past edit types paid off on held-out. Under a drift stream where the improver's dominant edit type stops paying, improvers fed this evidence lose at most half as much held-out score after each shift as fixed or permission-only improvers, at matched compute.

**If the opposite holds.** If permission or salience alone recovers the benefit, then the drought is a framing artifact (consistent with SelfImprove-Bench's 0→45%). The practical fix becomes 'just prompt it', which is cheap, decisive and publishable. If no condition helps under drift, then self-editing is not the bottleneck for continual adaptation, which argues against the RSI agenda with clean evidence.

**Law.** n/a. Secondary hypothesis: meta-edit rate ∝ salience × P(improver-level evidence available), and successor quality ∝ evidence × horizon, with a significant interaction and no main effect of permission.

**Design.** MetaLite at L4: the improver's prompt and its selection/analysis code are editable. 2×2×2×2 factorial: permission (improver editable or not) × salience (improver self-edit named explicitly in the charter or not) × improver-level evidence (an auto-generated ledger of past edit types with same-start held-out swap deltas, computed by the harness) × credit horizon (whether a self-edit is credited by its children's one-step gain or by k=3-step successor gain via nested replay, as in Inheritance). 16 cells × 5 lineages. Stream: VersionShift-Drift, 4 phases. Phase 1 failures are prompt-addressable (missing instructions). Phase 2 failures need a tool or helper (a real API rename). Phase 3 needs control flow (retries and version detection). Phase 4 needs a gate or selection change (noisy validation, where the improver must adopt paired testing). Each phase uses real library-changelog items in pinned containers, with held-out task sets per phase. Compute is matched by rollouts and by proposer tokens, and self-edits cost the same budget as object edits. Models: local Qwen3-8B as task agent, Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B as improver, with a DeepSeek replication for 2 cells. Primary metrics: meta-edit rate, successor quality (the held-out gain of children produced by the edited improver vs the unedited one from the same start), and post-shift drop and recovery time per phase. Stats: ANOVA or a mixed model over factors, paired bootstrap on drop. Mechanism analysis: code each self-edit (selection, analysis prompt, budget allocation) and test whether the evidence ledger causes the right type of edit for the phase.

**Pilot.** Week 1: 4 cells (permission only; permission+salience; permission+evidence; fixed improver) × 2 lineages, on phases 1→2 only (about 80 tasks). Under $40 API. Go if the evidence cell's meta-edit rate is at least 2× the permission-only cell, and its phase-2 drop is smaller than the fixed improver's by more than seed SD. Kill if all 4 cells show a meta-edit rate under 5% (pivot to measuring the drought as the result) or the phase shift causes no measurable drop (rebuild the drift stream).

**Closest work (author's view).** Improvement by Inheritance (ZMmJDeDTXk): shows the drought and an evidence-fed modifier, but uses replay on fixed Hyperagents history with no factorial and no drift. SelfImprove-Bench HyperDSH (2611.02797): manipulates framing only. Predict Before You Modify (ZBxV5bEYUH) and HMED (2610.07979): give the improver evidence, but not as a factorial against permission and salience. MetaRSI (2609.06396) and RSI-Harness (EOrtreQLbv): optimize the optimizer on stationary tasks. AgentStream and continual-learning benchmarks test the task agent, not the improver. What is new here: a causal decomposition of the drought plus a benchmark grounded in real library drift, in which each phase shift deliberately invalidates the improver's current edit habit, which forces continual meta-learning.

**Scoop check: medium-risk.** I found nothing that runs the causal decomposition (permission × salience × improver-level evidence × credit horizon) on the meta-edit rate. I also found no drift stream built so that each phase invalidates the improver's current edit habit. Both remain new. However, the 'evidence drives successor quality' half is largely pre-empted: Improvement by Inheritance's evidence-fed modifier already gives a successor-quality gain, and SelfLineage already tests descendant-aware evidence. Flex-RSI already forces a 4-round review of which edit types paid off.

To survive:
(1) Lead with the drift benchmark and the 'habit invalidation' result, not with 'evidence helps'. The headline should be that under VersionShift-Drift, improvers' edit-type distribution stays locked on the phase-1 habit (prompt or memory edits) after shifts. Then show which manipulation unlocks it and by how much recovery time shrinks. Pre-register the evidence main effect as a replication of ZMmJDeDTXk. Claim novelty for the interaction, specifically that permission and salience have no effect without evidence, and for drift recovery.
(2) Use the Opinionated Optimizers account (proposer priors plus weak evidence) as the explicit hypothesis being tested causally. That makes the paper the interventional follow-up to an observational ICLR study.
(3) Add strong baselines: Inheritance's evidence modifier, a SelfLineage-style descendant ledger, Flex-RSI's forced periodic self-review, and an RSIFlow-style evidence-annotated skill ledger. The ledger contribution must be shown to beat these, specifically the same-start counterfactual held-out swap deltas against a non-counterfactual 'followed by accepted' review.
(4) Shrink the design. Make it 2×2×2 (permission × salience × evidence) with horizon as a single follow-up contrast in the evidence-on cells, and use ≥8 lineages per cell. Sixteen cells × 5 lineages is underpowered for interactions, and 80 lineages with Qwen3-Coder-30B over 4 phases is heavy for one undergrad.
(5) Ground the phases in existing real breaking-change corpora (Update from Hell 2608.30300, DepRepair 2607.17957, Δτ-Bench) and cite them, so the 'grounded in reality' claim is not contested. Also cite 2611.03219 and 2610.25858 to separate meta-level drift from object-level forgetting.
(6) Make phase 4 (noisy validation, adopt paired testing) the signature cell. It is the shift where only a gate or selection self-edit helps, so it directly tests whether self-editing becomes necessary rather than just permitted.

| Paper | Title | Summary | Overlap |
|---|---|---|---|
| OpenReview ZMmJDeDTXk | Improvement by Inheritance: Measuring Recursive Self-Improvement in Coding Agents (ICLR27 sub) | Uses nested replay on Hyperagents history to separate child quality from successor quality. The edited improver almost never edits itself. A modifier built to act on evidence about the improvement procedure gives a clear gain in successor quality. The gain in child quality is traced to evidence routing. | High on the mechanism claim. It already shows that the drought exists and that improver-level evidence raises successor quality. That pre-empts the expected main effect of the evidence factor. It has no permission × salience factorial, no k-step credit-horizon manipulation, and no drift stream. |
| OpenReview LhhLpXBFgo | Opinionated Optimizers: Why Agents Struggle to Improve Agents (ICLR27 sub) | Introduces the Meta-Optimization Testbed: 216 runs, 4 methods, 3 benchmarks, more than 3M rollouts. It finds 9 failure modes: proposers reuse a narrow set of familiar strategies shaped by their priors, noisy evaluations are weak evidence for revising those priors, and selection does not compound gains. | Medium-high on the 'analysis of how agents behave when they make meta-edits' angle and on the 'evidence is too weak to change habits' explanation. It is observational, with no factorial intervention, no improver self-edit permission, and no drift. It is the natural mechanism prior to cite and test. |
| OpenReview nVl2qz74V7 | SelfLineage: Learning the Long-Term Consequences of Self-Modification (ICLR27 sub) | Gives a descendant-aware lineage history (provenance links to later consequences) to a harness-level RSI improver at a fixed decision state. Controls include factual-access, shuffled-link and temporal-link arms. Benchmarks are on EvoHarnessBench. The central question is whether correct descendant relations improve later self-modification decisions. | Medium on the evidence-ledger × credit-horizon factors. It is close to a 'k-step successor credit' ledger. It does not cross that with permission or salience, and it is not a drift stream where the improver's habits stop paying. |
| [2611.08946](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08946) | Flex-RSI: Benchmarking Recursive Self-Improvement of Frontier Agents | An executor–improver benchmark in which the improver may edit itself, including its acceptance rule. Every 4 rounds the improver is structurally forced to review which kinds of harness changes were followed by accepted candidates and which by stalls. Paired frozen-vs-improver runs show harness editing helps in 6 of 7 pairs. Tasks are stationary. | Medium. It already has a forced periodic self-review over edit types (a non-counterfactual edit-type ledger) and a self-editable gate. It does not measure the meta-edit rate as a function of permission, salience or evidence, and it has no drift. |
| [2610.21791](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21791) | RSIFlow: Adaptive Self-Improvement Workflows with an Evolving Meta-Agent | The meta-agent diagnoses shifting bottlenecks (model, harness or artifacts) and picks a workflow. It keeps component-specific skills that carry supporting and contradicting evidence (for example +31/-5), and evolves its own meta-harness. Meta versions are compared on a common Task-Agent. | Medium-low. It frames 'bottlenecks shift, so the meta-agent must adapt' and uses an evidence-annotated skill ledger by edit type. It is a method paper on stationary benchmarks, with no factorial and no drift benchmark. |
| [2611.03219](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03219) | Can Coding Agents Sustain Continual Evolution without Forgetting? | Runs AHE and Meta-Harness continually across SWE-bench, SWT-Bench and FeatureBench (6 directions), finding severe forgetting (94% to 32%). It codes 520 trajectories into verification, workflow and budget conflicts. | Medium-low on the drift-benchmark half. The task sequence shifts, but the evolver is fixed, so this is object-level forgetting, not meta-level adaptation. It overlaps with the mechanism-coding analysis. |
| [2611.02797](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02797) | SelfImprove-Bench (HyperDSH) | 87.9% of retained edits touch prompts or memory, and 0% touch the improver unless forced. It manipulates framing. | Medium. It documents the drought and the framing (salience) lever only. It has no permission × evidence factorial and no drift. |
| OpenReview ZBxV5bEYUH / 2610.13991 | Predict Before You Modify (IDM) | Adds an editable Improvement Dynamics Model to HyperAgents that forecasts edit outcomes; the forecasts improve and transfer. | Medium-low. It gives the improver evidence about its own edits but is not a factorial against permission and salience, and it has no drift. |
| 2610.25858 / GGUmZybpbd / 2608.14441 / 2610.34238 / 2608.30300 | Are Self-Evolving Agents Continual Learners?; AgentStream; PACE-Bench; Δτ-Bench; Update from Hell | These are drifting or streaming benchmarks for self-evolving agents, covering tool-ecosystem evolution, physics shifts and real dependency-upgrade breakage. All test the task agent or a fixed evolver. | Low-medium. The ingredients for real library-drift phases already exist (Update from Hell and DepRepair supply real breaking-change items). None of these designs a phase sequence that invalidates the improver's dominant edit type. |

**Judge panel**

| Lens | N | S | R | C | F | T | Total | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ac | 3 | 3.5 | 4.5 | 3 | 2.5 | 4 | 67.5 | pursue-with-fixes |
| expert | 3 | 3.5 | 4.5 | 3 | 2.5 | 4 | 67.5 | pursue-with-fixes |
| mentor | 3 | 3.5 | 4.5 | 3 | 2.5 | 4 | 67.5 | pursue-with-fixes |

- **ac**: strongest: The causal decomposition of the self-edit drought (permission x salience x evidence) is something no prior paper runs as an intervention. Prior work is either observational (Opinionated Optimizers) or varies one lever (SelfImprove-Bench, framing only). The drift stream is built so that each phase makes the improver's current edit habit stop paying, so phase 4 (noisy validation, where only a gate or selection edit helps) can show whether self-editing becomes necessary. Every outcome is publishable: interaction only, framing artifact, or no help under drift. · weakest: Three problems. (1) Novelty is half pre-empted: Inheritance (ZMmJDeDTXk) already shows that an evidence-fed improver gets better successors, and SelfLineage and Flex-RSI already cover descendant-aware ledgers and edit-type review, so the predicted big main effect is not new. (2) The ledger carries held-out swap deltas that are computed by the harness and not charged to the improver. That leaks held-out data into the improver's decisions and gives the evidence arm free compute, which breaks both the held-out claim and the matched-compute claim. (3) The phases always run in the order prompt, tool, control, gate, so phase type is confounded with lineage age and accumulated edits. With 16x5 lineages over 4 phases on a 30B improver, interaction tests are underpowered, and the engineering is too much for one undergrad. There is also a floor-effect risk: a 30B-A3B improver may be unable to write useful selection-code edits, in which case the drought is just a capability limit. · must fix: Cut the design to 2x2x2 with at least 8 lineages per cell. Run horizon as a single follow-up contrast in the evidence-on cells. Compute the ledger on a separate ledger-validation split that never touches the phase test sets, and charge the ledger's rollouts to the improver's budget, or give every arm an equal free-eval allowance. Counterbalance or randomize the phase order for at least a subset of lineages, including a gate-first order, so habit invalidation is not confounded with time. Add baselines: the Inheritance modifier, a non-counterfactual 'followed-by-accepted' review in the Flex-RSI and RSIFlow style, and a SelfLineage-style descendant ledger. Build the phases from Update from Hell, DepRepair and Δτ-Bench items. Run a capability check in the pilot: an oracle-hinted gate edit should help in phase 4, so that a null means drought rather than inability. Pre-register the evidence main effect as a replication, and claim novelty only for the interaction and for drift recovery.
  > "The factorial question is well posed and timely. As designed, though, the evidence arm sees held-out-derived signal for free, and phase type is confounded with lineage age. Fix the ledger split, charge its compute, and counterbalance the phases, and this becomes the interventional follow-up the observational meta-agent literature needs. Without those fixes it reads as a heavier replication of Inheritance."
- **expert**: strongest: The factorial is interventional. It splits the self-edit drought into permission, salience and evidence, with a stated null on permission and a quantitative threshold (at most half the post-shift drop). Its findings hold up either way. A "just prompt it" result is a cheap and useful negative. A "nothing helps under drift" result is real evidence against the RSI agenda. Phase 4 (noisy validation, where only a gate or selection self-edit helps) is a sharp test of whether self-editing becomes necessary rather than merely permitted. It is the interventional follow-up that the observational Opinionated Optimizers paper and the replay-only Inheritance paper leave open. · weakest: Three problems. (a) Circularity: the authors build each phase so that the improver's current habit stops paying, so "self-editing becomes necessary" is partly true by design. Reviewers will call VersionShift-Drift a constructed adversary, not grounded drift. (b) Confound: the counterfactual ledger is computed by the harness through extra same-start held-out swaps. Any gain may come from the harness doing meta-credit-assignment, plus the extra held-out rollouts it spends, rather than from the improver learning to self-edit. (c) Novelty and power: the evidence main effect is largely pre-empted by Inheritance (ZMmJDeDTXk) and SelfLineage. The remaining claims are interaction effects, and 16 cells × 5 lineages cannot detect them. Running 80 or more Qwen3-Coder-30B lineages over 4 phases is heavy GPU work for a solo undergrad. The "$40 API" figure leaves out GPU cost. A DeepSeek replication on only 2 cells is thin multi-model evidence. · must fix: 1. Charge every swap-delta rollout the ledger uses to the evidence cells' budget. Add a compute-matched control that gets the same extra held-out rollouts as a raw, non-counterfactual log. This separates "counterfactual structure" from "more held-out signal".
2. Shrink the design to 2×2×2 (permission × salience × evidence) with at least 8 lineages per cell. Run the credit horizon as a single contrast inside the evidence-on cells. Do a power analysis for the permission × evidence interaction before running.
3. Add a non-constructed drift condition to answer the by-design critique. Use a held-out real changelog sequence from Update from Hell, DepRepair or Δτ-Bench, label which edit type each item requires after the fact, and randomize phase order. Show the habit-lock effect there as well.
4. Include baselines: Inheritance's evidence modifier, Flex-RSI's forced periodic self-review (non-counterfactual), and a SelfLineage descendant ledger.
5. Lead with habit lock-in under drift (edit-type distribution after each shift, and recovery time). Pre-register the evidence main effect as a replication.
6. Budget local GPU hours explicitly. Extend the second model family beyond 2 cells, at least for the evidence-off vs evidence-on contrast at phase 4.
  > "The factorial is a welcome causal step beyond the observational and replay-based drought papers. But the drift stream is engineered so that the improver's habit fails, and the evidence ledger is itself a harness-side meta-learner paid for with extra held-out rollouts. I am not convinced the improvement comes from the improver learning to improve itself, rather than from the authors' benchmark design and the harness's counterfactual bookkeeping. With 5 lineages per cell, the headline interaction claims are underpowered."
- **mentor**: strongest: The drift stream is designed so that each phase shift makes the improver's dominant edit habit stop paying, with phase 4 (noisy validation, where only a gate or selection self-edit helps) as the signature case. That turns "self-editing is necessary" into something you can test, rather than leaving it at "self-editing is permitted". It speaks directly to the Gödel Agent line of work and to the user's question about why meta-harnesses rarely update themselves. Each outcome is informative: if framing alone works, the fix is cheap; if nothing works under drift, that is clean evidence against the RSI agenda. · weakest: The evidence main effect is already shown (Improvement by Inheritance, SelfLineage, Flex-RSI). The novelty that remains is the permission × salience × evidence interaction and the drift benchmark. The benchmark is hand-built so that specific edit types become necessary, so reviewers will call it tailored to produce the result. There is also a likely floor effect. Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B editing its own selection and analysis code may produce almost no competent self-edits in any cell, and the pilot's kill rule (meta-edit rate under 5% everywhere) is a real risk. Sixteen cells × 5 lineages × 4 phases is underpowered for interactions, and it is too heavy for one undergrad running local models on a modest GPU. · must fix: Five fixes. (1) Validate the benchmark with oracles before running the factorial. Show that a hand-written oracle edit of the right type (prompt, tool, control flow, gate) recovers each phase, and that edits of the wrong type do not. Without this, the "habit invalidation" claim is unsupported. (2) Shrink the design to 2×2×2 (permission × salience × evidence) with at least 8 lineages per cell. Run credit horizon only as a follow-up contrast in the evidence-on cells. Pre-register the evidence main effect as a replication of ZMmJDeDTXk, and claim novelty only for the interaction and for drift recovery. (3) Add baselines from the closest work: Inheritance's evidence-fed modifier, Flex-RSI's forced periodic self-review (a non-counterfactual ledger), and a SelfLineage-style descendant ledger. Show that the same-start counterfactual swap deltas beat these. (4) Frame the paper as the causal test of the Opinionated Optimizers account (proposer priors plus weak evidence). Build the phases from Update from Hell, DepRepair and Δτ-Bench items. (5) Replicate on a second open improver model family across the full 2×2×2, not in just 2 cells. Report edit-type distributions over time as the headline figure.
  > ""The finding that improver-level evidence raises successor quality is already known (Inheritance, SelfLineage). The new part is a drift benchmark the authors built themselves, where each phase is designed to require the edit type their ledger is meant to unlock. Without oracle validation of the phases and comparison to Flex-RSI-style self-review, I cannot tell whether the ledger matters or whether the benchmark was simply built to need it.""

### A Reach–Discoverability Law for Meta-Harness Openness: Optimal Search-Space Openness Grows with Evaluation Budget and Task Drift (`behav-openness-law`) — panel 65.6/100

**Claim.** The held-out gain from harness search is inverted-U in edit-surface openness L0–L4. The openness that maximizes gain, o*, increases with the log of the evaluation budget and with the task drift rate. On a stationary stream at budgets of 500 rollouts or fewer, prompt+memory (L1) beats improver-editable (L4). Under drift at budgets of 4k rollouts or more, L3/L4 beats L1 by at least 5 held-out points.

**If the opposite holds.** If gain rises monotonically with openness at every budget, then constraint is pure loss, which contradicts the RRSI, SelfImprove-Bench and Inheritance findings, and the result would be a strong 'just open it up' paper. If gain falls monotonically, then openness is a trap, and the paper becomes a quantitative argument for constrained harness search. Both outcomes come with a fitted functional form and a released sweep.

**Law.** G(o,B) = R(o) · [1 − exp(−B·q / S(o))] − λ·S(o)/n_val. R(o) is the reach: the fraction of failure mass whose cause is editable at openness o. It is measured by an oracle-localized fix probe. S(o) is the effective search-space size, measured by proposal entropy. q is the proposer hit rate. The last term is the overfitting penalty from validation size n_val. Implied optimum: o* is where R'(o)/R(o) = S'(o)/S(o)·(B q / S(o))·..., so o* rises with log B. Drift raises R for high o, which moves o* to the right.

**Design.** MetaLite with 5 nested openness levels. Openness is measured two ways: the nominal level, and the empirical log-size of the reachable edit space, estimated by the proposal entropy of the unfiltered proposer over 200 draws. A third dimension, 'disposition openness' (the Values-Shape 'openness' statement on or off), is added to separate the two variables that papers conflate. Grid: 5 levels × 4 budgets (250, 500, 1k, 4k rollouts) × 2 streams × 5 seeds. Stationary stream: LiveBench-Math plus τ-bench-lite. Drift stream: VersionShift, built from real library churn, i.e. tasks requiring pandas 1.x→2.x, numpy 1→2, pydantic 1→2 and sklearn API changes, sourced from CodeUpdateArena/VersiCode-style changelog items and executed in pinned containers so that the failures that matter move from prompt-addressable to tool/runtime-addressable. That is about 200 runs of a small local task agent (Qwen3-8B) with a local proposer (Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B). API is used only for an annotator. Controls: the same proposer and selection gate (a paired 2σ activation gate taken from HarnessBank, held fixed) at every level, and a random-edit arm at each level to separate reach from proposer skill. Metrics: held-out accuracy after a fixed budget, gain per rollout, generalization gap (search minus held-out) and pass@k. Fit the law with hierarchical nonlinear regression and test predictions on a held-out budget (8k) and a held-out task (LiveCodeBench-lite) before looking at them.

**Pilot.** Week 1: L0, L1, L3 and L4 only, 2 budgets (250, 1k), the VersionShift-mini drift stream with 60 tasks, 2 seeds. About 32 runs on local GPU and under $30 API. Go if the ranking of levels flips between the two budgets, or between stationary and drift, by more than seed SD for at least one pair. Kill if L1 dominates at every budget and stream with no crossover, which would mean the proposer cannot use higher surfaces at all. In that case, fold the work into idea 1 as evidence about 'reach'.

**Closest work (author's view).** EvoSpace (MQoRwlss2y): progressively expands edit space, but fits no law and has no budget×drift sweep. SelfImprove-Bench (2611.02797): a generalization ladder, not an openness sweep. HarnessBank (2607.13683): QD over levers, single budget. Harness-MAP (HM48DQvAqS): structure-token factorization for search efficiency, not openness vs efficacy. RRSI/Metaⁿ (ctx): report that wider scope or more depth stalls or overfits, with no functional form. What is new here: a falsifiable two-factor law, with reach and discoverability measured separately, plus a drift benchmark grounded in real library churn that predicts when openness pays.

**Scoop check: medium-risk.** I found no paper that fits a functional law for openness × budget × drift, or that runs the 5-level × 4-budget × 2-stream grid. The headline is therefore still open. The pieces it rests on are taken, though:
- RobustSGPO already sweeps nested permission levels, separates the nominal edit space from the realized and effective ones, and shows the ranking of levels flips over rounds, with one task shift.
- 2611.09701 formalizes the reach-vs-discoverability idea as restriction gap plus selection and discovery regret.
- EvoSpace states the tradeoff in words.
- The budget-splitting-trap paper shows wider surfaces losing at small budgets.

How to reposition:
(1) Lead with the empirical crossover and its out-of-sample prediction: o* shifts with log B and with drift, pre-registered and checked on the 8k budget and on LiveCodeBench-lite. Do not lead with the two-factor decomposition. Cite 2611.09701 as the theory you instantiate for harness openness, and derive G(o,B) from its regret bound rather than presenting the form as new.
(2) Treat RobustSGPO as the single-workflow, no-law precedent. Adopt its nominal/realized/effective split and add the random-edit arm and the oracle-localized reach probe R(o), which it lacks.
(3) Rebrand EvoSpace and RobustSGPO's 1->2->3 schedule as baselines. Show the law predicts when a schedule beats a fixed level.
(4) Fix the L4 problem before running the grid (SelfImprove-Bench / Inheritance). Either force a share of improver edits or redefine L4 as the improver's prompt and selection-policy surface, and pilot whether L4 ever differs from L3.
(5) Position VersionShift against Δτ-Bench, PACE-Bench and EvoHarnessBench. Its distinct property is that drift moves the failure cause from prompt-addressable to runtime-addressable, which can be checked with the R(o) probe. State that property and verify it in the benchmark section.
(6) Make the disposition-vs-structure separation (Values Shape) a secondary finding that is clearly yours. Expect neighbours in this space by ICLR/NeurIPS 2027 and submit early.

| arXiv:2609.09646 | RobustSGPO: Search-Space Control for Agent Harness Evolution (Kuaishou, Sep 2026) | Three nested permission levels: a1 edits one agent, a2 edits any agent, a3 can also add or remove agents and change routing. It defines a nominal, a realized and an effective (valid) edit space, and argues that broader permission does not yield more valid edits. Fixed a3 leads early, but a periodic 1->2->3 schedule finishes 0.28 points higher (fixed a1/a2/a3 = 3.85/4.07/4.06). It also runs one task-shift experiment and plots score against token budget. | High on framing and design. It sweeps openness levels with a fixed proposer and gate, separates the permitted space from the space actually reached (our reach vs discoverability), shows the ranking of levels changes over budget (rounds), and includes a task shift. Missing: no functional law, no grid over budget × drift, no random-edit arm, no improver-editable level, one industrial workflow. |
| arXiv:2611.09701 | Where Should Learning Live? Regret Analysis for Text, Parameter, and Joint Adaptation (Nov 2026) | Splits deployed excess loss into selection regret within a family, family-restriction gap, signed state effect, plus localization and discovery terms. Shows that text-only selection has lower within-family regret than joint selection but more failures, because its family excludes stronger configurations (Spider, Qwen3.5-9B). | Medium-high on theory. Its restriction-vs-selection tradeoff (regret about sqrt(T·log|C|) against restriction gap) is a formal version of our R(o) vs S(o) reach/discoverability law, including the dependence on budget T. A reviewer will say the two-factor decomposition is known. It covers text vs parameters, not harness openness levels, and has no drift × openness experiment. |
| [MQoRwlss2y](https://openreview.net/forum?id=MQoRwlss2y) | EvoSpace: Co-Evolving the Harness and Edit Space (ICLR 2027 sub) | States the dilemma directly: a fixed edit space can rule out needed updates, while opening every edit at once makes selection hard. Fix: grow permissible components, scopes and operations progressively, guided by diagnosis. Tested on LoCoMo memory QA. | Medium. Same qualitative claim (an openness tradeoff, with expansion paying later), offered as a method. No law, no budget or drift sweep, no separate measurement of reach and search-space size. |
| arXiv:2609.02889 | Where Does Harness-Optimization Value Live? Localized Gains and the Budget-Splitting Trap (Jun 2026) | Splits the harness into 4 slots. All gain sits in the control slot, and spreading a 64-rollout budget across slots leaves each one below the optimizer's accept floor, so the full method ties the baseline. Concentrating the budget recovers +0.119. | Medium. Gives direct evidence that widening the editable surface at a fixed small budget hurts (our low-budget prediction, L1 > L4), and explains it by the accept floor. No openness levels beyond slots, no law, no drift. |
| arXiv:2609.08175 | A Theory of Reliable Self-Evolution for Agent Harnesses (Sep 2026) | Ceiling set by validation and evaluation cost. Defines and estimates 'reachability' (the probability of generating a qualified modification) and uses it to tell generation limits from validation limits. | Low-medium. Uses the reachability and evaluation-budget concepts that our q and λ·S/n_val terms rely on. Does not vary openness. |
| arXiv:2611.02797 / ZMmJDeDTXk | SelfImprove-Bench; Improvement by Inheritance | 87.9% of retained edits touch prompts or memory, and the improver is edited 0% of the time unless forced. Improvers almost never edit themselves. | Threat to the design, not a scoop. The L4 (improver-editable) arm may be empty in practice, so 'openness' at L4 may equal L3 unless edits are forced. |
| [uuvpiQaG6k](https://openreview.net/forum?id=uuvpiQaG6k) | Values Shape What Self-Improving Coding Agents Build (ICLR 2027 sub) | An 'openness to change' value statement raises held-out performance, and a conservation statement lowers it. | Low. The source of the 'disposition openness' axis. The idea already plans to separate it from structural openness, which is a real contribution. |
| 2610.34238 / 2609.04280 / 2608.14441 / 2607.14642 / 2610.25858 | Δτ-Bench, EvoHarnessBench, PACE-Bench, MCPEvol-Bench, Are Self-Evolving Agents Continual Learners? | Drift benchmarks: evolving tool ecosystems and MCP servers, physics changes that require code evolution, and drifting streams for GEPA/Meta-Harness (plasticity–stability r = −0.72). | Medium for the VersionShift benchmark claim. Drift streams for self-evolving agents exist, including ones that need code or tool edits. None crosses drift with edit-surface openness. Library-churn sources (CodeUpdateArena, VersiCode, GitChameleon) exist, but not as a harness-evolution stream. |

**Judge panel**

| Lens | N | S | R | C | F | T | Total | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ac | 2.5 | 3.5 | 4.5 | 3 | 2.5 | 4 | 65 | pursue-with-fixes |
