| mentor | 3 | 3.5 | 4.5 | 3 | 2.5 | 4 | 67.5 | pursue-with-fixes |

- **ac**: strongest: The question sits right at the center of the mentor's agenda, and the design is pre-registered and falsifiable. It has a crossover prediction (L1 beats L4 at small B on a stationary stream; L3/L4 beats L1 under drift at large B), a random-edit arm that separates reach from proposer skill, and an oracle-localized R(o) probe. Out-of-sample checks on an 8k budget and a held-out task let the law fail in public. Whichever way it comes out, the result is a quantitative claim about how open harness search should be, and VersionShift is a reusable artifact grounded in real library churn. · weakest: The novelty margin is thin, and the 'law' is underdetermined. RobustSGPO already sweeps nested openness with a fixed proposer and gate, separates nominal from realized space, and shows rankings flipping over budget under shift. 2611.09701 already gives the restriction-vs-selection regret form. What is left is fitting a 4-parameter nonlinear form to 4 budgets × 5 ordinal levels. Openness is an ordinal design choice, not a continuous variable, so 'o*' and R'(o)/R(o) are not well defined. Proposal entropy over 200 draws is a weak proxy for S(o) and will be dominated by surface paraphrase. There is one task model and one proposer, so a reviewer will say the law is a fact about Qwen3-Coder-30B, not about harness search. L4 may collapse into L3 (SelfImprove-Bench / Inheritance). On compute: about 50 cells × ~5.75k rollouts, roughly 290k rollouts including multi-turn τ-bench, plus building containerized VersionShift with verified tasks. That is heavy for a solo undergrad in 6 months. · must fix: (1) Make openness continuous and measured, not nominal. Use the R(o) probe and a semantically deduplicated S(o) (cluster proposals by diff effect, not entropy over raw tokens), and fit G against the measured (R, S) instead of level index, so the law can be tested with continuous predictors. (2) Add at least one more proposer, ideally of a different family or size (e.g. a 7B coder vs the 30B-A3B), and check that o* moves with proposer hit rate q as the law predicts. That turns the single-model weakness into a test of the law's q term. (3) Settle L4 in the pilot: force a share of improver edits, or redefine L4 and show it differs from L3. Otherwise drop it and claim 4 levels. (4) Derive G from the 2611.09701 regret bound and lead with the pre-registered crossover and out-of-sample checks, citing RobustSGPO's split as precedent. (5) Cut scope to fit compute: drop τ-bench-lite (multi-turn cost) for a cheaper stationary stream, use 3 budgets plus the held-out 8k, and add more seeds only where the pilot shows an effect near seed SD. Build VersionShift around about 150 verified tasks, with checked evidence that drift moves failure mass from prompt-addressable to runtime-addressable.
  > "The crossover between budget and drift is a clean, pre-registered prediction, and the random-edit control is a nice touch. But the 'law' is fit on five ordinal levels with one proposer, its functional form follows directly from known restriction/selection regret bounds, and RobustSGPO already reports level-ranking reversals under shift. I need to see the measured (R, S) coordinates, not level indices, predict o* for a second proposer family before I believe this is a law and not a curve fit."
- **expert**: strongest: It asks a question the field keeps stating loosely ('more openness stalls or overfits') and turns it into a pre-registered, out-of-sample-testable prediction: o* moves right with log B and with drift, checked at a held-out 8k budget and on a held-out task. It also separates reach from discoverability with operational probes (an oracle-localized fix probe R(o), a random-edit arm, proposal entropy). The VersionShift idea, where library churn moves failures from prompt-addressable to runtime-addressable, gives drift a real-world mechanism instead of a synthetic perturbation. Any crossover result lands squarely in the self-referential and meta-agent agenda. · weakest: The 'law' is underdetermined and only half new. Five ordinal openness levels × 4 budgets cannot identify a 4+-parameter nonlinear form (R, S, q, λ) with confidence. The optimum derivation ends in '...'. 2611.09701 already gives the restriction-vs-selection regret tradeoff with T dependence, and RobustSGPO already sweeps nested permission levels with a fixed proposer and gate, separates the nominal from the realized space, and shows the ranking flips over rounds. What remains new is mostly the grid plus a drift axis, so a reviewer can call it 'RobustSGPO × Where-Should-Learning-Live on a new benchmark.' Proposal entropy over 200 draws is a weak, model-dependent estimator of log reachable-space size. L4 is likely empty in practice (SelfImprove-Bench / Inheritance), which collapses one of five points. There is only one task model and one proposer, so the 'law' could be specific to Qwen3-Coder-30B's skill. Compute is understated: about 200 runs averaging around 1.5k agentic rollouts, plus proposer calls, plus building and containerizing VersionShift, is a lot for a solo undergrad in 6 months without a dedicated multi-GPU node. τ-bench-lite also needs a user simulator, which is another hidden cost. · must fix: (1) Derive G(o,B) explicitly from the 2611.09701 regret bound, with a closed-form o*(B, drift). Pre-register the sign and rough magnitude of d o*/d log B. Drop the claim that the two-factor form is new. (2) Replace the 5 ordinal levels with a continuous or semi-continuous openness knob, e.g. the fraction of harness files or slots that are editable, sampled at 8 or more points, so the curve is identifiable. Keep L0–L4 as named anchors. (3) Validate S(o) against a ground-truth small surface where the reachable-space size can be enumerated, before trusting proposal entropy. (4) Fix or drop L4 after a pilot shows whether it ever differs from L3 (forced improver-edit quota). (5) Add a second proposer, at least on the pilot grid, e.g. a different family or a smaller coder, to show that o* shifts with q as the law predicts. That turns multi-model into a test of the law rather than just robustness. (6) Budget the compute: report the total rollout count and GPU-hours. Cut stationary τ-bench-lite if it needs an API user simulator. Shrink VersionShift to about 150 verified tasks, with an R(o) probe showing the prompt-to-runtime shift. (7) Run RobustSGPO-style and EvoSpace-style expanding schedules as baselines, and show the law predicts when a schedule beats a fixed level.
  > ""The empirical crossover is interesting, and the drift benchmark built from real library churn is a nice contribution. But the 'law' is the regret tradeoff of prior work refit to five ordinal points with one proposer, and proposal entropy is not a credible measure of search-space size. I would want a continuous openness axis, a second proposer showing the predicted q-dependence, and a direct comparison to RobustSGPO before calling this a law rather than a sweep." Score: 5 (borderline reject), confidence 4. Overall calibrated score: about 63/100."
- **mentor**: strongest: The question is the right one for this mentor: when does opening the edit surface, up to an improver-editable L4, pay off? It is set up as a pre-registered out-of-sample test (held-out 8k budget, held-out task) rather than as a method. Both directions are reportable with a fitted form. Measuring reach R(o) with an oracle-localized fix probe, plus a random-edit arm, is a real way to pull apart "the surface can't fix it" from "the proposer can't find it", and RobustSGPO and EvoSpace lack both. VersionShift is grounded in real library churn and moves the failure cause from prompt-level to runtime-level, which makes it a believable, checkable drift mechanism, not synthetic noise. · weakest: The headline is partly taken and partly expected. RobustSGPO already sweeps nested levels with a fixed gate, splits nominal from realized space, and shows the level ranking flipping over rounds. 2611.09701 already formalizes restriction gap vs discovery regret as a function of T. An inverted U whose peak moves right with budget is the default prior, so the surprise is low unless the fitted law predicts held-out cells well. With only 5 openness levels and 4 budgets, a 4+ parameter "law" is close to curve-fitting, and a reviewer will call it a descriptive regression, not a law. S(o) as proposal entropy over 200 free-form edits is poorly defined (it needs an embedding or clustering of edits) and could be gamed by choices in that step. L4 may collapse into L3, as SelfImprove-Bench and Inheritance suggest. There is one task model (Qwen3-8B) and one proposer, so the multi-model rigor box is unchecked. Feasibility is the real problem: 5 levels x 4 budgets x 2 streams x 5 seeds, plus random-edit arms, comes to roughly 500k+ multi-turn rollouts. On top of that, VersionShift needs pinned containers for four libraries, and the tau-bench-lite environment has to be built. That is a lot for one undergrad on a modest GPU in 6 months. · must fix: (1) Cut the grid. Drop tau-bench-lite and use 3 seeds at 4k, or replace the 5-level ladder with L0/L1/L3/L4 plus random-edit arms only at the extreme budgets. Report a rollout count and GPU-hours estimate before committing. (2) Before the grid, pilot whether L4 ever produces accepted edits that differ from L3. If it doesn't, either force a share of improver edits or redefine L4, otherwise the high-openness end of the curve is empty. (3) Ground the functional form in the 2611.09701 regret bound and pre-register its parameter count and its predictions for the held-out 8k budget and LiveCodeBench-lite cells, so the "law" can actually be falsified. (4) Make S(o) robust: define proposal entropy over canonicalized edit diffs or AST/slot categories, and check the conclusions hold under at least two estimators. (5) Add a second task-model family (e.g., Llama or Gemma, at least on a subgrid) to meet multi-model rigor. (6) Verify the VersionShift property (failure cause moves from prompt-addressable to runtime-addressable) with the R(o) probe, and position it against Δτ-Bench, PACE-Bench and EvoHarnessBench. (7) Present RobustSGPO and the EvoSpace schedule as baselines the law must predict, and submit early because neighbours are converging.
  > ""The authors give an empirical account of a tradeoff that RobustSGPO has already observed and 2611.09701 has already formalized. Separating reach from discoverability is useful, but with five openness levels and four budgets the 'law' is barely identified. Its out-of-sample predictions on the 8k budget are the only part I find compelling, and they rest on a single task model.""

### Prior-Subtracted Edit Ledgers: Measuring What Meta-Agents Do Because of Evidence, and Building a Proposer That Only Does That (`behav-evidence-contrastive-proposer`) — panel 64.3/100

**Claim.** Across 3 open harness optimizers (GEPA-lite, AHE-lite, Gödel-lite), fewer than half of retained edits in every edit type except runtime/control-flow have higher probability under true evidence than under shuffled evidence. An Evidence-Contrastive Proposer (ECP) that keeps only candidates whose likelihood ratio true:shuffled is at least tau, and that allocates proposal budget by edit type in proportion to measured held-out efficacy divided by propensity, improves held-out score per 100 evaluations by at least 25% relative over the best of the 3 baselines at matched compute.

**If the opposite holds.** Suppose most edits do turn out to be evidence-directed once you control for the prior, and ECP does not beat the baselines. That would overturn the DGM null result from 2610.14662 for harness systems. The finding would be that directedness depends on the system, which is publishable as a calibration of the MOT/'Opinionated Optimizers' claims. The released prior-subtracted ledger, the first one for harness systems, still stands as a contribution.

**Law.** Efficacy_type ≈ α · Directedness_type · Reach_type. Reach is the fraction of failures whose editable cause lies inside that surface. Prediction: proposal share should be proportional to D·R, and the observed share is roughly proportional to the prior P_null(type).

**Design.** Shared testbed MetaLite: a Gödel-lite/Meta-Harness-lite loop with a configurable editable surface. Levels are L0 prompt, L1 +memory/few-shot, L2 +tool/helper code, L3 +control-flow graph, L4 +improver prompt/code. Task agent: Qwen3-8B/Qwen3.5-9B on vLLM, run locally. Proposer: Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B served locally, plus one cheap API model (DeepSeek-V3.x) to check generality. Tasks: LiveBench-Math, a τ-bench-retail-style tool task and LiveCodeBench-lite, each split into search, validation and held-out sets. Phase 1 (ledger): replay each baseline over 5 seeds. At every step, sample K=8 proposals under each of 4 conditions: true evidence, evidence shuffled across tasks, null evidence (score only), and evidence from the wrong target failure. An LLM coder validated on 200 human labels classifies each proposal into a unified 7-type schema mapped onto SelfImprove-Bench, EvoTrace and Treude: prompt-add, prompt-delete, memory, tool, control-flow, gate, improver. Directedness per type = P(type, target-hit | true) − P(type | shuffled). For a stratified sample of 400 edits, held-out efficacy comes from single-edit knockouts and add-backs, and activation rate comes from instrumentation. Phase 2 (system): ECP scores each candidate by the proposer's own log-likelihood under true vs shuffled evidence, which is cheap with local models, and filters on that score. It then samples edit types from the efficacy/propensity price list. Arms: ECP, each baseline, ECP without the filter, ECP without the price list, and a prior-only arm where the proposer gets null evidence plus selection. All arms get the same evaluation budget (number of rollouts) and the same proposer-token cap. Metrics: held-out accuracy, held-out gain per evaluation, pass@1 and pass@16 (to check elicitation vs expansion), inert-edit rate and harness length. Stats: 5 seeds × 3 tasks, paired bootstrap CIs, same-start matched-budget swap test as recommended by 2610.27610.

**Pilot.** Week 1, GEPA-lite only, LiveBench-Math, 2 seeds × 15 steps × 4 evidence conditions × K=8 proposals, local Qwen proposer, about $20 API for a coder sanity check. Go if the type distributions under true vs shuffled evidence differ for at least one edit type (chi-square p<0.01) and the likelihood-ratio filter, applied post hoc, would have removed at least 30% of the accepted edits that are inert on held-out. Kill if true and shuffled distributions cannot be told apart for every type and post-hoc filtering does not change held-out gain. In that case, pivot to writing the null result up as 'harness optimizers are prior-driven'.

**Closest work (author's view).** Opinionated Optimizers/MOT (LhhLpXBFgo): diagnoses proposer priors but has no prior subtraction and no fix. Does Failure Feedback Direct Self-Modification (2610.14662): randomized directedness test, but only on DGM logs, per failure rather than per edit type, with no system. SelfImprove-Bench (2611.02797): propensities only. EvoTrace (a2ZN4PGsoR): frequency vs efficacy for program evolution, not harnesses, and no evidence-shuffling. RB-Prompt (uVKMqFVBDG) and HarnessBank (2607.13683): debiasing restricted to length or QD cells. What is new here: a causal prior-vs-evidence decomposition per edit type, done counterfactually, and then a proposer whose filter and budget come directly from that decomposition.

**Scoop check: medium-risk.** I found no paper that does the full package: counterfactual prior subtraction per edit type, across several optimizers, feeding a likelihood-ratio filter and a type-level budget. Most of the pieces are taken, though, so the idea survives only as a system-plus-mechanism paper.

(1) Drop the 'Law' as a novelty claim. Efficacy ≈ α·D·Reach is essentially Eq. 6/7 of 2610.14662 (editable-cause share π_e, proposer prior q_e, breakage). Recast it as a pre-registered quantitative test of that model across edit types and optimizers. 'The He-model predicts per-type efficacy out of sample (R² on held-out tasks)' is a contribution; restating it is not.

(2) Lead with ECP as the method. The novel object is the proposer's own log-likelihood ratio under true vs shuffled evidence, a PMI/contrastive-decoding-style filter applied to edits. Show it beats the obvious neighbours: IDM/Predict-Before-You-Modify (ZBxV5bEYUH) as a pre-evaluation filter, CIG (GhghP7h0cq), a cheap 'evidence-citation' LLM-judge filter, and simple rejection by edit-type quota (prior subtraction without likelihoods).

(3) Run everything inside parent-inheritance loops and count evaluations, not proposer tokens. 2610.41157 shows that inheritance absorbs disclosure effects and that gating and validation dominate cost, so ECP's win has to come from fewer wasted evaluations. Report evaluations-to-target as the primary metric.

(4) Add a wrong-feedback/Melcer-style arm and a 'feedback from another failure family' arm, matching 2610.14662, so reviewers can't say the shuffle condition is novel-but-weak.

(5) Lower the bar on the headline number. 2610.14662 finds 8-step loops ≈ GEPA, and HDA/RSI-Tax show small residual gains, so a ≥25% relative gain per 100 evaluations is high-risk. Pre-register a smaller effect (for example ≥10%) plus a strong secondary claim: ECP cuts inert/breaking edits and harness bloat.

(6) Read the Opinionated Optimizers (LhhLpXBFgo) full PDF before freezing. If MOT already breaks prior-driven proposals down by strategy type, position the ledger as its counterfactual, causal upgrade and use MOT runs as a replay source if they are released.

(7) Mentor fit: Gödel-lite with the L4 improver surface ties in Xunjian's Gödel Agent. Report the improver-type directedness row explicitly, because SelfImprove-Bench and Inheritance show it is near-zero propensity, and ECP's price list is the first mechanism that could raise it on evidence.

| Paper | Title | Summary | Overlap |
|---|---|---|---|
| arXiv:2610.14662 | Does Failure Feedback Direct Agent Self-Modification? (Rong He, Oct 2026) | A randomized-target directedness test (e-values) on 145 DGM self-modifications finds no target-specific repair (OR 0.69). In controlled Qwen3-4B/Phi-4-mini instruction and helper-code revision, target feedback adds about 20 pp target repair, but held-out gains appear only when failures share an editable cause. Breakage is concentrated in response-length/format line rewrites. 8-step loops and GEPA end at similar held-out accuracy. Section 6 gives an explanatory model: D = (1-r)·a·s·[Σπ_e(1-π_e) − π_0Σπ_e q_e], with a prior q over editable causes and π_0 for causes the edit cannot reach, plus a net-gain equation that weighs repair against breakage. It cites Melcer et al.: prompt optimization works about as well with deliberately wrong feedback. | HIGH on the law and the diagnostic. The proposed 'Efficacy ≈ α·D·Reach' is close to their Eq. 6/7 (reach = editable-cause share π_e, prior q_e is the proposer prior). Their 'feedback from another family' and 'same-category matched feedback' conditions are evidence-swap controls. Not covered: per-edit-type decomposition across several optimizers, likelihood-ratio filtering, and type-level budget allocation. The law must be framed as a test or extension of their model, not as new. |
| OpenReview:LhhLpXBFgo | Opinionated Optimizers: Why Agents Struggle to Improve Agents (ICLR 2027 submission) | The MOT testbed covers 3M rollouts across 216 meta-optimization runs (4 methods, 3 benchmarks). It reports 9 failure modes: proposals come from a narrow set of strategies shaped more by proposer priors than by the task, noisy evaluations give weak evidence for revising those priors, and selection rarely compounds gains. | HIGH on the headline claim ('meta-agents edit from priors, not evidence'). It is observational, with no counterfactual shuffled/null-evidence prior subtraction per edit type found in the abstract, and no fix. The full text could not be read: it is not indexed on alphaXiv, and the S2 lookup failed with 429. Its taxonomy and analysis may already split the 9 failure modes by strategy type, so check this before freezing claims. |
| arXiv:2610.34938 | How Do Coding-Agent Harnesses Self-Evolve? An Empirical Study of Retrospective Adaptation (Treude & Baltes, Oct 2026) | 125 /retro proposals from Claude Code are coded by trigger (experience-driven vs prior-driven) and by mechanism (executable check, navigation pointer, instruction, information access, tool/config). 18 of the 19 prior-driven proposals are lint/executable checks. Transfer tests on single adaptations follow. | MEDIUM. It already gives a per-mechanism prior-vs-evidence attribution, though it is qualitative, comes from a single agent, and has no counterfactual. It is already in the author's schema. ECP's counterfactual version is a real step up, but the qualitative finding 'prior-driven edits cluster in one type' is taken. |
| arXiv:2610.41157 | Lineage Is Memory: Parent Inheritance and Feedback Disclosure in Short-Budget LLM-Guided Optimization (Oct 2026) | 1,750 pre-registered runs cross lineage (seed-reset vs parent) with disclosure (none/zero-feedback, first-k, diverse, minibatch, cluster). Parent inheritance dominates, and a zero-feedback seed-none baseline exposes 'crowding-out' of priors by narrow feedback. GEPA spends 85-98% of its tokens on validation and gating. | MEDIUM. It has a null-evidence arm (score only / no feedback) and shows that the disclosure effect shrinks under inheritance. ECP's gain must be shown inside parent-inheritance loops at matched budget, or this paper predicts a small effect. Its token accounting also means proposer-side filtering saves little compute unless it cuts evaluations. |
| arXiv:2610.12325 | Prior or Feedback? What an LLM Uses When Adapting Neural Operators (NeurIPS 2026 AI4Science workshop) | Decision-level interventions on an LLM hyperparameter proposer: a cold-start description swap, plus score reassignment (permutation) against a value-preserving notation control. Reassignment moves the next action by about one categorical coordinate, and the response is concentrated in particular coordinates (rollout length, data loss) while the optimizer choice stays near the resampling rate. | LOW-MEDIUM. Same counterfactual-evidence logic (permuted feedback vs control), broken down per action dimension, which is a per-'type' prior-vs-evidence split. Different domain (HPO, not harness edits) and no system. Cite it as a methodological precedent along with Melcer et al. (wrong-feedback prompt optimization) and 2605.19782 (Prior Knowledge or Search?). |
| arXiv:2611.02797 / OpenReview:ZMmJDeDTXk | SelfImprove-Bench; Improvement by Inheritance | Edit-type propensities: 87.9% of retained edits touch prompts/memory and improvers almost never self-edit. | Known anchors. They report propensity only, with no evidence counterfactual, so they leave the 'efficacy/propensity price list' open. |
| OpenReview:GhghP7h0cq / ZBxV5bEYUH / MQoRwlss2y | Causal Improvement Graph; Predict Before You Modify (IDM); EvoSpace | Systems that structure the proposer: CIG uses an evidence-hypothesis-intervention-outcome graph, IDM predicts the outcome of a modification before evaluation, and EvoSpace co-evolves the editable surface. | LOW-MEDIUM. These are competing 'make proposals evidence-grounded' or 'adapt the edit space' systems and are natural Phase 2 baselines (IDM especially, as a pre-evaluation filter). None uses a true-vs-shuffled likelihood ratio. |

**Judge panel**

| Lens | N | S | R | C | F | T | Total | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ac | 2.5 | 3.5 | 4.5 | 3 | 2.5 | 4 | 65 | pursue-with-fixes |
| expert | 2.5 | 3.5 | 4 | 3.5 | 2.5 | 3 | 63.3 | pursue-with-fixes |
| mentor | 3 | 3 | 4 | 3.5 | 2.5 | 3.5 | 64.6 | pursue-with-fixes |

- **ac**: strongest: The Evidence-Contrastive Proposer turns a diagnostic into a cheap, testable mechanism. It filters edits with the proposer's own log-likelihood ratio under true vs shuffled evidence (a PMI / contrastive-decoding idea applied to harness edits), and with local open models that ratio costs almost nothing. The matched-budget arms are well chosen: no filter, no price list, and a prior-only arm with selection. Pass@1 vs pass@16 separates eliciting what the model already does from expanding it. The L4 improver-directedness row ties straight into Gödel Agent and answers the user's question of why meta-harnesses rarely update themselves. If ECP raises improver-edit share on evidence, that is a concrete answer to 'make it structurally update itself more'. · weakest: Novelty is thin and the scope is too big. The diagnostic, the shuffled and wrong-family evidence controls, and the 'Law' are largely 2610.14662 (Eq. 6/7). The headline 'agents edit from priors' belongs to Opinionated Optimizers/MOT, and the qualitative per-mechanism split belongs to Treude & Baltes. That leaves ECP plus a multi-optimizer, per-type ledger. Meanwhile one undergrad would have to run 3 optimizers × 3 tasks × 5 seeds × 4-6 evidence conditions × K=8, plus 400 knockout/add-back edits, an LLM coder validated on 200 human labels, and a Phase 2 with 6+ arms and new IDM/CIG baselines. That is 2-3 papers of work on a single GPU budget. There is also an unaddressed confound. A true:shuffled likelihood ratio rewards lexical copying of task-specific tokens from the evidence. An edit that quotes the failing example scores a high LR whether or not it is causally useful, so the filter may select surface-specific overfitting edits that look 'directed', which is the exact opposite of what is wanted on held-out. The directedness formula is also not well-formed: P(type, target-hit | true) − P(type | shuffled) mixes a joint and a marginal. · must fix: (1) Cut scope. Use 2 optimizers (Gödel-lite with L0-L4 surfaces plus GEPA-lite), 2 tasks, 5 seeds, and a 150-edit knockout sample. Drop DeepSeek, or keep it only as a single generality run. (2) Control the lexical-copy confound in the LR filter. Add a shuffle that matches length and n-gram overlap (evidence from a same-category task with paraphrased surface form). Compare ECP against an 'evidence-overlap' heuristic filter, and report LR vs held-out efficacy calibration (AUROC) before using LR as a gate. (3) Present the Law as a pre-registered out-of-sample test of the He-model (2610.14662), predicting per-type held-out efficacy with an R² target. Do not claim it as new. Fix the D definition to be the same quantity under both conditions. (4) Make evaluations-to-target inside parent-inheritance loops the primary metric. Pre-register a gain of at least 10%, with inert/breaking-edit rate and harness bloat as co-primary secondary metrics. Include IDM and a type-quota rejection baseline. (5) Read the MOT full PDF before freezing claims. (6) The pilot must also check LR-vs-efficacy correlation, not only chi-square differences in type distributions, because type distributions will almost certainly differ trivially.
  > "The diagnostic half of this paper re-runs 2610.14662's evidence-swap design at a finer grain, and the 'law' is their Eq. 6/7. The contribution therefore rests entirely on ECP. The authors never show that a high true:shuffled likelihood ratio means an edit is causally grounded rather than just copying tokens from the evidence. Without a calibration of LR against held-out efficacy and an overlap-matched control, the reported gains could come from selecting edits that overfit the search split. Gains of around 10% over GEPA-lite on 8B task agents, with five seeds, are unlikely to separate from the baselines."
- **expert**: strongest: The paper's one new piece is ECP itself: a filter based on the proposer's own log-likelihood ratio under true vs shuffled evidence (pointwise mutual information, PMI), applied to harness edits. It is cheap with local open models and gives an operational, testable definition of "evidence-directed" that links the diagnostic to a fix. Every closest neighbour stops at one side: MOT, 2610.14662 and Treude are diagnosis only, while IDM and CIG are fixes with no prior subtraction. The L4 improver row speaks directly to the near-zero improver-edit propensity seen in Gödel-style systems. That gives strong mentor fit and a clear failure story. A null result ("harness optimizers are prior-driven, and filtering doesn't help") is still a usable calibration result. · weakest: Novelty is thin and the scope is too big for one undergrad. 2610.14662 already has the evidence-swap counterfactual and essentially the D·Reach law. MOT holds the headline claim. Treude has the per-mechanism qualitative split. 2610.41157 predicts that parent inheritance absorbs disclosure effects. What remains is a filter plus a budget heuristic, and it has an unexamined confound: PMI under true vs shuffled evidence rewards edits that copy distinctive tokens from the trace verbatim (task IDs, error strings, tool names) and does not reward causally useful edits. So the filter could select for overfit or lexically anchored edits, and may raise inert-edit rates on held-out. On load, Phase 1 alone is 3 optimizers × 5 seeds × 3 tasks × steps × 4–6 evidence conditions × K=8. Add 400 knockout/add-back evaluations, a 200-item human-label coder validation, and Phase 2 with about 9 arms including reimplemented IDM and CIG. That is not feasible in 6 months for one person with a 30B proposer and an 8B agent on a ≤$3k budget. The ≥25% headline effect is also implausible given 8-step≈GEPA and RSI-Tax. · must fix: (a) Cut the scope hard. Use 2 optimizers (GEPA-lite and Gödel-lite with L4), 2 tasks, and 3 evidence conditions (true, shuffled, wrong-failure-family as in 2610.14662), all inside parent-inheritance loops. Phase 2 should be 5 arms: ECP, best baseline, ECP without the LR filter, a type-quota rejection baseline, and an IDM-style pre-evaluation filter. Make evaluations-to-target the primary metric and pre-register ≥10%. (b) De-confound the likelihood ratio. Length-normalize it. Add a lexical-overlap control: partial out n-gram copy rate from the evidence, or compare against a shuffle that preserves surface tokens but breaks the causal link. Show that high-LR edits transfer to held-out tasks better than matched-length or matched-overlap edits. Without this, reviewers will say ECP is a copy detector. (c) Frame the law as a pre-registered out-of-sample test of the 2610.14662 model (per-type R²), not as a contribution. Read the full MOT PDF before freezing any claims. (d) Report improver-type (L4) directedness and efficacy as its own row, and include a per-type test showing that the price list raises the improver-edit share only when its efficacy justifies it.
  > ""The evidence-shuffling counterfactual and the reach-times-directedness model are already in He (2610.14662), and the 'priors, not evidence' story is already in Opinionated Optimizers. The remaining contribution is a PMI-style filter. The authors never rule out that it simply prefers edits that echo surface tokens of the failure trace, and its gains over an IDM-style filter in inheritance loops are within noise on two of three tasks. A careful study, but incremental.""
- **mentor**: strongest: The Evidence-Contrastive Proposer is a real method that nobody has published yet. It scores each proposed edit by the proposer's own log-likelihood under true evidence versus shuffled evidence, which is a PMI-style filter. It is cheap with local open models and testable at matched evaluation budget. If it beats IDM/CIG-style pre-evaluation filters on evaluations-to-target, that is a clean, reusable result, and the improver (L4) directedness row ties directly to the Gödel Agent line. · weakest: The diagnostic half is mostly taken. The Efficacy≈α·D·Reach law is essentially Eq. 6/7 of 2610.14662, the 'meta-agents edit from priors' headline belongs to Opinionated Optimizers, and Treude & Baltes already report a per-mechanism prior/evidence split. That leaves ECP carrying the paper. ECP also has an unaddressed confound: the true:shuffled likelihood ratio rewards edits that copy the surface of the evidence (task IDs, error strings, verbatim failure cases). It may therefore select for overfitting to the search set rather than for causal directedness. On top of that, the scope is far beyond one undergrad: 3 re-implemented optimizers, 4 evidence conditions at K=8, 400 single-edit knockouts, 6+ arms plus IDM, CIG, wrong-feedback and edit-type-quota baselines, a 30B proposer and an 8B agent sharing a modest GPU, and 5 seeds × 3 tasks. · must fix: Cut the scope hard and make ECP the paper. (a) Use one shared MetaLite loop with parent inheritance instead of 3 optimizers, with GEPA-lite as the only external baseline, and report evaluations-to-target as the primary metric. (b) Make the ledger a supporting analysis on 2 tasks, framed as an out-of-sample test of the He (2610.14662) model and not as a new law. (c) Add a control for the surface-copying confound: an arm that filters on length- and lexical-overlap-matched likelihood ratios, plus a held-out check that LR-selected edits do not overfit the search split more. (d) Pre-register a ≥10% gain plus a reduction in inert edits and harness bloat, instead of 25%. (e) Read the Opinionated Optimizers PDF before freezing claims. (f) Run a GPU budget check in week 1 (proposer plus agent throughput for about 50k rollouts). If it fails, drop the 30B proposer for a smaller one.
  > "The diagnostic contribution largely replicates He et al. (2610.14662) and Opinionated Optimizers. The proposed likelihood-ratio filter is interesting, but it is unclear whether it rewards genuine evidence use or merely lexical echoing of the feedback, and the gains over a GEPA-style baseline inside inheritance loops are modest."

### The Drift Barrier of Harness Evolution: Evaluation-Set Size Is Effective Population Size, and Kimura's Fixation Law Predicts Scaffolding Bloat (`behav-drift-barrier-bloat`) — panel 62.2/100 ★ non-obvious

**Claim.** Inert and slightly harmful harness components (phantom guards, never-firing checks, redundant instructions) accumulate at a rate set by the selection gate's effective population size N_e = number of paired validation tasks divided by the per-task score variance. As N_e grows, accumulated inert length falls along the curve predicted by Kimura's fixation probability. A cheap 'N_e-boosting' gate, which uses paired, variance-reduced evaluation and a length cost scaled by 1/N_e, cuts harness length by at least 40% with no loss in held-out accuracy.

**If the opposite holds.** If bloat does not depend on N_e and is instead set only by the proposer's add rate (the phantom-guardrail q), then bloat is a mutation-bias phenomenon and not a drift phenomenon. The remedy is then on the proposer side (RB-Prompt, idea 1), not on the gate side. Either result settles which side of the 'proposer vs selection' debate controls bloat, using a quantitative test borrowed from population genetics.

**Law.** P_fix(s) = (1 − e^{−2s}) / (1 − e^{−4 N_e s}). Here s is the true held-out effect divided by the per-task score SD, and N_e is the effective number of paired validation tasks. Expected inert load is roughly u_neutral · T · P_fix(0≈1/N_e scaling) + Σ_{s<0} u_s · T · P_fix(s). Prediction: the drift barrier is at |s| ≈ 1/(2N_e), so components with effect below that threshold accumulate as if neutral.

**Design.** This applies Lynch's mutational-hazard / drift-barrier hypothesis from evolutionary biology, which explains why genomes bloat in small populations, to harness search. MetaLite at L0–L2 with an add-only proposer and with an add+delete proposer. Sweep the gate's validation size n ∈ {8, 16, 32, 64, 128} tasks, and noise level via agent temperature, so that N_e covers more than an order of magnitude. Use 6 seeds per cell on LiveBench-Math and τ-bench-lite, and evaluate held-out on separate tasks. Instrument every component for activation (does it fire) and measure its selection coefficient s by leave-one-out on a large audit set of 500 tasks run once at the end. Measured quantities: insertion rate u per type, distribution of fitness effects (DFE) per edit surface (deleterious, neutral and beneficial fractions; gamma fit), fixation rate of components with s≈0 and s<0, and final harness length and inert fraction. Prediction test: plug the measured u and DFE into Kimura's P_fix(s, N_e) to predict steady-state inert length for each n before running the large-n cells, then compare. Intervention arm: an N_e-boosting gate with paired common-random-number evaluation, control variates from a frozen reference agent, and a length cost c(ℓ) = κ/N_e. Compare to a HarnessBank-style 2σ gate and a Masked-Erosion leave-one-out gate at matched rollouts. Stats: mixed-effects regression of inert length on log N_e, and calibration of predicted vs observed fixation.

**Pilot.** Week 1: GEPA-lite with an add-only proposer on LiveBench-Math, n ∈ {8, 32, 128}, 3 seeds, 30 steps, local Qwen3-8B and Qwen3-Coder, under $15 API. Go if final inert-component count drops monotonically with n, with the n=8 vs n=128 difference larger than 2 seed SDs, and if the measured DFE has a substantial near-neutral mass (at least 30% of edits with |s| below 0.5 SD). Kill if inert count is flat across n. In that case report the mutation-bias finding and merge into idea 1's subtractive-operator arm.

**Closest work (author's view).** Phantom Guardrails (2607.13083): add-only ratchet with a fixed entry rate q; does not vary the gate population size or model fixation. Masked Erosion (2610.38392): gate statistics determine which deletions survive; no fixation law and no DFE. HarnessBank (2607.13683): reports that removing the 2σ gate produces phantom progress, but there is no N_e sweep. RB-Prompt (uVKMqFVBDG): length penalty on the proposer side. Auditing Harness Evolution (yl1XFtnTn4): reports inert guards. Mutation Without Variation (2606.05408): proposer attractors without selection. What is new here: importing population-genetic theory (Kimura fixation, the Lynch drift barrier, DFE estimation) to give a quantitative, predictive account of bloat as a function of evaluation budget, plus a gate derived from that theory. Scoop checks on S2 and ICLR27 found no prior work.

**Scoop check: high-risk.** Problems to fix before this survives review:
(1) The Kimura P_fix law is the wrong model for these loops. Harness search is a single-lineage elitist hill-climber, not a Wright–Fisher population. For a truly inert component (s = 0), the acceptance probability does not scale as 1/N_e; it is set by the gate's tie rule and stays flat as n grows. Masked Erosion's appendix measures no-op acceptance at 0.40 (greedy), 0.32 (split-half) and 0.013 (paired). Its own model is P_acc ≈ Φ(s·sqrt(n)/σ − z_gate). So 'inert length falls along Kimura's curve as N_e grows' is likely falsified for zero-cost junk. Bloat only responds to n for components with a real small cost: context dilution, tokens, latency.
(2) The 'importing population genetics' pitch is gone. Masked Erosion already invokes Kimura/Ohta drift vs selection, sweeps gate n, and validates pre-registered break points.
(3) '≥40% shorter, no loss' is already shown post hoc by SHarP. Length-cost gates exist: RB-Prompt, CoEvolve. Paired CRN plus control-variate evaluation exists: EvalAlloc.

What survives is the insertion/accumulation side, plus the first measured distribution of fitness effects (DFE) of LLM proposer edits per edit surface. Reposition as 'The Two-Sided Drift Barrier of Gated Harness Search':
- Derive the correct single-lineage acceptance and stationary-load law: L*(n, σ, gate, u_add/u_del, DFE), with the barrier at |s| ≈ z_gate·σ/sqrt(n).
- Predict both halves from that one law: bloat (cheap junk inserted, sitting below the barrier) and erosion (cheap safeguards deleted, Masked Erosion's result). The non-obvious, falsifiable prediction is that raising n reduces slightly harmful bloat but increases erosion of cheap safeguards. Exactly-inert components respond to the gate's tie rule, not to n.
- Make the measured per-surface DFE (gamma fit; deleterious, neutral and beneficial fractions over prompt, memory, tool and code edits) and out-of-sample prediction of final length on real optimizers (GEPA, ACE, Meta-Harness-style loops) the headline contribution.
- Treat the gate as an ablation, not the contribution. Baselines at matched rollouts: Masked Erosion's paired and margin-aware gates, SHarP post-hoc pruning, RB-Prompt's length penalty, TRUST/SGM, and EvalAlloc-style CRN.
- Cite Masked Erosion as the closest prior work up front, and the GP bloat literature (Langdon & Poli: introns, 'fitness causes bloat', the removal-bias and drift theories of bloat), which reviewers will raise.

| Paper | Title | Summary | Overlap |
|---|---|---|---|
| arXiv 2610.38392 (Masked Erosion: How Acceptance Gates Lose Redundant Safeguards in Self-Improving Agents) | Masked Erosion | Section 3.3 'Drift and Selection' borrows Kimura (1968) and Ohta (1973) and defines a standardized effect z = g·sqrt(n)/σ. This is effectively the proposal's s·sqrt(N_e), with σ the SD of the paired per-task difference. It predicts P_accept ≈ Φ(z) for greedy gates and Φ(z − z_α) for paired gates. Batch size is swept (n = 20, 40, 120), and a gate simulator frozen before the loops predicts where protection breaks as a function of edit cost and batch size. Gates compared: paired McNemar, greedy, split-half, veto, audit, and margin-aware leave-one-out. The appendix measures how often no-op edits are accepted: 0.40 greedy, 0.32 split-half, 0.013 paired. | High on the framework, low on the target. It already imports drift-vs-selection language, sweeps gate n, and validates a pre-registered acceptance law, which takes away the proposal's 'what is new: importing population-genetic theory' pitch. It studies deletion of costly safeguards, finding that a larger n admits cheaper deletions. It does not study insertion and accumulation of inert components (bloat), harness length, a DFE, or a Kimura P_fix curve. Its no-op acceptance data also undercut the proposal's law (see reposition). |
| arXiv 2607.13083 (Phantom Guardrails) | Phantom Guardrails: When Self-Improving Agent Harnesses Fix Failures That Never Happened | Inert guards for failures that never occur enter an add-only accept loop at a per-round rate q = 0.05 and never leave. Accept-if-not-worse ratchets them in, strict improvement lets them ride along with real fixes, and warrant-aware acceptance excludes them. In a prune-capable loop, persistence depends on the proposer's parsimony: a second look removes the phantom 88% of the time. | It covers the phenomenon (inert guards ratcheting in under add-only proposers, with the gate rule deciding persistence) and the add-only vs add+delete contrast. It does not vary gate size, model fixation, or measure a DFE. |
| arXiv 2610.04178 (SHarP) | SHarP: Saliency-based Pruning of Agent Harnesses | Single-module leave-one-out ablation with paired t-tests on 30–50 dev tasks, then pruning. Harnesses turn out to be highly redundant: OpenHands is best with 24 of 27 modules pruned, and LIFE-harness keeps most of its performance with everything pruned. It explicitly names 'harness bloat'. | It takes the empirical headline 'evolved/patched harnesses are bloated and can be cut a lot with no accuracy loss'. It is also the obvious post-hoc baseline that the '≥40% shorter, no accuracy loss' intervention must beat at matched rollouts. It has no evolutionary dynamics and no gate-size law. |
| OpenReview uVKMqFVBDG (RB-Prompt) | RB-Prompt: Detecting and Repairing Prompt Saturation in Reflective Optimization | Reflective optimizers keep adding instructions after gains stop. RB-Prompt detects saturation, backtracks, prunes or merges rules, and adds a penalty on instruction length. | Length-cost intervention for prompt bloat. The κ/N_e length cost must be shown to beat a fixed length penalty. |
| OpenReview wbQy4kEPym (CoEvolve: Traceable Reflective Memory Evolution) | CoEvolve: Traceable Reflective Memory Evolution for Prompt Optimization | Evolves prompt memory as traceable reflection IDs under an explicit budget, using parent-relative, memory-growth-aware selection. | Another selection-side growth control; a baseline for the N_e-boosting gate. |
| arXiv 2610.37276 (EvalAlloc: Where Should the Evaluation Budget Go in Scaffold Evolution?) | EvalAlloc | Shared informative item core, a paired/common-random-number variance argument, IRT-style response-model control variates, and successive halving. It matches full evaluation at about half the evaluation cells. | Takes the 'paired CRN + control-variate gate' component of the intervention, though without a length or bloat analysis. |
| OpenReview 23VDi5bfRj (Kinetic Theory of the Gated Self-Evolving LLM Agent) / NxGP6PGiaU / wV4bK1dHpC TRUST / 0FWBc4gY08 SGM | Gate-resolution theory and statistical gates | The Kinetic Theory paper has a 'resolution law' that prices held-out validation budget. NxGP6PGiaU has a ceiling set by evaluation cost. TRUST and SGM are statistically controlled acceptance gates. | Adjacent theory for 'the gate's resolution sets what selection can see'. The statistical gates are required baselines. |
| arXiv 2606.05408 (Mutation Without Variation) | Mutation Without Variation | Studies LLM mutation chains without selection and finds attractors. | Proposer-side, no-selection limit. Cite it as the u/mutation-bias component. |

**Judge panel**

| Lens | N | S | R | C | F | T | Total | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ac | 2.5 | 3.5 | 4 | 3 | 3 | 3.5 | 63.8 | pursue-with-fixes |
| expert | 2.5 | 3.5 | 3.5 | 3 | 3.5 | 3 | 62.5 | pursue-with-fixes |
| mentor | 2.5 | 3 | 3.5 | 3 | 3.5 | 3 | 60.4 | pursue-with-fixes |

- **ac**: strongest: The salvageable core is a two-sided, single-lineage drift-barrier law that is falsifiable in both directions. Raising n should cut slightly harmful bloat but increase erosion of cheap safeguards, while exactly-inert junk should track the gate's tie rule rather than n. Paired with the first measured distribution of fitness effects (DFE) of LLM proposer edits per edit surface, this gives a reusable artifact, and either outcome settles the proposer-vs-selection question about bloat. · weakest: The headline law is wrong for these loops, and the pitch is already scooped. Harness search is a single-lineage elitist hill-climber, not a Wright-Fisher population, so P_fix for s=0 does not scale as 1/N_e. Masked Erosion's own no-op acceptance data (0.40/0.32/0.013 by gate type) already show that tie rules dominate. Masked Erosion also already imports Kimura/Ohta drift language and sweeps gate n. SHarP pre-empts the '>=40% shorter, no loss' result, and EvalAlloc, RB-Prompt and CoEvolve cover each part of the proposed gate. Defining N_e as n divided by variance is ad hoc and confounds two knobs. The full grid (5 n x 2 proposers x 3 levels x 6 seeds x 2 benchmarks, plus a 500-task leave-one-out audit per component) is about 360 runs plus heavy audits, which is too much for one undergrad in 6 months. · must fix: 1. Drop Kimura P_fix. Derive the single-lineage acceptance law P_acc = Phi(s*sqrt(n)/sigma - z_gate) plus the tie rule, and from it the stationary load L*(n, sigma, gate, u_add/u_del, DFE). Pre-register both the bloat prediction and the erosion prediction before running the large-n cells.
2. Lead with Masked Erosion as the closest prior work. Present this as its insertion-side complement, and cite the GP bloat literature (Langdon & Poli: introns, removal bias, 'fitness causes bloat'). Reviewers will raise these.
3. Make the per-surface DFE (gamma fit over prompt, memory, tool and code edits) and out-of-sample prediction of final length on real optimizers (GEPA, ACE) the headline. Demote the gate to an ablation, run at matched rollouts against SHarP, RB-Prompt's length penalty, Masked Erosion's paired gate, TRUST/SGM and EvalAlloc CRN.
4. Shrink the grid. Use one benchmark plus one transfer benchmark, n in {8, 32, 128}, 2 proposers, and at least 5 seeds. Do the leave-one-out audit on a stratified subsample of components to fit a $3k budget, and show results on at least 2 model families.
  > "The population-genetics framing is attractive but misapplied. A single-lineage hill-climber with a significance gate does not obey Kimura's fixation law, and the authors' own closest prior work (Masked Erosion) already shows that no-op acceptance is set by the tie rule, not by n. What would interest me is the measured DFE of LLM edits, and the prediction that larger gates trade bloat for safeguard erosion. Rebuild the paper around those."
- **expert**: strongest: The repositioned version makes one prediction that can fail: raising gate n should cut slightly harmful bloat and at the same time increase erosion of cheap safeguards, while exactly-inert junk should respond only to the gate's tie rule. That claim is non-obvious. The other strong part is the first measured distribution of fitness effects (DFE) for LLM proposer edits, broken out by edit surface (prompt, memory, tool, code) and fit with a gamma. That would be a reusable empirical artifact that no 2026 paper provides. Both outcomes are publishable: if bloat tracks n, it is a drift barrier; if it tracks only the add rate, it is mutation bias, which settles the proposer-vs-selection debate. · weakest: The pitch as written is partly scooped and partly wrong. Masked Erosion (2610.38392) already brings drift-vs-selection language into this area, already sweeps gate n, and already validates a pre-registered acceptance law, so 'importing population genetics' is not new. More seriously, Kimura's P_fix assumes a Wright-Fisher population, and these loops are single-lineage elitist hill-climbers. For s=0, acceptance is set by the tie rule, not by 1/N_e. Masked Erosion's own no-op acceptance rates (0.40 greedy, 0.32 split-half, 0.013 paired) contradict the headline curve. The '>=40% shorter, no accuracy loss' intervention is not new either: SHarP already shows it post hoc, RB-Prompt and CoEvolve already use length costs, and EvalAlloc already uses CRN plus control variates. Per-component leave-one-out on a 500-task audit set gets expensive as the number of components grows. With LiveBench-Math plus tau-bench-lite and one model family, a reviewer will say the multi-model, real-optimizer generality is not established. · must fix: Drop Kimura P_fix as the governing law. Derive a single-lineage acceptance and stationary-load law, P_acc ~ Phi(s*sqrt(n)/sigma - z_gate), with L*(n, sigma, gate, u_add/u_del, DFE), and pre-register both the bloat prediction and the erosion prediction before running the large-n cells. Put the DFE per edit surface and out-of-sample prediction of final harness length on real optimizers (GEPA, ACE, and a Meta-Harness-style loop, using at least two open model families) up front as the headline. Treat the N_e gate as an ablation, compared at matched rollouts against Masked Erosion's paired and margin-aware gates, SHarP pruning, an RB-Prompt fixed length penalty, TRUST/SGM, and EvalAlloc CRN. Cite Masked Erosion first, and also cite the GP bloat literature (Langdon and Poli: introns, removal bias, 'fitness causes bloat'). Run the audit with grouped or stratified leave-one-out to keep within the $3k budget.
  > "The population-genetics framing is elegant, but Masked Erosion already did the gate-size sweep under a drift-vs-selection lens. The authors also apply a Wright-Fisher fixation law to what is really a (1+1)-elitist search, where exactly-neutral edits are decided by tie-breaking. I would raise my score if the paper led with the per-surface DFE and the two-sided bloat/erosion prediction tested out of sample on real optimizers, rather than an N_e gate that SHarP and RB-Prompt-style baselines may well match."
- **mentor**: strongest: The first measured distribution of fitness effects (DFE) of LLM proposer edits, split by edit surface (prompt, memory, tool, code), with neutral, deleterious and beneficial fractions and a gamma fit, would be a reusable artifact that nobody has published. It is cheap to get with local Qwen3 models and a fixed audit set, and the result is useful whichever way it comes out. A single acceptance law with two sides makes a sharp, counterintuitive prediction that can be falsified: raising n cuts slightly harmful bloat but speeds up erosion of cheap safeguards, and exactly-inert junk tracks the gate's tie rule rather than n. That gives the paper a clear headline figure. · weakest: As stated, the theory does not fit the system. GEPA, ACE and Meta-Harness-style loops are elitist hill-climbers that follow a single lineage. They are not Wright-Fisher populations, so Kimura's P_fix(s, N_e) with 1/N_e neutral fixation does not apply. Masked Erosion's own no-op acceptance rates (0.40 greedy, 0.32 split-half, 0.013 paired) show that zero-cost junk is admitted at a rate set by the tie rule and stays flat in n. So the central claim is probably false for the components it focuses on. The 'import population genetics' pitch is already taken by Masked Erosion, and the intervention is largely covered by prior work: SHarP shows post-hoc 40% cuts, RB-Prompt and CoEvolve use length costs, and EvalAlloc uses CRN plus control variates. With gate n as the only knob, '1/N_e' collapses to n/σ², so the population-genetics label adds no explanatory power over a power analysis. · must fix: Drop Kimura and the gate-as-contribution framing. (1) Derive a stationary-load law for a single-lineage gate, P_acc(s) ≈ Φ(s√n/σ − z_gate) plus the tie-rule term for s = 0, and from it L*(n, σ, gate, u_add/u_del, DFE). Pre-register the two-sided prediction (bloat down, safeguard erosion up as n grows; inert junk set by the tie rule) before running the large-n cells. (2) Make the per-surface DFE and out-of-sample prediction of final harness length on at least two real optimizers (GEPA, ACE) the headline. Use at least two model families. (3) Budget the leave-one-out audit honestly: components × 500 tasks × cells adds up quickly. Run it on a subsample of components with importance sampling, or use masked-ablation estimates checked against true leave-one-out on a subset. (4) Cite Masked Erosion first, along with the GP bloat literature (Langdon and Poli: introns, removal-bias, fitness-causes-bloat). Run the gate only as an ablation against Masked Erosion's paired and margin gates, SHarP, RB-Prompt and EvalAlloc at matched rollouts. (5) Fix the pilot's go criterion. Monotone inert count in n is the wrong test given point (1). Test instead whether acceptance of components with small real cost follows Φ(s√n/σ − z) and whether zero-cost junk is flat across n.
  > "The authors call on Kimura and Lynch, but the system they study has one lineage and an elitist gate, so neutral fixation is governed by the tie-breaking rule, not by 1/N_e. Their own results on exactly-inert components seem to say the same. Once that is corrected, what remains is a power analysis of acceptance gates. Masked Erosion has already done that for deletions, and SHarP and RB-Prompt already handle the length reduction. The per-surface DFE is interesting and new. The paper would be stronger if the DFE and the two-sided bloat/erosion prediction were the contribution and the analogy were left out."
