Report the measured rerun-reproducibility sigma, VERSE-style. (4) Add a placebo arm that forces a semantically null edit, and a forced-but-reverted-unless-verified arm. Report the edit-semantics distribution per q. (5) Add fixed-cadence (every 1/q generations, MetaSkill-Evolve) and an EvoLyapunov-style adaptive gate as baselines. (6) Cite and differentiate VERSE, Winner's Curse, MetaSkill-Evolve, Flex-RSI, SelfImprove-Bench, IFR, MetaRSI, RSI-Harness, EvoLyapunov, Masked Erosion and Recoverability & Verifier Strength. (7) Budget realistically. With the nested replay (3 states x 4 generations of held-out evaluation per final improver), $9/run is optimistic. Plan for $1.5-2.5k and keep a fallback reduced grid.
  > "The question matters and the successor-quality metric is the right one, but as written this is a hyperparameter sweep stapled to the winner's-curse inequality. The grid is too coarse and the seeds too few to localize the claimed q*, and the validation-size noise arm is both borrowed from 2610.09239 and compute-confounded. I would move to accept only if the authors preregister a quantitative q* from pilot-estimated parameters, include a horizon-compounding term that prior theory lacks, and show the prediction holds, with a placebo-edit arm ruling out the possibility that forcing helps merely by injecting exploration."
- **expert**: strongest: The axis being studied is real and nobody has measured it. Every paper uses a fixed meta-cadence (MetaRSI, Flex-RSI, RSI-Harness, IFR) or a binary on/off switch (VERSE), and the only sweep (MetaSkill-Evolve) is confounded with iteration count. A matched-compute dose-response, scored by successor quality and crossed with verification and gate noise, would be a clean descriptive contribution. It also bears directly on the debate over why meta-harnesses rarely edit themselves. Every outcome (inverted-U, monotone, flat) has a pre-registered reading. · weakest: On its own this is a hyperparameter sweep plus a textbook law. The law G(q) ~ a*q - b*q^2*(sigma/delta)^2 is the Winner's Curse (2610.09239) applied to meta-edits. VERSE already owns 'verification is needed'. Winner's Curse already owns the 200-vs-40 validation-size noise manipulation. So the factorial is VERSE x Winner's Curse x a q axis, which is close to K4.

The quadratic form is also ad hoc. Nothing in the setup derives the q^2 term: harmful edits that compound would give a horizon-dependent term, not q^2.

Three practical problems:
- A 'non-empty AST diff' mandate is trivially gamed by Qwen3-32B, so high-q arms may measure no-op churn.
- With 5 seeds and 5 q levels on noisy successor-quality estimates, the bootstrap CI on q* will likely cover the whole 0.1 to 0.5 range. That would make the q* x noise interaction contrast underpowered.
- About $900 for 100 runs, each with nested replay (3 states x 4 generations on held-out tasks) plus a gpt-oss-120b replication, looks optimistic by 2-3x. · must fix: Make the law predictive and derive it properly:
- Measure proposal mean and spread for improver edits (mu_meta, s_meta) and the gate noise sigma in an oracle pilot.
- Derive a compounding term in which a harmful improver edit taxes every remaining generation, so b scales with the horizon H. The horizon dependence is something neither Winner's Curse nor VERSE has.
- Pre-register a numeric q* for each noise cell before running the grid.

Add a placebo arm that forces semantically null AST edits, plus an edit-semantics audit for each q, to separate exploration from content.

Add the obvious baselines:
- deterministic cadence k = 1/q, MetaSkill-Evolve style
- an EvoLyapunov adaptive gate

Cut the grid to fund power. Drop q=1.0 or use only 3 noise/verification cells, and raise the seed count to 8 or more at the q values that bracket q*. Run a power analysis on the pilot variance before committing.

Re-check the ICLR 2027 revisions of VERSE and IFR for frequency ablations.
  > ""This is a careful sweep of one hyperparameter, and the proposed 'law' is the winner's-curse selection model with q substituted in. I'd raise my score if the authors predicted q* ahead of time from pilot-estimated noise, and showed that the optimum moves with horizon length because harmful improver edits compound. Without that, it reads as a well-run ablation that VERSE's appendix could have contained.""
- **mentor**: strongest: This turns the "is the improver's reluctance to rewrite itself a bug or a feature" debate into a matched-compute dose-response, scored by successor quality instead of task score. That fits the mentor's Gödel Agent line closely. Nobody has swept the forcing rate q with seeds and crossed it with verification and gate noise. MetaSkill-Evolve's sweep is confounded with iteration count and has no seeds. VERSE, MetaRSI and Flex-RSI only test on/off or fixed cadences. A cheap $50 pilot gives a clean go/kill signal on curvature, and there is a fallback (the code-vs-state transplant). · weakest: Every piece is owned by someone else, so a reviewer will likely read it as a hyperparameter sweep. VERSE owns the claim that unverified meta-edits don't help. Winner's Curse (2610.09239) owns the noise-to-effect law and the 200-vs-40 validation-size noise manipulation. The proposed G(q) = a*q - b*q^2*(sigma/delta)^2 is that law relabelled. The predicted range q* in [0.1, 0.25] is picked by hand, not derived.

The statistics are also weak. The nested-replay successor-quality metric is noisy, and 5 seeds per cell across 5 q levels is probably too few to put a bootstrap CI on a quadratic peak or to detect the q* x noise interaction. The likely result is "flat within noise", which is the K2-adjacent outcome. The rule that a forced generation must produce a "non-empty AST diff" is easy for Qwen3-32B to game with cosmetic edits. Without a placebo arm, forcing-as-exploration can't be separated from forcing-as-content.

On cost: 100 runs plus a gpt-oss replication plus the reviewer-requested placebo and adaptive-gate baselines will push past the $900 estimate. The space is also very crowded, with roughly ten 2026 papers on adjacent points. · must fix: 1. Make the law predictive and genuinely new. Estimate mu_meta, s_meta and sigma_gate from an oracle pilot. Derive a compounding-horizon term: a harmful improver edit damages every later proposal, so b should scale with the remaining generations. Then pre-register numeric q* predictions for each noise cell before running the grid. Without this step the paper is Winner's Curse applied to meta-edits.

2. Add a placebo arm in which forced edits must be semantically null, plus a forced-but-reverted-unless-verified arm. Report the distribution of edit semantics at each q.

3. Run a power analysis on pilot variance. Then reshape the grid: drop the verification-off half to a single q=0 vs q=0.25 contrast (VERSE already covers it), and spend the freed budget on more seeds and a third noise level, so the interaction becomes a slope rather than a two-point contrast.

4. Add the EvoLyapunov adaptive gate and a deterministic cadence (every 1/q generations) as baselines.

5. Pre-register the monotone and flat outcomes so a null result still publishes as a falsified law.
  > ""The authors run a carefully controlled sweep of a single hyperparameter and fit a quadratic whose form follows directly from the winner's-curse analysis of 2610.09239. The verification-off half reproduces VERSE. With 5 seeds and a noisy successor-quality assay, the claimed inverted-U is not distinguishable from flat in Fig. 3, and the q* x noise interaction rests on two noise levels. I would be more positive if the compounding term had been derived and its predicted q* pre-registered and confirmed out of sample.""

### Sharing Starves the Meta-Level: Population-Genetic Modifier Theory Predicts That Cross-Lineage Edit Sharing Suppresses Improver Self-Edits (`selfupdate-modifier-hitchhiking`) — panel 66/100 ★ non-obvious

**Claim.** In a multi-lineage self-improving population, raising the rate r at which accepted harness edits are shared across lineages monotonically lowers the retained improver-edit frequency and the final improver's successor quality. Attributing shared edits back to the improver that generated them (clade credit) restores both.

**If the opposite holds.** If sharing has no effect on meta-edit retention, improver edits survive through their direct effects rather than by hitchhiking on the edits they produce. That rules out a linkage-based design worry for MetaShare- and Looped-RSI-style systems and shows the low natural rate is not a population-structure artifact. Combined with a calibrated null, this is still a clean, publishable negative from a cross-field prediction.

**Law.** Modifier dynamics: delta p ~ p(1-p) * [s_ind * h * (1 - r) - c], where s_ind is the improver edit's effect on the beneficial-edit rate, h is product heritability within the lineage, and c is the direct cost. This predicts a critical sharing rate r* = 1 - c/(s_ind h) above which improver edits are purged, and that clade credit restores the effective (1 - r) term to about 1.

**Design.** Lens: in evolutionary biology, mutation-rate and evolvability modifier alleles are selected only indirectly, by hitchhiking with the beneficial mutations they cause. Recombination breaks this linkage, and modifiers then drift or are purged when they carry any cost. Mapping: improver edits are modifiers, harness edits are their products, and cross-lineage sharing of harness edits acts as recombination. Testbed: GodelLite population of 8 lineages, each with its own editable improver and harness. Every generation, each accepted harness edit is copied into other lineages with probability r in {0, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0}, and acceptance is gated per lineage on held-out-of-search validation. A 5th arm uses r=1.0 with clade credit: a lineage's improver-edit selection score includes the gains its shared edits produce elsewhere (CMP-style attribution). 5 arms x 5 seeds (populations) x 15 generations. Model: Qwen3-14B for both roles (cheap, self-hostable), with a Qwen3-32B replication at r in {0, 1}. Compute is matched per population. Metrics: retained improver-edit frequency trajectory, measured selection coefficient of improver edits (Price-equation decomposition of the change in improver-edit frequency into direct and indirect, product-linked components), nested-replay successor quality on held-out tasks, and population-best held-out score. Preregistered test: a Jonckheere-Terpstra trend in r and the clade-credit vs r=1 contrast. Cost: about $800.

**Pilot.** r in {0, 1.0} plus clade-credit, 3 populations x 4 lineages x 8 generations, Qwen3-14B on the code family, about $40. Go if retained improver-edit frequency at r=0 is at least 2x that at r=1.0, or if the Price decomposition shows a non-zero indirect component at r=0. Kill if improver edits are essentially absent in all arms (base rate too low to measure); in that case run on top of the forced-quota arm (q=0.25) so that proposals exist and only retention is studied.

**Closest work (author's view).** MetaShare 2610.21887 (cross-lineage sharing, 0/24 self-modification records, not linked to sharing); Looped-RSI sBH9cdajAw (population consolidation of the improver by descendants); HGM 2510.21614 (clade metaproductivity, single lineage tree, no sharing manipulation); Gödel Ecology N4GJVmrzsF (delayed credit, no ablation); population-genetics modifier theory (mutator hitchhiking and recombination). Delta: the first test of a quantitative linkage prediction for meta-edit retention in LLM self-improvement, with a manipulated sharing rate and a Price-equation decomposition.

**Scoop check: medium-risk.** Not scooped. Across about 25 queries no paper manipulates the cross-lineage sharing rate and measures retained improver-edit frequency, and none does a Price-equation split of improver-edit selection into direct and product-linked parts. Several things still need to change for the idea to survive review.

(1) Floor effect. ZMmJDeDTXk and SelfImprove-Bench show improver edits are about 0% even at r=0, so start from a non-zero frequency.
- Run with a proposal quota: a fixed share of proposals, about 30%, must target the improver. The proposal rate is then fixed by design and only retention is under selection.
- Alternatively, seed half the lineages with improver variants of known differing s_ind.
- Make the main dependent variable the selection coefficient or retention probability per proposed improver edit, not the raw frequency. This also separates proposal bias from selection, which is the key confound.
- Pilot on Qwen3-14B first to confirm that improver edits can carry non-zero s_ind at all. If they can't, switch to a stronger cheap improver such as DeepSeek V4 Flash.

(2) Frame the paper as a test of a quantitative law, and give up 'clade credit' as a contribution.
- Cite Looped-RSI (sBH9cdajAw), HGM CMP and Group Elite Selection 2204.04817 as existing credit fixes.
- The novelty is the predicted critical sharing rate r* and its fit: estimate s_ind, h and c independently from the r=0 arm, then predict r* before running the high-r arms.
- Add a cost manipulation, c raised by charging improver edits extra tokens, to test the -c term. This makes it a law rather than a trend.

(3) Rule out the Solo-to-Social alternative (2609.38516). Under that account, sharing suppresses all private exploration (scrounging), not specifically meta-edits.
- Report harness-edit proposal and retention rates alongside improver-edit rates.
- Add a sharing arm that copies edits but strips provenance and keeps the recipient's harness edit count matched.
- The linkage account predicts improver-specific suppression; scrounging predicts uniform suppression.

(4) Use MetaShare's 0/24 self-modification records and GEA as a natural experiment, or as an external high-r audit target. Cite Wright-GM and Mendel-GM for the pop-gen lens and recombination framing, so novelty is not claimed for the metaphor.

(5) Power. Five populations per arm with near-zero base rates is thin. Consider 8 lineages x 8 seeds at r in {0, 0.5, 1}, plus the clade arm, rather than 4 r levels.

| Paper | Title | Summary | Overlap |
|---|---|---|---|
| [2610.21887](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21887) | MetaShare: Failure-Aware Collective Self-Improvement for Open-Ended Agents (NeurIPS 2026 Meta-Agents workshop) | Shares signed records of each modification across lineages, with a six-type mechanism classifier that includes self_modification. Its Table 16 run logs 0 self_modification records before and after the audit. It ablates the pool (on vs off) but never varies the sharing rate. Credit is attached to the source parent's provenance, not fed back into selection of the meta-agent. No forward citations on S2 yet. | Same setting (cross-lineage sharing plus editable meta-agent) and the same phenomenon (absent self-modification). It does not link the two, has no sharing-rate dose, no clade credit, and no Price decomposition. This is a data point the idea explains, not a scoop. |
| https://openreview.net/forum?id=sBH9cdajAw | Looped-RSI: When the Whole is Greater Than The Sum of Its parts (ICLR 2027 sub) | Branching RSI with population-level consolidation. The improver is consolidated separately, scored by the quality of the descendants it produces, and the resulting improvers transfer to unseen environments. | Its improver credit by descendant quality is essentially the 'clade credit' rescue arm, published as a method. It does not manipulate sharing or show that sharing suppresses improver edits. Reviewers will say the fix already exists, so the contribution has to be the causal and quantitative law, not the remedy. |
| [2609.38516](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38516) | From Solo to Social Learning: Characterizing Recursive Social Improvement in LLMs | Populations of skill-revising LLM agents that can copy peers, compared at matched tokens. Copying concentrates the population on 1-2 early lineages (vs about 5 for solo) and reduces independent exploration and private revisions. It manipulates observation timing and access but never lets agents revise their own improvement procedure ('cannot revise the prompts that guide their decisions'). | Object-level analogue of the claim: sharing starves independent search, a producer-scrounger effect. It does not touch the meta level, so the meta-level linkage prediction stays open. Must be cited as the object-level precedent and as an alternative mechanism (scrounging rather than broken linkage). |
| [2510.21614](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.21614) | Huxley-Gödel Machine (HGM): clade metaproductivity | Scores agents by the aggregate performance of their clade (CMP) in a single lineage tree. Forward citations (about 70 on S2, including HGM-Dual 2610.30042, Gödel Forest, The View from Nowhere) contain no sharing-rate manipulation and no analysis of improver-edit retention. | The source of the clade-credit attribution. Single tree, no recombination or sharing axis. |
| https://openreview.net/forum?id=cQkSamYGQi | Wright–Gödel Machine: Mitigating Homogenization in Self-Improving Agents (ICLR 2027 sub) | Population-genetics framing (Wright's shifting balance). Proves that Thompson-sampling parent selection concentrates reproduction on one lineage, and proposes EXP3 over clade statistics. | Same pop-gen-lens genre and clade statistics, but about lineage homogenization under parent selection, not meta-edit retention under sharing. It shows reviewers are receptive to pop-gen theorems in this space, and it is a competitor for the 'pop-gen lens' novelty. |
| https://openreview.net/forum?id=co3NCcIZ7f | Mendel Gödel Machine (2608.07645): cross-lineage hybridization | Adds cross-lineage hybridization edits, proves faster convergence under an additive landscape, and reports gains on SWE-bench and Polyglot. | Cross-lineage recombination as a method, framed with Mendelian genetics. No meta-edit or improver analysis. Shows that 'sharing as recombination' is not a new metaphor. |
| https://openreview.net/forum?id=HpnuHDtvwJ | The View from Nowhere: Population-Centered Agent Evolution (2610.36660) | Distils population-wide task records and modification histories into a block that guides each parent's harness edits. Beats group evolution (GEA) by 3-6 pp. | Another sharing-heavy method that could serve as a high-r arm or audit target. It does not report improver-edit frequency. |
| [2204.04817](https://arxiv.org/abs/2204.04817) | Effective Mutation Rate Adaptation through Group Elite Selection (2022) | Classical evolutionary-computation result: individual-level self-adaptation of mutation rates is brittle, and selecting mutation rates at the group level fixes it. | Direct classical analogue of the 'clade credit restores modifier selection' prediction. It, together with the classic mutator-hitchhiking and recombination modifier literature (Sniegowski, Tenaillon, Feldman reduction principle; 2603.02496 on recombination modifiers), makes the theory itself non-novel. Novelty rests on the LLM-RSI test. |
| https://openreview.net/forum?id=ZMmJDeDTXk | Improvement by Inheritance (ICLR 2027 sub) and SelfImprove-Bench 2611.02797 | Improvers almost never edit themselves, and 0% of retained edits touch the improver unless forced, even without sharing. | Not a scoop but the biggest threat: a floor effect. If the improver-edit frequency is about 0 at r=0, a monotone decrease in r is unobservable. It also offers an alternative explanation: LLM proposal bias rather than selection. |

**Judge panel**

| Lens | N | S | R | C | F | T | Total | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ac | 3 | 2.5 | 4 | 2.5 | 3.5 | 4 | 62.5 | pursue-with-fixes |
| expert | 3 | 3.5 | 4 | 3 | 3.5 | 3.5 | 67.5 | pursue-with-fixes |
| mentor | 3 | 3 | 4.5 | 3 | 3.5 | 4 | 67.9 | pursue-with-fixes |

- **ac**: strongest: It speaks directly to the user's question of why meta-harnesses rarely update themselves. It gives a falsifiable, mechanistic answer from another field: shared products break the link between an improver and its fitness. It turns MetaShare's 0/24 self-modification records into a prediction. The manipulated dose (r), the clade-credit contrast, the provenance-stripped control against the scrounging account, and the Price-equation split of direct and indirect selection are the right tools to separate mechanisms. It is cheap on open models. · weakest: As specified, the headline result is close to built in by design. If improver edits are selected through lineage-level harness fitness, and r=1 copies every accepted harness edit into every lineage, then the lineages' harnesses converge. Lineage fitness then cannot tell improvers apart, so suppression follows from the mechanics, not from an empirical law. The surprising part, the quantitative fit of r* = 1 - c/(s_ind·h), cannot be estimated with 8 lineages x 15 generations x 5 seeds. Estimating s_ind, h and c from noisy LLM-edit populations with near-zero base rates will give confidence intervals on r* that cover the whole [0,1] range. Other problems: the floor effect (improver edits about 0% even at r=0), proposal bias versus selection, and clade credit already existing (Looped-RSI, HGM CMP, Group Elite Selection). Together these leave thin novelty and a weak null story. · must fix: (1) Specify the selection rule for improver edits. They must be accepted through a channel where sharing is not mechanically guaranteed to erase the signal. Otherwise, state in advance the non-trivial part: the functional form and the location of r*. (2) Use a proposal quota or seeded improver variants with known s_ind. Make the main measure retention probability, or the selection coefficient per proposed improver edit, not raw frequency. (3) Estimate s_ind, h and c from the r=0 arm and a cost-manipulation arm, and register the predicted r* before running intermediate r values. Use more r levels (at least 5) and fewer generations if needed, so a threshold can be told apart from a linear decline. (4) Add the provenance-stripped, edit-count-matched sharing arm and report harness-edit retention, to rule out uniform scrounging (2609.38516). (5) Power: aim for at least 8 seeds per arm. Run a power analysis from the pilot variance. Replicate on a second model family, not just a larger Qwen. (6) Give up clade credit as a contribution, and do not claim novelty for the pop-gen framing (Wright-GM, Mendel-GM).
  > "The modifier-theory lens is elegant, but at r=1 the lineages share identical harnesses by construction, so of course lineage selection can no longer favor any improver. What the authors would need to show is that the critical sharing rate predicted from independently estimated s_ind, h and c matches the observed one. With 8 lineages, 5 seeds and improver edits that almost never occur, I am not convinced that estimate is identifiable. Clade credit as a remedy is already Looped-RSI/HGM."
- **expert**: strongest: It makes a real, falsifiable, quantitative prediction: a critical sharing rate r* = 1 - c/(s_ind h), set by parameters you can estimate from the r=0 arm. It also gives a mechanistic account of an observed anomaly, MetaShare's 0/24 self-modification records under heavy sharing. That is a rare outcome-agnostic design in a field full of method papers. The Price-equation split of direct and product-linked selection would be a reusable analysis tool for any population-based RSI system, and the sharing rate is a dose knob that is cheap to manipulate. · weakest: The modifier-theory mapping only holds if selection on improver edits happens at the lineage or population level: lineages reproduce or are replaced according to relative fitness. As written, acceptance is gated per lineage on that lineage's own validation. If an improver edit is kept or dropped by its own lineage's successor quality, sharing harness edits to other lineages never enters that decision, the (1 - r) term is zero by construction, and the null is trivial rather than informative. Several other problems compound this.
- The floor effect: natural improver-edit rates are about 0. The forced-quota fix changes the system being studied.
- The theory is borrowed. Reduction principle, mutator hitchhiking and Group Elite Selection 2204.04817 make it non-novel.
- The rescue arm (clade credit) is already published as Looped-RSI and HGM's CMP.
- With 8 lineages and 5 populations, the Price covariance terms will be dominated by noise. s_ind for LLM improver edits is probably tiny and highly context-dependent, so fitting r* before running the high-r arms is likely to produce confidence intervals spanning [0, 1]. · must fix: 1. Specify an explicit between-lineage selection step and preregister it, for example Moran or tournament replacement of lineages by population-relative held-out fitness. Hitchhiking can only operate through that step. Then show analytically that per-lineage gating alone predicts no r effect. That null becomes a built-in control arm, and the design is what makes it informative.
2. Make the dependent variable the per-proposal retention probability or selection coefficient, under a fixed improver-proposal quota (about 30%). Also add the seeded-known-s_ind arm, so s_ind and h are measured rather than assumed.
3. Before spending, run a power analysis or simulation. With the pilot's s_ind and c estimates, find the number of populations needed to tell r* apart from 0 and from 1. Likely result: drop to r in {0, 0.5, 1} and use 8 or more populations per arm.
4. Add the cost manipulation (raised c) and the provenance-stripped, count-matched sharing arm. Report harness-edit retention alongside improver-edit retention, so the Solo-to-Social scrounging account (2609.38516) is excluded.
5. Reframe clade credit as a replication of Looped-RSI and HGM, not a contribution. Cite Wright-GM and Mendel-GM for the pop-gen lens.
6. Replicate on a second model family (for example DeepSeek V4 Flash or Llama) and a second task family. Qwen-only results on the code family will draw a generality critique.
  > "Mapping cross-lineage sharing onto recombination is elegant, and r* is a genuinely falsifiable target. But the authors force improver proposals with a quota, choose the lineage-replacement rule themselves, and then fit a three-parameter law to 8-lineage populations whose Price covariances are within noise. I am not convinced the fitted r* reflects LLM self-improvement rather than the selection scheme they built. The clade-credit 'restoration' is Looped-RSI under another name. Weak accept if the lineage-level selection is principled, the r* prediction is made out of sample with tight intervals, and a second model family is shown; otherwise reject."
- **mentor**: strongest: It makes a quantitative, falsifiable prediction from another field: a critical sharing rate r* = 1 - c/(s_ind h). The parameters can be estimated from the r=0 arm and checked out of sample. It speaks directly to the mentor's core puzzle of why improvers rarely modify themselves (Gödel Agent lineage, MetaShare's 0/24). It is cheap on open models, and the Price-equation split is a reusable measurement tool. · weakest: The headline result risks being true by construction. If a lineage's improver edit is accepted on that lineage's own harness gain, then at r=1 every lineage gets the same harness edits. The improver's differential advantage then disappears mechanically, and reviewers will call it an accounting identity rather than a finding about LLM agents. This sits on top of a floor effect: base improver-edit rates are about 0. That forces an artificial proposal quota, which weakens the claim that this is about how real systems behave. The theory is classical (mutator hitchhiking, the reduction principle, Group Elite Selection). The clade-credit fix already exists (Looped-RSI, HGM CMP). The pop-gen lens is already taken (Wright-GM, Mendel-GM). So novelty rests entirely on whether the r* fit is non-trivial. With 8 lineages x 15 generations, estimates of s_ind and h per edit will be very noisy. · must fix: 1. Show the effect is not tautological. State the selection rule explicitly, derive what a pure-accounting model predicts, and then show the LLM system departs from it or matches it with independently estimated, non-trivial s_ind, h and c. Predict r* before running the high-r arms. Add the cost manipulation on c so the claim is a law, not a trend.
2. Handle the floor effect. Use a fixed proposal quota of about 25-30% improver-targeted proposals, plus seeded improver variants with known differing s_ind. Make retention probability per proposed improver edit the main dependent variable, not raw frequency.
3. Rule out producer-scrounger. Run a provenance-stripped sharing arm with the recipient's harness-edit count matched, and report harness-edit and improver-edit rates side by side.
4. Increase power. Use 8 seeds at r in {0, 0.5, 1} plus the clade arm, rather than 5 seeds across 4 r levels. Do a multi-model replication, and also try a stronger cheap improver.
5. Position clade credit as a known fix, not a contribution. Use MetaShare/GEA as an external high-r audit target.
  > "The authors manipulate a sharing rate that, by the design of their own acceptance rule, removes any lineage-specific benefit from improver edits. They then report that improver edits are no longer retained. Without an independent, out-of-sample prediction of r* and a control separating linkage from generic scrounging, this is a re-derivation of the Feldman reduction principle in a toy setting, with clade credit already known from HGM and Looped-RSI."


---

# Thread: A reality-grounded benchmark that forces continual self-improvement

## A reality-grounded benchmark that forces continual self-improvement

*Scope and method.* I ran about 45 queries across the three tools:
- alphaXiv discover: 12 calls;
- Semantic Scholar: about 12 keyword or batch calls, plus forward-citation expansion of CL-Bench 2606.05661, Are-SE-CL 2610.25858, EvoHarnessBench, SelfImprove-Bench, Δτ-Bench and HarnessOpt-Bench;
- ICLR 2027 OpenReview `/notes/search`: about 20 queries.

I read the full text (targeted PDF queries) of 2610.25858, 2606.05661, 2610.34238, 2611.05108, 2610.27524 and 2610.34938. Every other row has a fetched abstract unless marked †.

**Bottom line:** no existing benchmark meets all four criteria: (i) real drift, (ii) object-level adaptation insufficient, (iii) forward transfer of improvement ability measured, (iv) time-split.
- **Real-drift data sources exist but are used statically.** API and library version histories, real repo histories, benchmark-repo commit histories, real MCP/REST schema release pairs and replayed news streams are each used either for one-shot model evaluation or for memory evaluation, never for meta-level adaptation.
- **Agentic continual benchmarks use synthetic drift.** Every one I found uses synthetic or LLM-generated drift: block reshuffles of existing datasets, capability-preserving tool transforms, injected schema renames, mirror-world rule edits.
- **No benchmark forces the improver to change.** None makes the improver's own procedure, rather than its prompt or memory output, the bottleneck, and none scores forward transfer of improvement ability across real time slices.

### Key papers

| id/link | when | summary | what it says about this thread |
|---|---|---|---|
| [CL-Bench 2606.05661](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2606.05661) (Asawa, …, Zaharia, Gonzalez) | Jun 2026 | Six expert-validated domains: real SWE issues from tablib/tenacity, an obfuscated SQLite DB, poker, RF spectrum, sales, cohort studies. Each has hidden latent structure and a "gain" metric (stateful minus stateless). Full-context ICL beats Mem0, ACE and notepad memories: Sonnet 4.6 ICL gain 25.4% vs ACE 8.6%. | Reality-flavoured, but the drift is designed: one mid-run "DB migration", and the BSM drift is "epistemic rather than real" by the paper's own wording. No meta-level requirement, no time split. Strong lesson: simple ICL is the baseline to beat. |
| [Are Self-Evolving Agents Continual Learners? 2610.25858](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2610.25858) (Mila/GDM) | Oct 2026 | Four drifting streams (topic, workflow, domain, compound) built by blocking BIRD/BFCL/ScienceWorld groups. ReasoningBank, GEPA and Meta-Harness all trade plasticity against stability (r = −0.72). GEPA compresses earlier-block rules out of its prompt; the Meta-Harness gate rejects at least half of edits and freezes. | Synthetic block reshuffles, not real drift (fails i). The evolver is fixed (fails ii). It shows global self-modification overwrites. It never varies the improver. |
| [Δτ-Bench 2610.34238](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2610.34238) / [ICLR27 xgCSt7IRVo](https://openreview.net/forum?id=xgCSt7IRVo) | Oct 2026 | 1,588 tasks, 42 domains, 6 ecosystem states. Between states, 110–200 tools are added, retired or modified via LLM-generated "capability-preserving transformations" (rename, split, merge). Best SR is 42.3%. EvoSkill, AutoSkill, Trace2Skill and SkillOpt give v1 gains that fail to track later states; DAR drops to 0 at v3 for two methods. | Closest to "API drift at scale", but the drift is synthetic and capability-preserving, so it is mostly renames and splits. Its fix (STEWARD) is a hand-designed object-level manager, not a self-changing improver. |
| [AgentDriftBench (ICLR27 5xFWggnePr)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=5xFWggnePr) | Nov 2026 | Injects five drift families (param renames, new required fields, deprecations) mid-episode. Scores recovery AUC, recovery steps and oscillation on Qwen 0.5–7B. Scratchpad memory hurts 0.5B and helps Coder-3B. | Recovery-trajectory metrics are reusable. Drift is injected, the models are tiny, and there is no improver. |
| [DynaToolBench / CATE (ICLR27 AHOdphwgU7)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=AHOdphwgU7) | Nov 2026 | Tool replacement over three timesteps on a MirrorAPI backend. A skill-population method localizes the affected skills. | Synthetic and object-level. |
| [EvoHarnessBench 2609.04280](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2609.04280) | Sep 2026 | 17 multi-stage streams in which the externally supplied harness (tools, skills, agents) changes, built deterministically from verifier benchmarks. | Puts non-stationarity in the harness rather than the task. Still synthetic, and the agent does not author the change. |
| [Continual AutoResearch 2611.05108](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2611.05108) | Nov 2026 | Five frontier CLI agents, persistent vs reset, nine task families. Persistence speeds research (time-convergence +3 to +21 pts) but final outcome gain is about 0 (−0.9 to +0.4, except Sonnet +7.6). Matched vs shuffled insight cards make little difference. "Safe-first" lessons over-applied kill exploration. | Retaining experience ≠ using it. A matched/shuffled/no-memory ablation is the right control template. Drift is not real; the streams are 3–5 tasks. |
| [Continual-ARC 2611.14505](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2611.14505) | Nov 2026 | Lifelong ARC stream in which tasks return after dormant gaps and new ones keep arriving. Feedback-budget scoring compares memory- and weight-based learners. | "Dormant return" is a good retention probe, but the setting is abstract and stationary. |
| [Agent Plasticity 2610.08902](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2610.08902) / [Jjh60xpFVY](https://openreview.net/forum?id=Jjh60xpFVY) | Oct 2026 | Measures self-improvement through experience (plasticity) for task agents. | Actor-level only, so the meta-level is open. |
| [SelfImprove-Bench 2611.02797](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2611.02797) | Nov 2026 | RSI evaluation on games; separates capability from scenario fitting. Prior-context reading: 87.9% of retained edits touch prompts/memory and 0% touch the improver unless forced. | Direct evidence that stationary game tasks never make the improver the bottleneck. |
| [Flex-RSI 2611.08946](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2611.08946), [RSI-Env 2611.01736](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2611.01736), [RSIBench Q9AHmuAk4Y](https://openreview.net/forum?id=Q9AHmuAk4Y) | Nov 2026 | Benchmarks in which the improver may revise itself: vision/robotics tasks (Flex-RSI), model+harness under Open/Capability/Benchmark-guided goals (RSI-Env), held-out Terminal/SWE/Science tasks (RSIBench, where GPT-5.5 loses 8.3 pp held-out). | Permit improver edits but do not require them. All are stationary; no drift. |
| [Evo-Bench 2608.09096](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2608.09096), [HarnessOpt-Bench 2608.06301](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2608.06301), [SEAGym 2606.17546](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2606.17546), [EvoAgentBench 2607.05202](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2607.05202), [ContinualSkillBench 2608.03874](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2608.03874) | Jun–Aug 2026 | Harness-evolution and self-evolution benchmarks: train/val/test/replay records (SEAGym on Terminal-Bench 2.0/HLE), budgeted optimizer protocols (HarnessOpt), Ability-graph transfer (EvoAgentBench). ContinualSkillBench finds that retaining context without skill maintenance matches an explicit skill library. | Static task pools. AnytimeVal-style budgeted reporting† (attributed in the brief and prior context, not re-verified) is useful methodology, not drift. |
| [AgentStream 2608.00155](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2608.00155) (GGUmZybpbd), [PATH-Bench 2608.01149](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2608.01149), [PAST-Bench 2608.04003](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2608.04003), [S3Gym 2608.31100](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2608.31100), [EmbodiedCLBench 2610.13021](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2610.13021), [CLE 2610.33956](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2610.33956), [AgentCL V8XlaeHbmi](https://openreview.net/forum?id=V8XlaeHbmi) | Jul–Nov 2026 | Streaming, path-dependent, personal-agent, self-test/judge, embodied-compositional, professional-ladder and dependency-controlled CL streams. | A crowded "stream of existing tasks" lane. None uses real temporal drift; all are object-level. |
| [ReScopeBench 2611.05450](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2611.05450), [SkillShift-Bench 2610.33528](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2610.33528), [PACE-Bench 2608.14441](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2608.14441), [MirrorCraft 2607.29218](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2607.29218), [AdaExploreBench 2610.25950](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2610.25950), [AGNI pfPGJ6T7ld](https://openreview.net/forum?id=pfPGJ6T7ld) | Jul–Nov 2026 | Controlled environment changes: experience lifecycle across 18 environments; contextual/perceptual/execution web shifts; source→mutated physics; hidden Minecraft datapack rule changes; implicit rule drift; trajectory-assumption novelty in terminals. AGNI finds agents see evidence of change but fail to localize it. | Paired before/after designs, but the shifts are synthetic. AGNI's post-training on novelty improves held-out adaptation at the weight level. |
| [BreakingWeb 2609.35814](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2609.35814) (mentor) | Sep 2026 | 519 clean/intervened browser task pairs on 7 self-hosted sites. Interventions are labelled by required recovery behaviour. | Mentor-owned intervention engine. Interventions are controlled, not historical DOM drift. A natural base for a "real-drift replay" extension. |
| [WebVariants TYSv9rIhiQ](https://openreview.net/forum?id=TYSv9rIhiQ), [WebHarbor 2611.03634](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2611.03634), [EnvOS 2610.40246](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2610.40246) | Oct–Nov 2026 | Same functionality in five implementations (up to 50% rank reversals); 153 dockerized real-site reconstructions that "evolve as tasks expand"; certified mid-task change (stale info). | Infrastructure for frozen website versions; WebHarbor could host time-indexed snapshots. EnvOS certification (change fires, task stays solvable, grader separates) is a must-have protocol. |
| [MemDev-Bench 2610.27524](https://www.alphaxiv.org/abs/2610.27524) | Oct 2026 | 4,869 executable historical tasks for 555 SWE-bench base tasks across 22 repos, built from real PR histories with strict temporal validity.
